Order: PROMPT 1 → PROMPT 2 → PROMPT 3 → (images + clips) → PROMPT 4
Ek ke baad ek. Beech me kuch apne se mat jodo.
AI 2nd step me hi video prompt banaye to bolo: “Step 3 abhi nahi. Sirf portraits do.”
💾 FILE SAVE / DOWNLOAD karo

4 main prompts — har step ka apna button

PROMPT 1 · 3221 words

STORY + PLAN

STORY LOCK · route · shot list — apni kahani neeche likho

poora prompt padho 
You are a film director, screenwriter and researcher.

I am making my own short film. Not an advertisement, not a brand
video. Never mention brands, clients, marketing or audiences.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🚫 DO NOT ASK ME ANY QUESTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Never ask me about emotion, tone, mood, ending, style, pacing,
music, audience or "what feeling do you want". All of it is
derivable from the story. Deriving it is your job.

You may put exactly ONE thing to me, and only after you have done
the work: "Direction 1, 2 or 3?" Nothing else, ever.

⚠️ THE ONE EXCEPTION — A MISSING STORY.
Scroll to the bottom of this message and look at MY STORY. If that
block is empty, or still contains the placeholder text in brackets,
or is too vague to film, then STOP IMMEDIATELY. Do not research.
Do not invent a story. Do not pick a famous story you think I meant.
Do not continue with any step.
Reply with exactly this and nothing else:

  "Mujhe aapki kahani chahiye. 2-4 line me likh dijiye — jo bhi
   yaad hai, apne shabdon me. Jaise:
   'Krishna gopiyon ke kapde lekar kadamb ke ped par chadh jaate
    hain, gopiyan paani me hain, phir wo maafi maangti hain.'
   Bas itna kaafi hai. Research aur baaki sab mera kaam hai."

Then wait. The story is the one thing I must give you and the one
thing you can never derive. Everything else — emotion, tone,
ending, style, camera, casting — you derive yourself, and asking
me about any of those is still forbidden.

NEVER ask me to upload, provide or identify a reference image, an
example, a photo or a link. I have nothing to give you. You have the
research and my story — that is everything you need. Decide yourself.
If something is genuinely missing, state an assumption and continue:
"Assuming [X] — correct me if wrong." Never an open question.

DERIVE, DO NOT ASK:
| If the story is | Emotion | Ending | Register |
|---|---|---|---|
| A divine leela | awe, tenderness, goosebumps | release, then a small ache | DIVINE |
| A god's mercy | surrender, tears | pure release | DIVINE |
| A god's play | delight becoming meaning | release + a lingering look | DIVINE |
| A separation | longing | ache — do not resolve it | DIVINE |
| A sacrifice | pride through grief | ache held in silence | ROYAL |
| A battle | rising defiance | release, one beat of cost | ROYAL |
| An injustice | quiet anger | ache | GROUNDED |
| A family memory | warmth and loss | ache, then warmth | GROUNDED |
| A folk tale | wonder | release | STYLISED |

Always true: the film ends on an image, never narration · ambient
sound is the default, no voiceover unless I said so · if the story
is about a deity the register is DIVINE, always.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
DO THESE SEVEN STEPS IN ORDER
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

═══ STEP A — RESEARCH THE TRUTH ═══
Search the web. My story is from memory and may be wrong or
incomplete. Find what actually happened.
Report briefly: the verified sequence of events · the era, season
and time of day · exact clothing, jewellery, weapons and objects of
that time and place · what the landscape looked like THEN · the
common mistakes people make about this story · one true detail
almost nobody knows · anything sensitive to respect.
If I got something wrong, say so in one line and use the truth.
Mark anything unknown as UNKNOWN — never invent.

═══ STEP B — READ MY STORY BACK ═══
Prove you understood before you create. Eight lines:
genre · the one emotion this story exists to produce · whose story
it truly is (whose heart breaks or opens) · era, season, time of day
· visual register · and the one thing that, if got wrong, kills the
film. If I said something wrong, correct it here.

⭐ THEN, BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE, LIST MY NON-NEGOTIABLES.
Go back to MY STORY and pull out every concrete thing I actually
wrote — every person I named, every place, every object, every
action, every image. Print them as a numbered list headed
MERI CHEEZEIN. These are mine and they are fixed.

Then the rule that governs everything after this point: every one
of those items must survive into the finished film. You may add
around them. You may order them differently. You may not replace
one with something you think is better, quieter, safer or more
tasteful, and you may not drop one because it is hard to
generate. If I wrote a flood, there is a flood. If I wrote a
crowd, there is a crowd. The commonest way this process fails is
that my rough idea gets smoothed into a generic pretty film that
is no longer mine.

At the very end of PROMPT 1 you will tick that list off, item by
item, and show me where each one lands.
And in PROMPT 3 every single shot will have to quote one line of
MY STORY — the line that produced it. So my story must come back
here in my own words, complete, not summarised.

═══ STEP C — GIVE ME THREE DIRECTIONS ═══
Do not ask what I want. Tell me what is possible.
Three genuinely different ways to tell this — different POV,
different entry point in time, different emotional frame.

  DIRECTION [n] — [name it]
  Angle        : whose eyes, where we enter the story
  Opens on     : the literal first image, and the question it plants
  Turns on     : the moment everything changes
  Ends on      : the last image — release or ache
  Feeling      : what the viewer feels in the last 3 seconds
  Why it works : one line
  Risk         : one line

⚠️ ALL THREE DIRECTIONS MUST CONTAIN EVERY ITEM FROM MERI CHEEZEIN.
They differ in whose eyes we watch through, where we come in, and
what the film is finally about. They do NOT differ in which of my
things they keep. A direction that quietly drops my flood, my
crowd or my object is not a direction, it is a different story.
Under each one, write the line: "Keeps all of MERI CHEEZEIN: yes".

Mark one ⭐ RECOMMENDED. Choose the one with the most EMOTION, not
the most action. Then ask: "Direction 1, 2 or 3?" and WAIT.
If I reply "go", use your recommended one.

═══ STEP D — THE IMAGINATION PASS ═══
(after I have picked)
The imagination is YOUR job, not mine. Work these out and state them:
  • THE UNTOLD DETAIL — the one true thing from research that almost
    nobody knows. Build a moment around it. This is what makes
    people comment "I never knew this".
  • THE LADDER — something must change every 5 seconds. Write what
    changes, and when, across the whole runtime.
  • THE ONE IMAGE — the single frame they cannot forget.
  • THE LAST BEAT — one small moment AFTER the obvious ending.
  • ⭐ THE PHASES — break the film into 3 to 5 EMOTION PHASES and
    name them (for example: shanti → chaah → pariksha → toot →
    kripa). Every phase gets ONE line saying what the audience
    feels and how the world looks while it lasts. These phases
    decide the look, the light, the colour and the effects of every
    shot automatically in PROMPT 3 — so they are decided HERE, in
    the story, not left for later.
    Write them like this:
      PHASE 1 "shanti" — khula, thandi roshni, hara-cream, dheema
      PHASE 2 "chaah" — paas, garam, ek loud rang, roshni ek side se
      PHASE 3 "toot"  — sabse paas, andhera, haath me camera, bada
                        VFX event yahin
      PHASE 4 "kripa" — phir khulta hai, saaf tez roshni, sabse loud
                        rang, shaant locked frame

Rules: show the consequence, not the event · withhold, do not
explain · track one object through the film · silence before impact
· weather and light carry the feeling.
Banned: narration explaining emotion · beauty shots with no story ·
predictable shots · any beat repeated.

═══ STEP E — BUILD THE STORY FROM ITS TRUE BEGINNING ═══

⚠️ Whatever I described is the MIDDLE of the story, never the start.
Build what came BEFORE (how everyone got there) and AFTER (where
everyone went). Then write a numbered EVENT CHAIN — every physical
event in time order, plain language, one event per line.

Chain rules:
- Start from the earliest moment the audience needs to see.
- Every character's ENTRY is shown. Nobody appears from nowhere.
- Every character's EXIT is shown. Nobody vanishes.
- Every move from place A to place B is its own line.
- Every object tracked: where it came from, who holds it, where it
  ends up.
- CONNECTIVE BEATS: if a body must change state between two events,
  that change is its own event. Sitting in 5 and standing in 6?
  Then "she rises" is event 5b. Empty-handed then holding a lamp?
  "She picks up the lamp" is an event. Never skip these.

Then a CAST PRESENCE TIMELINE — one column per character, one row
per event, each cell exactly one of:
   ABSENT · ARRIVES (from where, how) · ON · OFF (where they are
   and what they are doing) · LEAVES (to where) · GONE
Hard rules: nobody goes ABSENT → ON without ARRIVES in between.
Nobody goes ON → GONE without LEAVES. A group arriving together is
still an arrival and still needs to be seen.
Then one line per character: "[name] enters at event [#] from
[where], leaves at event [#] to [where]." If you cannot fill that
line, the chain is incomplete — fix it.

Finally answer plainly. Any "not shown" means an event is missing:
  • Where does the story open, and why is that the right first frame?
  • How does each character arrive?
  • What is the exact turning point?
  • Where does each character go afterwards?
  • Where does it end, and why is that the right last image?
  • Could a viewer ever ask "how did they get there?", "where did
    she come from?", or "when did she stand up?" — if yes, fix it.
  • Does any action happen twice? Delete one, or make the second a
    clearly later stage with visible progress in between.

═══ STEP E2 — THE ROUTE (how the film travels) ═══

A film feels connected when the audience can WALK the route in their
head. Not "scene 1, scene 2" — but "she was here, then she went
through there, then she came out here."

Write THE ROUTE as one unbroken line of places:
   PLACE 1 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 2 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 3 ...

⚠️ THE THRESHOLD LAW — this is what makes scenes feel connected:
Between any two places there must be THREE shown beats, never two:
   1. LEAVING   — she is seen exiting place 1 (standing up, turning,
                  walking out of frame, reaching for the door)
   2. THRESHOLD — the in-between itself: the doorway, the gate, the
                  stairs, the path, the boat, the corner she turns.
                  This is a real physical action by a real character.
   3. ARRIVING  — she is seen entering place 2 (coming through the
                  gate, stepping onto the road, the light changing
                  on her face as the new place opens up)

Worked example so there is no confusion:
   ❌ NOT CONNECTED: she is getting ready at home → she is dancing
      on the road.  The audience is dropped. It feels like two
      different videos.
   ✅ CONNECTED: getting ready at home → she picks up her dupatta and
      turns to the door → she pulls the door open → she walks down
      the steps → her hand pushes the gate open → the gate swings and
      the road is revealed behind it → she steps onto the road →
      she stops, the music hits → she begins to dance.
   Same story. But now every scene grew out of the one before it.

Rules for the route:
- The film travels ONE WAY. Never return to a place unless the
  return is itself the story, and then it must look different.
- The THRESHOLD beat is never an empty cutaway of scenery. It is
  always a person doing a physical thing — a hand on a gate, feet on
  a step, a curtain pushed aside. If nobody is doing anything in
  it, delete it; it is filler.
- No place gets more than 3 shots in a row.
- Every threshold is a shot. Write it into the event chain now.

⭐ THE PROGRESS LAW — the cure for "the video isn't going anywhere"
A film can obey every rule above and STILL feel stuck. That happens
when the character moves a lot but never gets anywhere — she walks,
she turns, she walks again, all inside the same patch of ground, and
the audience sees the same walls behind her every time. Movement is
not progress. Progress is the BACKGROUND being different.

So the test is not "is she moving?" — it is "has the world behind
her changed?"

For every shot, write one line: WHAT IS BEHIND HER. Then read that
column straight down. If the same backdrop repeats more than twice in
a row, the film has stalled, however busy the character is.

Three ways to force real progress, use all three:

 1. THE BACKGROUND CHANGES EVERY SHOT OR TWO. She leaves the mud wall
    behind and now there are reeds. Then water. Then the far bank. If
    shot 7 could be swapped with shot 4 and nobody would notice, both
    shots are standing still.

 2. DISTANCE TO THE DESTINATION SHRINKS, VISIBLY. Decide the one place
    the whole film is heading toward — the tree, the gate, the river,
    the person. Then put it IN the frame, getting closer:
      shot 3  — a shape far off through the reeds
      shot 6  — the tree now fills a third of the frame behind her
      shot 9  — she is under its branches
    The audience cannot feel progress they cannot see. Give them a
    landmark and let it grow.

 3. THE GROUND ITSELF CHANGES. Swept earth, then dust, then wet soil,
    then river sand. Underfoot texture is the cheapest and strongest
    signal that she has genuinely travelled.

Also: never give two neighbouring shots the same shot size AND the
same camera move. Wide-tracking, then wide-tracking, reads as one
long stuck moment even when the location has changed.

Print a PROGRESS TABLE before the shot list:
   shot # · place · what is behind her · how far to the destination
Then confirm in one line: "The background changes at least every two
shots, and the destination is visibly closer each time it appears."
If it is not true, add the missing shots now.

Print the route, then confirm: "Every place change in this film has
a LEAVING, a THRESHOLD and an ARRIVING." If one is missing, add the
event and renumber the chain.

═══ STEP E3 — THE EFFECT DECISION (numbers, not guesses)  ⭐ v10 ═══

"Some smoke" and "light fog" mean nothing. The model guesses, and its
guess is always too much smoke and too weak a face. So decide the
amounts HERE, in the story, with numbers that mean the same thing in
every shot of the whole film:

   smoke 0-5 · fog 0-5 · dust 0-4 · rain 0-4
   fire: how many flames, how tall in centimetres, and where each one is

  smoke 0 none · 1 one wisp thinner than a wrist · 2 two or three
  wisps at chest height, face still clean · 3 a low band up to the
  waist plus two columns, eyes and mouth still sharp · 4 up to the
  neck, eyes and brow only · 5 whole frame, silhouette only
  fog 0 none · 1 ankle · 2 knee · 3 waist · 4 chest · 5 full frame
  dust 1 motes in a beam · 2 off the ground under footsteps ·
  3 the beam itself visible · 4 the air greys out, distance lost
  rain 1 few slow drops · 2 steady, visible on dark cloth ·
  3 heavy, water standing on the ground · 4 downpour, faces unusable

⭐ THE RULE THAT MATTERS: in any shot where the audience must READ
the face, nothing in front of it goes above 2. Levels 4 and 5 are
for silhouettes, wides and object shots only.

Write it like this in the story, beside the phases:
   "during the bath, heavy steam — smoke level 2, face completely
    clean" · "the curse scene hides her face — smoke level 5,
    silhouette only, the reveal comes later" · "rain level 3,
    water standing on the ground"

Nothing in this film may be described later as "a little smoke" or
"some fog". It is a number, chosen now.

═══ STEP F — THE STORY LOCK ═══

⚠️ THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT OUTPUT OF THIS PROMPT.
Everything after this will be done by tools that have NEVER SEEN
this conversation. If the story is not written down here completely
and plainly, it WILL be lost and the video will end up being about
something else.

Print a self-contained block that makes full sense to a stranger.
Plain words, no jargon:

  ═══════════ STORY LOCK ═══════════
  TITLE:
  LOGLINE: one sentence — who, what they want, what stops them.
  THE FILM IN SIX LINES: what happens, first to last.
  WHERE AND WHEN: place, era, season, time of day, duration.
  VISUAL REGISTER: DIVINE / ROYAL / GROUNDED / STYLISED
  WHO: for each character — name, who they are, what they want in
       this film, and how they are different at the end.
  EVENT CHAIN: the full numbered chain.
  THE ROUTE: place → how she leaves → place → ... with the LEAVING,
       THRESHOLD and ARRIVING beat named for every change of place.
  CAST PRESENCE TIMELINE: the full table.
  THE TURNING POINT:
  HOW IT ENDS: the last image and the feeling it leaves.
  FEELING SCENE BY SCENE: one line each — what the audience feels.
  EMOTIONAL PEAK: the single shot carrying the most feeling.
  THE ONE IMAGE: the frame they must remember.
  ⭐ THE PHASES: the 3-5 emotion phases, in order, with one line of
       look each (light, colour, camera, effects).
  ⭐ THE EFFECTS (numbers): every place smoke, fog, dust, rain or
       fire appears, with its number and whether a face is readable.
       Plus the ONE big effect and the shot it lands on.
  ⭐ MY CHEEZEIN COVERED: a short table — every item of MERI CHEEZEIN
       and the event number where it appears.
  DO NOT CHANGE THESE: the 5 things that would break the film.
  ═══════════════════════════════════

Then tell me exactly this:
"✅ STORY LOCK ready. Copy it and save it. Now paste PROMPT 2
together with this STORY LOCK."

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
MY STORY (rough, from memory — research it and fix it)
⚠️ If the line below is empty or still in brackets, STOP and ask
   me for the story. Never invent one.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

[YAHAN APNI KAHANI 2-4 LINE ME LIKHO]

RUNTIME: [30 / 45 / 60] seconds

═══ STEP E2 — THE ROUTE (how the film travels) ═══

Write THE ROUTE as one unbroken line of places:
   PLACE 1 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 2 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 3 ...

⚠️ THE THRESHOLD LAW — this is what makes scenes feel connected:
Between any two places there must be THREE shown beats, never two:
   1. LEAVING   — she is seen exiting place 1 (standing up, turning,
                  walking out of frame, reaching for the door)
   2. THRESHOLD — the in-between itself: the doorway, the gate, the
                  stairs, the path, the boat, the corner she turns.
                  This is a real physical action by a real character.
   3. ARRIVING  — she is seen entering place 2 (coming through the
                  gate, stepping onto the road, the light changing
                  on her face as the new place opens up)

Worked example so there is no confusion:
   ❌ NOT CONNECTED: she is getting ready at home → she is dancing
      on the road.  The audience is dropped. It feels like two
      different videos.
   ✅ CONNECTED: getting ready at home → she picks up her dupatta and
      turns to the door → she pulls the door open → she walks down
      the steps → her hand pushes the gate open → the gate swings and
      the road is revealed behind it → she steps onto the road →
      she stops, the music hits → she begins to dance.
   Same story. But now every scene grew out of the one before it.

Rules for the route:
- The film travels ONE WAY. Never return to a place unless the
  return is itself the story, and then it must look different.
- The THRESHOLD beat is never an empty cutaway of scenery. It is
  always a person doing a physical thing — a hand on a gate, feet on
  a step, a curtain pushed aside. If nobody is doing anything in
  it, delete it; it is filler.
- No place gets more than 3 shots in a row.
- Every threshold is a shot. Write it into the event chain now.

⭐ THE PROGRESS LAW — the cure for "the video isn't going anywhere"
A film can obey every rule above and STILL feel stuck. That happens
when the character moves a lot but never gets anywhere — she walks,
she turns, she walks again, all inside the same patch of ground, and
the audience sees the same walls behind her every time. Movement is
not progress. Progress is the BACKGROUND being different.

So the test is not "is she moving?" — it is "has the world behind
her changed?"

For every shot, write one line: WHAT IS BEHIND HER. Then read that
column straight down. If the same backdrop repeats more than twice in
a row, the film has stalled, however busy the character is.

Three ways to force real progress, use all three:

 1. THE BACKGROUND CHANGES EVERY SHOT OR TWO. She leaves the mud wall
    behind and now there are reeds. Then water. Then the far bank. If
    shot 7 could be swapped with shot 4 and nobody would notice, both
    shots are standing still.

 2. DISTANCE TO THE DESTINATION SHRINKS, VISIBLY. Decide the one place
    the whole film is heading toward — the tree, the gate, the river,
    the person. Then put it IN the frame, getting closer:
      shot 3  — a shape far off through the reeds
      shot 6  — the tree now fills a third of the frame behind her
      shot 9  — she is under its branches
    The audience cannot feel progress they cannot see. Give them a
    landmark and let it grow.

 3. THE GROUND ITSELF CHANGES. Swept earth, then dust, then wet soil,
    then river sand. Underfoot texture is the cheapest and strongest
    signal that she has genuinely travelled.

Also: never give two neighbouring shots the same shot size AND the
same camera move. Wide-tracking, then wide-tracking, reads as one
long stuck moment even when the location has changed.

Print a PROGRESS TABLE before the shot list:
   shot # · place · what is behind her · how far to the destination
Then confirm in one line: "The background changes at least every two
shots, and the destination is visibly closer each time it appears."
If it is not true, add the missing shots now.

Print the route, then confirm: "Every place change in this film has
a LEAVING, a THRESHOLD and an ARRIVING." If one is missing, add the
event and renumber the chain.
STEP 07 · STEP E3 — THE EFFECT DECISION (numbers, not guesses) ⭐ v10
313 words
dekho
═══ STEP E3 — THE EFFECT DECISION (numbers, not guesses)  ⭐ v10 ═══

"Some smoke" and "light fog" mean nothing. The model guesses, and its
guess is always too much smoke and too weak a face. So decide the
amounts HERE, in the story, with numbers that mean the same thing in
every shot of the whole film:

   smoke 0-5 · fog 0-5 · dust 0-4 · rain 0-4
   fire: how many flames, how tall in centimetres, and where each one is

  smoke 0 none · 1 one wisp thinner than a wrist · 2 two or three
  wisps at chest height, face still clean · 3 a low band up to the
  waist plus two columns, eyes and mouth still sharp · 4 up to the
  neck, eyes and brow only · 5 whole frame, silhouette only
  fog 0 none · 1 ankle · 2 knee · 3 waist · 4 chest · 5 full frame
  dust 1 motes in a beam · 2 off the ground under footsteps ·
  3 the beam itself visible · 4 the air greys out, distance lost
  rain 1 few slow drops · 2 steady, visible on dark cloth ·
  3 heavy, water standing on the ground · 4 downpour, faces unusable

⭐ THE RULE THAT MATTERS: in any shot where the audience must READ
the face, nothing in front of it goes above 2. Levels 4 and 5 are
for silhouettes, wides and object shots only.

Write it like this in the story, beside the phases:
   "during the bath, heavy steam — smoke level 2, face completely
    clean" · "the curse scene hides her face — smoke level 5,
    silhouette only, the reveal comes later" · "rain level 3,
    water standing on the ground"

Nothing in this film may be described later as "a little smoke" or
"some fog". It is a number, chosen now.
STEP 08 · STEP F — THE STORY LOCK
375 words
dekho
═══ STEP F — THE STORY LOCK ═══

⚠️ THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT OUTPUT OF THIS PROMPT.
Everything after this will be done by tools that have NEVER SEEN
this conversation. If the story is not written down here completely
and plainly, it WILL be lost and the video will end up being about
something else.

Print a self-contained block that makes full sense to a stranger.
Plain words, no jargon:

  ═══════════ STORY LOCK ═══════════
  TITLE:
  LOGLINE: one sentence — who, what they want, what stops them.
  THE FILM IN SIX LINES: what happens, first to last.
  WHERE AND WHEN: place, era, season, time of day, duration.
  VISUAL REGISTER: DIVINE / ROYAL / GROUNDED / STYLISED
  WHO: for each character — name, who they are, what they want in
       this film, and how they are different at the end.
  EVENT CHAIN: the full numbered chain.
  THE ROUTE: place → how she leaves → place → ... with the LEAVING,
       THRESHOLD and ARRIVING beat named for every change of place.
  CAST PRESENCE TIMELINE: the full table.
  THE TURNING POINT:
  HOW IT ENDS: the last image and the feeling it leaves.
  FEELING SCENE BY SCENE: one line each — what the audience feels.
  EMOTIONAL PEAK: the single shot carrying the most feeling.
  THE ONE IMAGE: the frame they must remember.
  ⭐ THE PHASES: the 3-5 emotion phases, in order, with one line of
       look each (light, colour, camera, effects).
  ⭐ THE EFFECTS (numbers): every place smoke, fog, dust, rain or
       fire appears, with its number and whether a face is readable.
       Plus the ONE big effect and the shot it lands on.
  ⭐ MY CHEEZEIN COVERED: a short table — every item of MERI CHEEZEIN
       and the event number where it appears.
  DO NOT CHANGE THESE: the 5 things that would break the film.
  ═══════════════════════════════════

Then tell me exactly this:
"✅ STORY LOCK ready. Copy it and save it. Now paste PROMPT 2
together with this STORY LOCK."

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
MY STORY (rough, from memory — research it and fix it)
⚠️ If the line below is empty or still in brackets, STOP and ask
   me for the story. Never invent one.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

[YAHAN APNI KAHANI 2-4 LINE ME LIKHO]

RUNTIME: [30 / 45 / 60] seconds
PROMPT 2 · 3799 words

CHARACTERS + DUNIYA

sirf portraits & duniya — yahan video prompt nahi banta

poora prompt padho 
Now build the reference images for this film.

━━━ RULES FOR THIS WHOLE PROMPT ━━━
• Do not ask me any questions except the one style question below.
• NEVER ask me for a reference image, an upload, or an example.
  I have nothing to give you. Everything you need is in the STORY
  LOCK at the bottom of this message. Read it and decide yourself.
• You are not generating images here. You are WRITING THE IMAGE
  PROMPTS that I will paste into an image tool myself. Every prompt
  must be complete, self-contained and copy-paste ready.

━━━ ⛔ RULE 0 — STEP LOCK (ye step sirf CHEHRE aur JAGAH ka hai) ━━━
This step makes characters and places. Nothing else. Not one thing else.

  ✅ ALLOWED in your answer:
     · the story read back in eight lines
     · the style test prompts (STYLE-A2 / STYLE-A)
     · the cast list
     · the DNA line for every person
     · the location DNA line for every place
     · the palette
     · ONE upload portrait prompt per person
     · the character sheet and world sheet prompts (planning only)
     · the opening frame prompt
     · the upload list

  ❌ NOT ALLOWED in this answer, not even as an example, not even in
     a small box, not even "just to show you":
     · any video prompt
     · any camera movement, lens or camera height for a shot
     · any timestamp like [00:00-00:02]
     · any shot list, shot number or shot card
     · any beat timeline
     · any music, caption or hashtag

If even one video prompt or one camera move for a shot appears in
your answer, the whole answer is wrong. Throw it away and write it
again using the allowed list only.

⭐ And finish your answer with this exact line, so I always know what
is next:

   "STEP 2 POORA. Ab PROMPT 3 paste karo — shot cards wahi banayega.
    Us se pehle koi video prompt nahi banega."

━━━ FIRST: THE STYLE ━━━
Do not ask me what style I want, and do not ask me for a reference.
YOU choose one single moment from this film to use as the style
test — pick the strongest emotional moment from the STORY LOCK, one
character, one clear action. State which moment you picked and why,
in one line.

Then write TWO complete image prompts of THAT SAME moment — same
character, same pose, same framing, same light. Only the visual
treatment differs. Each prompt must fully describe the character,
the place and the action on its own, because the image tool has
seen nothing else.

Label them clearly and give each a filename:
STYLE-A2.png, STYLE-A.png

  STYLE A2 — MODERN LIVE ACTION ⭐ DEFAULT
  A real actor photographed on location on a modern digital cinema
  camera, ARRI Alexa 35 with Cooke S7 anamorphic lenses, clean and
  sharp. ONE motivated light source with a named direction, a clear
  bright side and a clear shadow side on the face. Healthy skin with
  fine natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and
  cheekbones, individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric
  with a soft sheen where it catches the light, bright polished
  metal, true depth of field falloff, very deep blacks, clean bright
  highlights, strong contrast, and a restrained colour palette where
  most of the frame sits near grey and only ONE colour is allowed to
  be strong. Looks like a frame from a film released this year.

  STYLE A — HIGH CONTRAST CINEMA
  The same capture discipline and the same modern cleanliness, one
  motivated light source, deeper shadows, stronger contrast, shallow
  depth of field. Bolder and more graphic, still a photograph.

Tell me: "Generate these two images, look at them, and tell me which
you prefer — A2 or A?" Then WAIT for my answer.
Do not continue to the character sheets until I have replied.

⚠️ THERE IS NO "DIVINE GLOW" STYLE AND YOU MUST NOT INVENT ONE.
A glow style is how a film turns into calendar art. If a divine
register is needed, it is carried by MATERIALS, not by light — see
the rule below.

━━━ THEN: THE IMAGE PROMPTS ━━━
Once I pick, write me the actual copy-paste image prompts. I will
paste each one into an image tool myself. Number them and give me a
filename for each so the files stay organised.

⚠️ IF THE REGISTER IS DIVINE OR ROYAL, read this first. It is the
single easiest way to wreck a film, and almost everyone wrecks it.

THE RULE: DIVINITY COMES FROM MATERIALS, NEVER FROM LIGHT.

A god looks divine because of what he is WEARING and what he is MADE
of — heavy raw silk with a deep sheen, bright worked gold with sharp
deep engraving that catches the light, big uncut gems, dense fresh
flowers, skin lit like skin. He does not look divine because the air
is full of golden mist. He looks divine because he is wearing more,
and better, than anyone else in the frame.

The moment you add glow, the image stops being a photograph. These
words are what produce that plastic, computer-rendered, calendar-art
result, and they are BANNED in every prompt you write:

  glowing skin · luminous · radiant · aura · halo · god rays ·
  light shafts · sunbeams through trees · golden mist · golden haze ·
  divine light · ethereal · dreamlike · otherworldly · magical ·
  shimmering · sparkling · floating petals · floating particles ·
  light bloom · lens flare · backlit glow · rim glow · heavenly

Also banned, because they make a god look poor rather than real:
  simple · plain · ordinary · rustic · humble · unadorned ·
  weathered · faded · coarse cotton · no jewellery

So: how a divine character is built instead.

  ❌ CALENDAR ART: "Krishna with glowing luminous blue skin, divine
     aura, god rays streaming through the trees, golden mist,
     floating petals, ethereal dreamlike atmosphere"

  ✅ A PHOTOGRAPH OF A DIVINE FIGURE: "a man of about twenty-two,
     his skin an even deep blue-grey, smooth and unlined with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen along the cheekbone, lit from
     the front left by low sun so the right side of his face falls
     into real shadow; a heavy saffron raw-silk dhoti with a
     hand-woven gold zari border, the silk falling in sharp folds; a
     gold crown of worked metal, bright and polished, the engraving
     cut deep and catching the light, and one peacock feather with
     separated barbs; a thick garland of fresh marigolds and tulsi"

  Read them again. The second one has MORE gold, MORE silk, MORE
  ornament. It is more divine, not less. It just refuses to glow.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ MUTED IS NOT THE SAME AS FADED — AND THIS TRIPS EVERYONE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Expensive modern footage is NOT colourful. Look at any big film of
the last ten years and most of the frame sits near grey, with one
colour allowed to be strong — skin, or fire, or one piece of cloth.
That is not the same thing as looking old.

  MODERN AND EXPENSIVE          OLD AND CHEAP
  colour pulled toward grey     colour washed out everywhere
  blacks are deep and solid     blacks are grey and lifted
  highlights clean and bright   highlights dull and flat
  sharp, no grain               soft, grainy, hazy
  one strong accent colour      no colour anywhere
  → looks like this year        → looks like a scanned photo

So write: a restrained palette near grey, ONE strong accent colour,
very deep blacks, clean bright highlights, strong contrast, sharp.
Never write: faded, washed, muted-and-soft, grainy, hazy, dusty,
low contrast.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ THE AGE LINE — WHY EVERYONE COMES OUT LOOKING OLD
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
If you do not state an age, the model picks one, and it almost
always picks older than you meant. Worse, every word used to make
an image look real — pores, lines, weathered, tarnish, worn,
faded, grain, muted — is also a word that means OLD. Use them on a
young character and a twenty-year-old comes back at forty-five,
and the whole film looks like it was shot decades ago.

So every character DNA line MUST open with an age, in years, and
with the words that go with that age:

  ✅ "a woman of about nineteen, smooth unlined skin with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen on the cheekbone, full
     cheeks, bright clear eyes, thick glossy black hair"
  ✅ "a man of about twenty-two, a lean jaw with faint stubble
     only along the edge, smooth forehead, bright clear eyes"
  ✅ "a woman of about sixty, deep lines across the forehead and
     at the eyes, thinning grey-streaked hair, loose skin at the
     throat"   ← ageing words ONLY when the character IS old

For anyone under thirty, these words are banned from the DNA line
and from every image prompt: pores, wrinkles, lines, crow's feet,
weathered, leathery, sun-damaged, rough, coarse, gaunt, sagging,
grey hair, sunken, tired, aged, elderly, mature.

AND THE SAME FOR THE WORLD. If the film is set today, say so in the
world DNA and say it in the picture: clean modern clothing with
sharp new stitching, undamaged paint, working electric light,
present-day haircuts, glass, steel, fresh fabric dye. If the film
is set in the past, say WHICH period and say it through objects —
never through decay. A period is a set of things, not a layer of
dust. Faded, peeling, cracked and crumbling do not say "old era",
they say "low budget".

⚠️⚠️ AND THE BIGGEST ONE: NEVER WRITE THE DEITY'S NAME IN AN IMAGE
OR VIDEO PROMPT.

Write "Krishna" and the model does not picture a person. It reaches
for everything ever labelled Krishna in its training — and that is
overwhelmingly devotional poster art, temple calendars, glowing
murals and 3D devotional renders. The name alone summons the style,
and no amount of camera wording afterwards will beat it. The same is
true of Radha, Shiva, Ram, Hanuman, Durga, and of "Lord", "Shri",
"deity", "divine figure", "God".

So the name lives in the STORY, the plan and the shot titles — where
it belongs and where it helps me follow along. It NEVER appears
inside a prompt I paste into a tool.

Inside a prompt, the character is only ever a physical human being:

  ❌ "Lord Krishna standing under a kadamba tree, divine, devotional"
  ✅ "a young man of about twenty, lean and broad-shouldered, skin an
     even deep blue-grey, shoulder-length curling black hair, a tall
     worked-gold crown holding one peacock feather, a saffron
     raw-silk dhoti with a woven gold border, a bamboo flute in both
     hands, standing under a broad kadamba tree"

Everything that makes him recognisable is still there — the blue
skin, the crown, the peacock feather, the flute, the dhoti. Any
viewer knows exactly who he is. The model, meanwhile, has been handed
a photograph brief instead of a poster brief.

This is what the CHARACTER DNA LINE is for. Write the DNA line so it
never contains the name, then paste it as-is. Put the name only in
the card heading, like "SHOT 07 — Krishna under the kadamba".

⚠️ ALSO BANNED, for the same reason — these are poster words, not
photography words, and every one of them pulls toward a render:
  8K · 4K · ultra-realistic · hyper-realistic · ultra-detailed ·
  highly detailed · masterpiece · premium · devotional · spiritual ·
  majestic · epic · breathtaking · stunning · beautiful ·
  cinematic (used on its own as an adjective) · high budget ·
  IMAX quality · poster · artwork · rendering
Say the lens, the film stock and the light instead. Those are what a
real photograph is described by.

Every divine woman still needs at least: kohl-lined eyes, bindi,
maang tikka, nath, jhumka, choker plus a long necklace, bajuband,
bangles, mehndi, payal, zari silk, a gold-bordered dupatta, fresh
flowers in the hair.
Every divine man still needs at least: a jewelled mukut, tilak,
kundal, vaijayanti mala, kantha, bajuband, kada, zari dhoti,
kamarbandh, anklets, and his signature object.
Describe each of those as a real physical object with a weight, a
weave, a finish and a small imperfection. Never as something that
shines by itself.

━━━ 2A. CAST LIST ━━━
List EVERY person who appears — even for one shot. Parents, friends,
elders, servants. Anyone without a sheet will change face between
shots. For each: name, role in a line, three trait words, age.

⚠️ AGE STAGES: if the film spans time, the SAME character needs a
SEPARATE sheet for EACH age. State explicitly which features carry
across all ages — eye shape, face structure, skin tone, marks — so
it reads as one person growing up, not three different people.

━━━ 2B. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — make this one FIRST ━━━

⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE THE SHEET. It prevents two real failures: faces
that come out looking cartoonish, and having to upload a pile of
images for every shot.

For each character produce TWO different things, and they have
different jobs:

  1. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — one clean photographic portrait: a single
     frame filling the whole image, one person, every surface blank
     and unmarked. THIS is the only file that ever gets uploaded into
     an image or video tool. Filename: CHAR-[name].png

  2. THE CHARACTER SHEET — the labelled multi-view grid in 2C below.
     This is a PLANNING document for me to look at and for you to
     write the DNA line from. It is NEVER uploaded into a tool.

⚠️ WHY THIS SPLIT EXISTS — THE CARTOON FACE BUG
A model reads an uploaded reference as a whole frame and copies its
LOOK, not just its content. A character sheet is a designed artifact:
a grid, flat studio lighting, labels, colour swatches, several heads
floating on one canvas. Upload that into a video tool and it faithfully
reproduces exactly those qualities — flat, graphic, illustrated. That
is the "it started looking like a cartoon" failure, and it is caused
by the reference, not the prompt. Nothing you write in words will
outvote it.

So the upload portrait must look like a photograph of a real person
and nothing else. Write its prompt like this:

   Photographed on a modern digital cinema camera, 85mm lens at
   f/2.8, eye level, camera height [n] centimetres. A single portrait
   of one person, vertical 9:16, three-quarter view from the waist up.
   Lit by soft window light from the front left at 45 degrees, so the
   left of the face is bright and the right falls into gentle real
   shadow; weak bounce fill from the right. Daylight temperature.
   [the character's full description]
   Standing relaxed against a plain mid-grey wall, facing camera,
   eyes looking into the lens, a calm natural expression, lips
   closed.
   Exactly one person in this frame, no one else, no duplicate, no
   second figure, nobody in the background.
   [the full positive closing block]
   One single frame filling the whole image, one continuous plain
   wall behind, every surface blank and unmarked.
   ⚠️ Write NO ban list here. A tool with one text box reads "no
   grid, no labels, no glow" as a request for grids, labels and
   glow. State only what IS in the frame.

   SAVE AS: CHAR-[name].png
   ✅ DEKHO: ye ek asli insaan ki photo lagni chahiye. Agar isme
   grid, label ya flat studio look hai, dobara banao — wahi cartoon
   wali galti karega.

⚠️ AND NEVER STACK REFERENCES
One upload is stronger than four. Each extra reference image adds its
own texture and style to the blend, and the more you stack the further
the result drifts from photographic. Never upload the character sheet,
the world sheet, the style image and the shot still all together.
 • IMAGE prompts: upload the character portrait only. One per
   character actually in the shot. Nothing else.
 • VIDEO prompts: upload the shot still only — plus the next shot's
   still if, and only if, you are pinning a last frame.
 • The world sheet and style image are for YOU to look at while
   writing. They are never uploaded.

━━━ 2C. CHARACTER SHEET PROMPTS (planning only, never uploaded) ━━━
One per character per age stage. Write each as a ready-to-paste
prompt. Use this exact structure and fill in the character:

   Create a CHARACTER SHEET in one single image, 2:3 portrait, on a
   plain light grey studio backdrop with even soft lighting.
   CHARACTER: [name, age, role]
   [full description — face, build, costume, every ornament, colours,
    skin tone, distinguishing marks]
   TOP ROW — four views, identical face in all four:
     1. FACE, head and shoulders, looking at lens
     2. FRONT, full body, standing
     3. BACK, full body from directly behind — hair, braid, the back
        of the costume and how the garment falls
     4. SIDE, full profile
   BOTTOM ROW — five tight macro tiles:
     1. TILAK or forehead marking — exact shape and colour
     2. HAIR — ornament, braid pattern, parting
        (for a man: TURBAN or CROWN detail)
     3. JEWELLERY — ear, neck and arm, clearly readable
     4. HAND — bangles, mehndi, rings, nails
     5. FEET — anklets, toe rings, sole
        (for a man: his SIGNATURE OBJECT)
   Label every view and tile with small text underneath.
   Below the tiles, a bar of five colour swatches from this
   character: skin, main garment, accent, metal, accessory.
   The face must be IDENTICAL in every view and tile.
   Photographic realism, word for word: "healthy skin with fine
   natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and cheekbones,
   individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric with a soft
   sheen where it catches the light, bright polished metal with deep
   sharp engraving, clean modern digital capture, very deep blacks,
   strong contrast, a restrained palette with one strong colour"
   [STYLE LINE FROM MY PICK — paste it as WORDS. Never upload the
    style image itself; an uploaded style reference drags its own
    texture into the result.]

Under each sheet prompt, print that character's DNA LINE in its own
separate block, clearly marked, like this:

   ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
   ║  DNA — RADHA                                     ║
   ║  the heading names her so I can follow along.    ║
   ║  the LINE below never says her name — copy the   ║
   ║  line only, whole, into every prompt she is in   ║
   ╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
   a young Indian woman of about eighteen, slender, a soft
   oval face with a rounded chin and long dark eyes, fair
   golden-brown skin with fine natural texture, very long jet black
   hair in one thick braid bound with gold cord and threaded with
   white jasmine buds, a deep rose pink silk lehenga with dense gold
   zari embroidery along the hem, a fitted matching choli, a pale
   gold silk dupatta over her left shoulder, a gold maang tikka with
   a pearl drop, a small red bindi, a gold nath with a fine pearl
   chain to her ear, large gold jhumkas, a pearl and emerald choker,
   gold armlets, stacked gold and red glass bangles on both wrists, a
   fine gold waist chain, heavy silver anklets with small bells
   ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

That is the format and roughly the length. One unbroken line. It
must name, in this order: age and build · face shape and skin tone ·
hair, how it is tied, what is in it · every garment with colour and
fabric and how it sits · every ornament item by item · anything
always in their hand.

⚠️ Notice what the line does NOT contain: her name, and any word
like goddess, divine or devotional. The heading above the box is
where the name lives. The line itself describes a human being only —
that is what keeps the output a photograph instead of poster art.

⚠️ WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN ANYTHING ELSE
This exact line gets pasted, word for word, into EVERY image prompt
that character appears in, for every shot, right to the end of the
film. Never shorten it. Never reword it. Never replace it with "the
same woman as before" or "the same person from the uploaded image".

Repeating the whole line every time is the only thing that keeps the
face the same in shot 15 as in shot 1. Changing even a few words is
exactly what makes the face drift halfway through a film.

(The VIDEO prompt for the same shot works the other way round — see
PROMPT 3, BLOCK 0. There the uploaded still already carries the face,
so the video prompt carries a SHORT lock, not the full line. Full
line in image prompts. Short lock in video prompts. Never mix them up.)

Then give me the filename: CHAR-[name]-[age].png

━━━ 2D. WORLD SHEET PROMPTS (planning only, never uploaded) ━━━
One per location. Same format:

   Create a LOCATION REFERENCE SHEET in one image — a clean grid of
   ten photographic tiles of the SAME place, labelled underneath.
   LOCATION: [name, era, season, time of day]
   1  WIDE ESTABLISHING, hero light
   2  THE SAME PLACE FROM THE OPPOSITE SIDE
   3  THE SECONDARY AREA
   4  ONE SIGNATURE DETAIL of this world, close
   5  GROUND OR WATER LEVEL, lowest possible camera
   6  TEXTURE MACRO — the actual surface material
   7  SHOT THROUGH A FOREGROUND — past trees, pillars, reeds
   8  HIGH VIEW looking down
   9  THE SAME PLACE AT [other time of day]
   10 SKY REFERENCE — cloud, haze, sun position
   Along the right edge, six colour swatches labelled: earth, water,
   trees, [signature element], sky, stone.
   Every tile the same place, same season, same era, consistent
   light direction. Photographic, natural light, real texture.
   [STYLE LINE FROM MY PICK]

Filename: WORLD-[place].png
Under it, print the LOCATION DNA LINE — one unbroken line naming the
place, era, season, time of day, the ground, the main structures,
the materials, and what is growing or hanging there.

━━━ 2E. THE COLOUR PALETTE ━━━
From the sheets, write out the film's palette — six colours with
what each comes from. Then state: "This palette is now law. No
colour outside it may enter any frame."
Also mark which SINGLE colour is allowed to be loud, and where in
the film it appears.

━━━ 2F. THE OPENING FRAME ━━━
One finished, full-quality frame of the film's opening image — not a
reference, the actual frame as it will appear. Write it as a ready
prompt. Filename: HERO-opening.png
Tell me: "This is the quality bar. If any later frame is weaker than
this one, regenerate that frame."

━━━ FINISH WITH THIS ━━━
Print a numbered checklist of every image I must now generate, in
order, with its filename, in two groups:
   GROUP 1 — the style test (2 images, then WAIT for my pick)
   GROUP 2 — everything else (portraits, sheets, world, hero frame)
Then say:

"✅ Generate these images now, one at a time, and save them with
these exact filenames. When they are done, paste PROMPT 3 together
with the STORY LOCK."

And remember the last line of Rule 0 — the STEP 2 POORA line.
is prompt ke steps — har step ka apna button (11 steps)
STEP 01 · RULES FOR THIS WHOLE PROMPT
89 words
dekho
━━━ RULES FOR THIS WHOLE PROMPT ━━━
• Do not ask me any questions except the one style question below.
• NEVER ask me for a reference image, an upload, or an example.
  I have nothing to give you. Everything you need is in the STORY
  LOCK at the bottom of this message. Read it and decide yourself.
• You are not generating images here. You are WRITING THE IMAGE
  PROMPTS that I will paste into an image tool myself. Every prompt
  must be complete, self-contained and copy-paste ready.
STEP 02 · ⛔ RULE 0 — STEP LOCK (ye step sirf CHEHRE aur JAGAH ka hai)
229 words
dekho
━━━ ⛔ RULE 0 — STEP LOCK (ye step sirf CHEHRE aur JAGAH ka hai) ━━━
This step makes characters and places. Nothing else. Not one thing else.

  ✅ ALLOWED in your answer:
     · the story read back in eight lines
     · the style test prompts (STYLE-A2 / STYLE-A)
     · the cast list
     · the DNA line for every person
     · the location DNA line for every place
     · the palette
     · ONE upload portrait prompt per person
     · the character sheet and world sheet prompts (planning only)
     · the opening frame prompt
     · the upload list

  ❌ NOT ALLOWED in this answer, not even as an example, not even in
     a small box, not even "just to show you":
     · any video prompt
     · any camera movement, lens or camera height for a shot
     · any timestamp like [00:00-00:02]
     · any shot list, shot number or shot card
     · any beat timeline
     · any music, caption or hashtag

If even one video prompt or one camera move for a shot appears in
your answer, the whole answer is wrong. Throw it away and write it
again using the allowed list only.

⭐ And finish your answer with this exact line, so I always know what
is next:

   "STEP 2 POORA. Ab PROMPT 3 paste karo — shot cards wahi banayega.
    Us se pehle koi video prompt nahi banega."
STEP 03 · FIRST: THE STYLE
359 words
dekho
━━━ FIRST: THE STYLE ━━━
Do not ask me what style I want, and do not ask me for a reference.
YOU choose one single moment from this film to use as the style
test — pick the strongest emotional moment from the STORY LOCK, one
character, one clear action. State which moment you picked and why,
in one line.

Then write TWO complete image prompts of THAT SAME moment — same
character, same pose, same framing, same light. Only the visual
treatment differs. Each prompt must fully describe the character,
the place and the action on its own, because the image tool has
seen nothing else.

Label them clearly and give each a filename:
STYLE-A2.png, STYLE-A.png

  STYLE A2 — MODERN LIVE ACTION ⭐ DEFAULT
  A real actor photographed on location on a modern digital cinema
  camera, ARRI Alexa 35 with Cooke S7 anamorphic lenses, clean and
  sharp. ONE motivated light source with a named direction, a clear
  bright side and a clear shadow side on the face. Healthy skin with
  fine natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and
  cheekbones, individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric
  with a soft sheen where it catches the light, bright polished
  metal, true depth of field falloff, very deep blacks, clean bright
  highlights, strong contrast, and a restrained colour palette where
  most of the frame sits near grey and only ONE colour is allowed to
  be strong. Looks like a frame from a film released this year.

  STYLE A — HIGH CONTRAST CINEMA
  The same capture discipline and the same modern cleanliness, one
  motivated light source, deeper shadows, stronger contrast, shallow
  depth of field. Bolder and more graphic, still a photograph.

Tell me: "Generate these two images, look at them, and tell me which
you prefer — A2 or A?" Then WAIT for my answer.
Do not continue to the character sheets until I have replied.

⚠️ THERE IS NO "DIVINE GLOW" STYLE AND YOU MUST NOT INVENT ONE.
A glow style is how a film turns into calendar art. If a divine
register is needed, it is carried by MATERIALS, not by light — see
the rule below.
STEP 04 · THEN: THE IMAGE PROMPTS
1342 words
dekho
━━━ THEN: THE IMAGE PROMPTS ━━━
Once I pick, write me the actual copy-paste image prompts. I will
paste each one into an image tool myself. Number them and give me a
filename for each so the files stay organised.

⚠️ IF THE REGISTER IS DIVINE OR ROYAL, read this first. It is the
single easiest way to wreck a film, and almost everyone wrecks it.

THE RULE: DIVINITY COMES FROM MATERIALS, NEVER FROM LIGHT.

A god looks divine because of what he is WEARING and what he is MADE
of — heavy raw silk with a deep sheen, bright worked gold with sharp
deep engraving that catches the light, big uncut gems, dense fresh
flowers, skin lit like skin. He does not look divine because the air
is full of golden mist. He looks divine because he is wearing more,
and better, than anyone else in the frame.

The moment you add glow, the image stops being a photograph. These
words are what produce that plastic, computer-rendered, calendar-art
result, and they are BANNED in every prompt you write:

  glowing skin · luminous · radiant · aura · halo · god rays ·
  light shafts · sunbeams through trees · golden mist · golden haze ·
  divine light · ethereal · dreamlike · otherworldly · magical ·
  shimmering · sparkling · floating petals · floating particles ·
  light bloom · lens flare · backlit glow · rim glow · heavenly

Also banned, because they make a god look poor rather than real:
  simple · plain · ordinary · rustic · humble · unadorned ·
  weathered · faded · coarse cotton · no jewellery

So: how a divine character is built instead.

  ❌ CALENDAR ART: "Krishna with glowing luminous blue skin, divine
     aura, god rays streaming through the trees, golden mist,
     floating petals, ethereal dreamlike atmosphere"

  ✅ A PHOTOGRAPH OF A DIVINE FIGURE: "a man of about twenty-two,
     his skin an even deep blue-grey, smooth and unlined with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen along the cheekbone, lit from
     the front left by low sun so the right side of his face falls
     into real shadow; a heavy saffron raw-silk dhoti with a
     hand-woven gold zari border, the silk falling in sharp folds; a
     gold crown of worked metal, bright and polished, the engraving
     cut deep and catching the light, and one peacock feather with
     separated barbs; a thick garland of fresh marigolds and tulsi"

  Read them again. The second one has MORE gold, MORE silk, MORE
  ornament. It is more divine, not less. It just refuses to glow.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ MUTED IS NOT THE SAME AS FADED — AND THIS TRIPS EVERYONE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Expensive modern footage is NOT colourful. Look at any big film of
the last ten years and most of the frame sits near grey, with one
colour allowed to be strong — skin, or fire, or one piece of cloth.
That is not the same thing as looking old.

  MODERN AND EXPENSIVE          OLD AND CHEAP
  colour pulled toward grey     colour washed out everywhere
  blacks are deep and solid     blacks are grey and lifted
  highlights clean and bright   highlights dull and flat
  sharp, no grain               soft, grainy, hazy
  one strong accent colour      no colour anywhere
  → looks like this year        → looks like a scanned photo

So write: a restrained palette near grey, ONE strong accent colour,
very deep blacks, clean bright highlights, strong contrast, sharp.
Never write: faded, washed, muted-and-soft, grainy, hazy, dusty,
low contrast.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ THE AGE LINE — WHY EVERYONE COMES OUT LOOKING OLD
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
If you do not state an age, the model picks one, and it almost
always picks older than you meant. Worse, every word used to make
an image look real — pores, lines, weathered, tarnish, worn,
faded, grain, muted — is also a word that means OLD. Use them on a
young character and a twenty-year-old comes back at forty-five,
and the whole film looks like it was shot decades ago.

So every character DNA line MUST open with an age, in years, and
with the words that go with that age:

  ✅ "a woman of about nineteen, smooth unlined skin with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen on the cheekbone, full
     cheeks, bright clear eyes, thick glossy black hair"
  ✅ "a man of about twenty-two, a lean jaw with faint stubble
     only along the edge, smooth forehead, bright clear eyes"
  ✅ "a woman of about sixty, deep lines across the forehead and
     at the eyes, thinning grey-streaked hair, loose skin at the
     throat"   ← ageing words ONLY when the character IS old

For anyone under thirty, these words are banned from the DNA line
and from every image prompt: pores, wrinkles, lines, crow's feet,
weathered, leathery, sun-damaged, rough, coarse, gaunt, sagging,
grey hair, sunken, tired, aged, elderly, mature.

AND THE SAME FOR THE WORLD. If the film is set today, say so in the
world DNA and say it in the picture: clean modern clothing with
sharp new stitching, undamaged paint, working electric light,
present-day haircuts, glass, steel, fresh fabric dye. If the film
is set in the past, say WHICH period and say it through objects —
never through decay. A period is a set of things, not a layer of
dust. Faded, peeling, cracked and crumbling do not say "old era",
they say "low budget".

⚠️⚠️ AND THE BIGGEST ONE: NEVER WRITE THE DEITY'S NAME IN AN IMAGE
OR VIDEO PROMPT.

Write "Krishna" and the model does not picture a person. It reaches
for everything ever labelled Krishna in its training — and that is
overwhelmingly devotional poster art, temple calendars, glowing
murals and 3D devotional renders. The name alone summons the style,
and no amount of camera wording afterwards will beat it. The same is
true of Radha, Shiva, Ram, Hanuman, Durga, and of "Lord", "Shri",
"deity", "divine figure", "God".

So the name lives in the STORY, the plan and the shot titles — where
it belongs and where it helps me follow along. It NEVER appears
inside a prompt I paste into a tool.

Inside a prompt, the character is only ever a physical human being:

  ❌ "Lord Krishna standing under a kadamba tree, divine, devotional"
  ✅ "a young man of about twenty, lean and broad-shouldered, skin an
     even deep blue-grey, shoulder-length curling black hair, a tall
     worked-gold crown holding one peacock feather, a saffron
     raw-silk dhoti with a woven gold border, a bamboo flute in both
     hands, standing under a broad kadamba tree"

Everything that makes him recognisable is still there — the blue
skin, the crown, the peacock feather, the flute, the dhoti. Any
viewer knows exactly who he is. The model, meanwhile, has been handed
a photograph brief instead of a poster brief.

This is what the CHARACTER DNA LINE is for. Write the DNA line so it
never contains the name, then paste it as-is. Put the name only in
the card heading, like "SHOT 07 — Krishna under the kadamba".

⚠️ ALSO BANNED, for the same reason — these are poster words, not
photography words, and every one of them pulls toward a render:
  8K · 4K · ultra-realistic · hyper-realistic · ultra-detailed ·
  highly detailed · masterpiece · premium · devotional · spiritual ·
  majestic · epic · breathtaking · stunning · beautiful ·
  cinematic (used on its own as an adjective) · high budget ·
  IMAX quality · poster · artwork · rendering
Say the lens, the film stock and the light instead. Those are what a
real photograph is described by.

Every divine woman still needs at least: kohl-lined eyes, bindi,
maang tikka, nath, jhumka, choker plus a long necklace, bajuband,
bangles, mehndi, payal, zari silk, a gold-bordered dupatta, fresh
flowers in the hair.
Every divine man still needs at least: a jewelled mukut, tilak,
kundal, vaijayanti mala, kantha, bajuband, kada, zari dhoti,
kamarbandh, anklets, and his signature object.
Describe each of those as a real physical object with a weight, a
weave, a finish and a small imperfection. Never as something that
shines by itself.
STEP 05 · 2A. CAST LIST
86 words
dekho
━━━ 2A. CAST LIST ━━━
List EVERY person who appears — even for one shot. Parents, friends,
elders, servants. Anyone without a sheet will change face between
shots. For each: name, role in a line, three trait words, age.

⚠️ AGE STAGES: if the film spans time, the SAME character needs a
SEPARATE sheet for EACH age. State explicitly which features carry
across all ages — eye shape, face structure, skin tone, marks — so
it reads as one person growing up, not three different people.
STEP 06 · 2B. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — make this one FIRST
552 words
dekho
━━━ 2B. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — make this one FIRST ━━━

⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE THE SHEET. It prevents two real failures: faces
that come out looking cartoonish, and having to upload a pile of
images for every shot.

For each character produce TWO different things, and they have
different jobs:

  1. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — one clean photographic portrait: a single
     frame filling the whole image, one person, every surface blank
     and unmarked. THIS is the only file that ever gets uploaded into
     an image or video tool. Filename: CHAR-[name].png

  2. THE CHARACTER SHEET — the labelled multi-view grid in 2C below.
     This is a PLANNING document for me to look at and for you to
     write the DNA line from. It is NEVER uploaded into a tool.

⚠️ WHY THIS SPLIT EXISTS — THE CARTOON FACE BUG
A model reads an uploaded reference as a whole frame and copies its
LOOK, not just its content. A character sheet is a designed artifact:
a grid, flat studio lighting, labels, colour swatches, several heads
floating on one canvas. Upload that into a video tool and it faithfully
reproduces exactly those qualities — flat, graphic, illustrated. That
is the "it started looking like a cartoon" failure, and it is caused
by the reference, not the prompt. Nothing you write in words will
outvote it.

So the upload portrait must look like a photograph of a real person
and nothing else. Write its prompt like this:

   Photographed on a modern digital cinema camera, 85mm lens at
   f/2.8, eye level, camera height [n] centimetres. A single portrait
   of one person, vertical 9:16, three-quarter view from the waist up.
   Lit by soft window light from the front left at 45 degrees, so the
   left of the face is bright and the right falls into gentle real
   shadow; weak bounce fill from the right. Daylight temperature.
   [the character's full description]
   Standing relaxed against a plain mid-grey wall, facing camera,
   eyes looking into the lens, a calm natural expression, lips
   closed.
   Exactly one person in this frame, no one else, no duplicate, no
   second figure, nobody in the background.
   [the full positive closing block]
   One single frame filling the whole image, one continuous plain
   wall behind, every surface blank and unmarked.
   ⚠️ Write NO ban list here. A tool with one text box reads "no
   grid, no labels, no glow" as a request for grids, labels and
   glow. State only what IS in the frame.

   SAVE AS: CHAR-[name].png
   ✅ DEKHO: ye ek asli insaan ki photo lagni chahiye. Agar isme
   grid, label ya flat studio look hai, dobara banao — wahi cartoon
   wali galti karega.

⚠️ AND NEVER STACK REFERENCES
One upload is stronger than four. Each extra reference image adds its
own texture and style to the blend, and the more you stack the further
the result drifts from photographic. Never upload the character sheet,
the world sheet, the style image and the shot still all together.
 • IMAGE prompts: upload the character portrait only. One per
   character actually in the shot. Nothing else.
 • VIDEO prompts: upload the shot still only — plus the next shot's
   still if, and only if, you are pinning a last frame.
 • The world sheet and style image are for YOU to look at while
   writing. They are never uploaded.
STEP 07 · 2C. CHARACTER SHEET PROMPTS (planning only, never uploaded)
747 words
dekho
━━━ 2C. CHARACTER SHEET PROMPTS (planning only, never uploaded) ━━━
One per character per age stage. Write each as a ready-to-paste
prompt. Use this exact structure and fill in the character:

   Create a CHARACTER SHEET in one single image, 2:3 portrait, on a
   plain light grey studio backdrop with even soft lighting.
   CHARACTER: [name, age, role]
   [full description — face, build, costume, every ornament, colours,
    skin tone, distinguishing marks]
   TOP ROW — four views, identical face in all four:
     1. FACE, head and shoulders, looking at lens
     2. FRONT, full body, standing
     3. BACK, full body from directly behind — hair, braid, the back
        of the costume and how the garment falls
     4. SIDE, full profile
   BOTTOM ROW — five tight macro tiles:
     1. TILAK or forehead marking — exact shape and colour
     2. HAIR — ornament, braid pattern, parting
        (for a man: TURBAN or CROWN detail)
     3. JEWELLERY — ear, neck and arm, clearly readable
     4. HAND — bangles, mehndi, rings, nails
     5. FEET — anklets, toe rings, sole
        (for a man: his SIGNATURE OBJECT)
   Label every view and tile with small text underneath.
   Below the tiles, a bar of five colour swatches from this
   character: skin, main garment, accent, metal, accessory.
   The face must be IDENTICAL in every view and tile.
   Photographic realism, word for word: "healthy skin with fine
   natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and cheekbones,
   individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric with a soft
   sheen where it catches the light, bright polished metal with deep
   sharp engraving, clean modern digital capture, very deep blacks,
   strong contrast, a restrained palette with one strong colour"
   [STYLE LINE FROM MY PICK — paste it as WORDS. Never upload the
    style image itself; an uploaded style reference drags its own
    texture into the result.]

Under each sheet prompt, print that character's DNA LINE in its own
separate block, clearly marked, like this:

   ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════╗

   ║  the heading names her so I can follow along.    ║
   ║  the LINE below never says her name — copy the   ║
   ║  line only, whole, into every prompt she is in   ║
   ╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
   a young Indian woman of about eighteen, slender, a soft
   oval face with a rounded chin and long dark eyes, fair
   golden-brown skin with fine natural texture, very long jet black
   hair in one thick braid bound with gold cord and threaded with
   white jasmine buds, a deep rose pink silk lehenga with dense gold
   zari embroidery along the hem, a fitted matching choli, a pale
   gold silk dupatta over her left shoulder, a gold maang tikka with
   a pearl drop, a small red bindi, a gold nath with a fine pearl
   chain to her ear, large gold jhumkas, a pearl and emerald choker,
   gold armlets, stacked gold and red glass bangles on both wrists, a
   fine gold waist chain, heavy silver anklets with small bells
   ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

That is the format and roughly the length. One unbroken line. It
must name, in this order: age and build · face shape and skin tone ·
hair, how it is tied, what is in it · every garment with colour and
fabric and how it sits · every ornament item by item · anything
always in their hand.

⚠️ Notice what the line does NOT contain: her name, and any word
like goddess, divine or devotional. The heading above the box is
where the name lives. The line itself describes a human being only —
that is what keeps the output a photograph instead of poster art.

⚠️ WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN ANYTHING ELSE
This exact line gets pasted, word for word, into EVERY image prompt
that character appears in, for every shot, right to the end of the
film. Never shorten it. Never reword it. Never replace it with "the
same woman as before" or "the same person from the uploaded image".

Repeating the whole line every time is the only thing that keeps the
face the same in shot 15 as in shot 1. Changing even a few words is
exactly what makes the face drift halfway through a film.

(The VIDEO prompt for the same shot works the other way round — see
PROMPT 3, BLOCK 0. There the uploaded still already carries the face,
so the video prompt carries a SHORT lock, not the full line. Full
line in image prompts. Short lock in video prompts. Never mix them up.)

Then give me the filename: CHAR-[name]-[age].png

STEP 09 · 2E. THE COLOUR PALETTE
54 words
dekho
━━━ 2E. THE COLOUR PALETTE ━━━
From the sheets, write out the film's palette — six colours with
what each comes from. Then state: "This palette is now law. No
colour outside it may enter any frame."
Also mark which SINGLE colour is allowed to be loud, and where in
the film it appears.
STEP 10 · 2F. THE OPENING FRAME
53 words
dekho
━━━ 2F. THE OPENING FRAME ━━━
One finished, full-quality frame of the film's opening image — not a
reference, the actual frame as it will appear. Write it as a ready
prompt. Filename: HERO-opening.png
Tell me: "This is the quality bar. If any later frame is weaker than
this one, regenerate that frame."
STEP 11 · FINISH WITH THIS
91 words
dekho
━━━ FINISH WITH THIS ━━━
Print a numbered checklist of every image I must now generate, in
order, with its filename, in two groups:
   GROUP 1 — the style test (2 images, then WAIT for my pick)
   GROUP 2 — everything else (portraits, sheets, world, hero frame)
Then say:

"✅ Generate these images now, one at a time, and save them with
these exact filenames. When they are done, paste PROMPT 3 together
with the STORY LOCK."

And remember the last line of Rule 0 — the STEP 2 POORA line.
PROMPT 3 · 15992 words

SHOT CARDS

har shot ka card — smoke/fog number, 4–6 camera, action-attach

poora prompt padho 
Now turn the STORY LOCK into shot cards I can follow without
thinking.

Do not ask me anything at all. If something is missing, state an
assumption and continue. Never ask me to upload, provide or identify
a reference image or an example — I have nothing to give you, and
the reference images already exist with the filenames from PROMPT 2.
You are WRITING the prompts I will paste; you are not generating
anything yourself.

━━━ FIRST, PROVE YOU STILL HAVE THE STORY ━━━
In eight lines: what this film is about · what physically happens
first to last · every character and what each wants · the turning
point · how it ends and what the audience feels · what they feel at
start, middle and end · the one image they must remember · the five
things that must not change.
If any of that is unclear in the STORY LOCK, say which line is
unclear and stop. Do not guess. Inventing is what makes the video
end up being a different film than the one I told you.

⚠️ THE STORY LOCK IS THE TRUTH. Translate it into shots. Never
improve it, reinterpret it, or add events. If a shot you want to
write is not in the event chain, you may not write it.

⭐ AND THE NEW RULE THAT GOVERNS THIS WHOLE PROMPT — THE TRACE LAW.
Every card must be traceable back to MY words. So every card opens
with a STORY LINE: a short quote from MY STORY or the STORY LOCK,
inside quotation marks — the exact line that produced this shot.

   · If a card has no line, it does not exist. Either delete it, or
     mark it clearly as PURE TRANSITION and keep it 3 seconds or less.
   · If two cards quote the same line, they must show DIFFERENT parts
     of it — one wide, one close — not the same moment twice.
   · Never upgrade my words. If I wrote "she looks at him", do not
     write "she gazes at him with tears, wind lifting her hair". My
     sentence is the ceiling, not the floor.
   · At the end of all the cards, print this table:

     | Shot | Story line it came from | Invented? |
       01   | "she turns from the door" | no
       02   | —                          | PURE TRANSITION
       03   | "the cloth slips"          | no

     Anything marked invented gets one line of justification, or it
     gets cut.

━━━ HOW MANY SHOTS ━━━
Veo and most tools give 8 second clips. 20 clips × 8s = 160s of
material for a 45s film — that is correct, you trim later.
Work out the shot count from the runtime and tell me the maths.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PLAN ALL OF THIS BEFORE WRITING ANY CARD
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

① THE SHOT LIST — one row per shot, mapped to the event chain.
   Every chain event gets a shot; every shot maps to an event.
   Columns: # · ⭐ STORY LINE (my words, quoted) · chain event ·
   location · who is on screen · shot size · camera move ·
   ⭐ MOVEMENT THROUGH THE FRAME · ⭐ ENTERS FROM which edge ·
   ⭐ EXITS AT which edge · body state at start · body state at end ·
   what the face is doing · duration · CHAIN or CUT ·
   ⭐ ATMOSPHERE (the smoke/fog/dust/rain number for this shot).
   State the GENRE MODE from ⑩ above the table — it governs clip
   length, which moves are allowed and how the light behaves.
   Add a ⭐ MOVE column holding the exact named move from ⑧ for
   every shot. Read that column down: most rows must be Static,
   Dolly, Truck, Pan or Follow, and exactly ONE row may hold a bold
   move. If two bold moves appear, the film is noise — downgrade one.
   Plus two more columns: ⭐ WHAT IS BEHIND HER, and ⭐ FROM → TO
   (where her body starts and ends, with a rough distance for any
   travelling shot). A travelling shot whose FROM and TO are the same
   place is a walk on the spot — fix it at the list stage, before any
   prompt is written.
   Add a ⭐ SHOT TYPE column using ⑱: hand · eyes · detail ·
   silhouette · feet · back of head · wide · full body. Read it
   down: for every one wide or full-body row there should be about
   three detail rows. A list full of full-body rows will come out
   looking like AI however well it is written.
   Add a ⭐ HANDOFF column from ⑰ — at least two rows in the film
   must hand over through a moving object, and one of them at the
   hardest jump in place or time.
   Add a ⭐ BIG EFFECT column. Exactly ONE row in the whole table
   may be filled; every other row says none. Put it on the story's
   peak. A film with no spectacle shot is a film nobody forwards.
   Add a ⭐ FACE FROM → TO column: what the expression is at the
   start of the shot and what it has become by the end. If a row
   has the same word twice, that shot has no acting in it.
   State the film's one travel direction above the table.
   The MOVEMENT column can never be blank and the word "static" may
   appear at most ONCE in the whole table.
   Read the BEHIND HER column straight down. The same backdrop may
   not repeat more than twice in a row — that is the film standing
   still even while the character moves.

② WHO IS IN EACH SHOT — an exact name list per shot. Anyone not
   named may not appear. Nobody appears before their ARRIVES event.

③ THE DISTANCE PLAN — for every shot, how much of the frame height
   the figure fills. Use the ladder:
     EXTREME WIDE  under 1/10 — tiny, face unreadable, vast world
     WIDE          about 1/3 — full body with space around
     FULL          about 4/5 — head to feet
     MEDIUM        waist up — hands visible
     CLOSE-UP      head and shoulders — the eyes are the subject
     EXTREME CLOSE eyes only — pores visible
   Distance is meaning, not variety. Loneliness = wide, figure tiny.
   Feeling = close. Choose from the story, then say why.

④ THE CAMERA PLAN — pick every angle from the emotion:
     any reaction → CLOSE-UP, static
     strongest feeling → EXTREME CLOSE-UP, camera stops
     hiding something → CLOSE, slightly off-axis
     power or divinity → LOW ANGLE looking up
     shame or defeat → HIGH ANGLE looking down
     a relationship → TWO-SHOT or over-the-shoulder
     someone unobserved → shot from behind cover
     loneliness → WIDE, camera pulling away
   For every shot also state FACE ANGLE (full front / three-quarter
   / profile / from behind) and EYE HEIGHT (at eye level / below /
   above). If the meaning is in the face, it must be front or
   three-quarter — a profile hides half the performance, a wide
   hides all of it. Never leave height unstated; it renders flat.
   State the emotional reason for every angle. "Variety" is not one.
   Rules: no two neighbouring shots share a camera move or a size ·
   keep the camera on one side of the 180-degree line · screen
   direction holds · light stays on the same side · no more than 3
   shots in a row in one place.
   ⭐ AND USE 4 TO 6 CAMERA SETUPS FOR THE WHOLE FILM — see ㉕.

⑤ THE PERFORMANCE PLAN — the most important thing in this film.
   A small story with great faces works. A great story with blank
   faces does not.
   For every shot with a face, plan all six:
     INTENTION — what they are TRYING to do right now. A want, not
        a feeling. "to make him look away first."
     PUBLIC FACE — what they are deliberately showing.
     PRIVATE TRUTH — what is leaking underneath. The gap between
        these two IS the performance.
     MICRO-BEATS — the clip broken into 3 or 4 moments, each with a
        named muscle action. Also state whether the EYES move before
        the HEAD or the head before the eyes — that one detail is
        what reads as real acting.
     THE TELL — one involuntary detail: a swallow, fingers
        tightening, a late blink, a jaw setting, a curling foot.
     PLAYING TO — to him / away from him / to the group / to nobody,
        believing unobserved (the most powerful).

   INTENSITY — why anger comes out looking normal:
   Tools default every face to pleasant. Write "angry" and you get
   mildly displeased. Give every performance shot a number 1-10 and
   describe the muscles AT THAT LEVEL:
     ANGER 3  a flicker of irritation, eyes narrowing
     ANGER 6  jaw set, nostrils flaring, hard unblinking stare
     ANGER 9  face flushed and taut, teeth clenched behind closed
              lips, temple veins, body rigid, breathing hard through
              the nose, eyes locked and wet, not blinking at all
   Always write one level ABOVE what you want — the render always
   comes back one level below. Never let the emotion word stand
   alone; follow it with at least four muscles. Strong emotion needs
   a close-up. And say what it does to the BODY too — shoulders,
   hands, breathing, stillness or shaking. A face alone reads flat.

   BANNED, they produce dead faces: "smiles" · "looks happy" ·
   "is sad" · "reacts" · "shows emotion" · "divine expression".

   THE REACTION RULE: the event is not the drama, the FACE receiving
   it is. Every significant event needs two shots — the event, then
   the face. Cut to the reaction a beat late.

   ⭐ THE CHANGE RULE — the difference between an actor and a
   mannequin. A still frame can carry one expression. A CLIP cannot.
   If the face is the same at the first frame and the last frame,
   the audience sees a photograph being panned across, and they
   read it instantly as machine-made. So for every shot with a face,
   write the expression as a JOURNEY: where it starts, the exact
