   second it turns, and where it ends. Then write the THOUGHT
   behind it in the character's own words — that is what fills the
   eyes. Then one involuntary LEAK. Then give the hands something
   to do. Those four are carried into the video prompt as BLOCK 2B.

   ⭐ AND SUPPRESS IT. Tools render emotion as performance: the
   mouth opens, the brows go up, the whole face announces itself.
   Real screen acting is the opposite — a person holding something
   down while one part of them gives it away. Write the holding
   down, and write the one place it escapes. A person trying not to
   cry reads real. A person crying reads like an AI.

⑥ TRAVEL THROUGH THE FRAME ⭐ THE BIGGEST FIX OF ALL
   A shot where a person stands in one place and only their hand, their
   earring or their hair moves is a DEAD SHOT. It reads as a photograph
   with a filter running on it. It will never cut together with the shot
   before or after it, however good the prompt is.

     ❌ DEAD: "she sits by the mirror and her earring sways"
     ❌ DEAD: "she stands under the tree and her dupatta lifts"
     ✅ ALIVE: "she rises and leaves the top of frame"
     ✅ ALIVE: "she enters frame left, crosses, exits frame right"
     ✅ ALIVE: "she walks toward the lens and passes it"

   So for EVERY shot, write one line called MOVEMENT THROUGH THE FRAME,
   chosen from these — the frame itself must change during the clip:
     she enters frame left and exits frame right
     she walks toward the camera and passes the lens
     she walks away from the camera and gets smaller
     she rises up out of the top of frame
     the camera tracks alongside her and the background streaks past
     the camera follows behind her as she pulls away
     the camera pushes in / pulls back / orbits, so the background moves
     her feet or hands travel through the frame and out of it

   HARD LIMIT: at most ONE shot in the entire film may stay still, and
   it must be the single biggest emotional close-up, where the feeling
   moves instead of the body. Every other shot travels. If your shot
   list has two static shots, you have a slideshow — fix it now.

   Also: a character must EXIT a place on screen and ENTER the next one
   on screen. The last second of a shot in place A should already be
   her leaving; the first second in place B should already be her
   arriving. Never let a shot simply begin with someone standing there.

   ⭐ AND TRAVELLING MEANS COVERING GROUND, NOT JUST MOVING
   There is a second kind of dead shot, and it is harder to spot: the
   character walks convincingly for the whole clip and ends up exactly
   where she started. Legs moving, hips moving, braid swinging — and
   the same wall behind her at the last frame as the first. A walk on
   a treadmill.

   It happens because the shot was planned as "she walks" instead of
   "she gets from here to there". A walk is a body doing something. A
   travelling shot is a body CHANGING PLACE. Only the second one moves
   a film forward.

   So plan every travelling shot as a JOURNEY WITH TWO ENDS:
       FROM: level with the blue door
       TO:   under the neem tree, about eight metres on
       PASSES ON THE WAY: the stacked clay pots, the tethered cow
   Then the beats can be written against real landmarks, and the
   audience can see how far she has come.

   Three rules that make ground-covering visible on screen:
     1. SOMETHING MUST LEAVE THE FRAME BEHIND HER. If nothing exits
        the back of the shot, she has not gone anywhere. Name the
        thing and the second it disappears.
     2. SOMETHING MUST GROW AHEAD OF HER. The tree, the gate, the
        ghat — small at 0s, large at the end. That growth IS the
        distance, as far as the viewer is concerned.
     3. HER SIZE OR POSITION IN FRAME MUST CHANGE, unless the camera
        is deliberately matching her pace. Walking away, she gets
        smaller. Walking past a locked camera, she crosses it and
        exits. Only a tracking camera holds her the same size — and
        then the BACKGROUND has to carry the whole job.

   Write the FROM, the TO and the metres on every travelling shot
   before you write a word of its prompt.

⑦ SCREEN DIRECTION ⭐ WHY TWO CLIPS MERGE OR CLASH
   This is the real editing rule behind "the frames don't feel joined".

   Pick ONE travel direction for the whole film — normally LEFT to RIGHT
   — and hold it. If she exits a shot at frame right, she enters the
   next shot from frame left, still travelling right. The eye then
   already knows where she is going before the new shot is understood,
   and the cut disappears.

   Break it and the audience feels she turned around for no reason —
   that is exactly the "alag-alag video" feeling.

   Rules:
   - State the direction once, at the top of the shot list, and put the
     entry edge and exit edge on every single shot row.
   - Never flip the direction unless you SHOW her turning around inside
     a shot, or you place a neutral shot (walking straight at or
     straight away from the lens) between the two sides.
   - The same applies to eyelines. If she looks off frame right in one
     shot, the thing she is looking at appears from frame left in the
     next.
   - Never cut between two camera positions less than 30 degrees apart.
     That is a jump cut and it reads as a mistake, not a cut.

⑧ THE CAMERA MOVE — USE THE REAL NAME ⭐ THE HIGGSFIELD METHOD
   There is a reason footage from camera-control platforms looks
   directed rather than drifting, and it is not a secret model. It
   is that every shot is assigned ONE NAMED, REAL CAMERA MOVE from a
   fixed list — the same words a camera operator says on a set.

   Those names are standard cinematography terms, so every video
   model already knows them from its training. Describing a move in
   your own words gets you drift. Naming it gets you the move.

     ❌ "the camera moves quickly towards her face"
     ✅ "crash zoom in on her face"

     ❌ "the camera goes around him in a circle"
     ✅ "360 orbit around him, moving left to right"

     ❌ "the camera follows her while the background stretches"
     ✅ "dolly zoom in — camera pushes forward while the lens zooms
        out, so she stays the same size and the background stretches
        behind her"

   Pick ONE move per shot from this list, write its exact name, then
   add the direction and the speed. Nothing else.

   ── EVERYDAY MOVES — these carry most of a film ──
     Static              camera locked off, subject moves in frame
     Dolly In / Out      camera travels toward or away, on a track
     Super Dolly In/Out  the same, longer and more dramatic
     Truck Left / Right  camera slides sideways, staying parallel
     Pan Left / Right    camera pivots in place, horizontally
     Tilt Up / Down      camera pivots in place, vertically
     Handheld            small natural shake, human and unstaged
     Follow / Head Tracking  camera holds behind or on the subject
                         at their pace as they travel

   ── MOVES WITH SHAPE — one or two in a film ──
     Crane Up / Down     camera rises or falls vertically, high arc
     Jib Up / Down       a shorter, gentler crane
     Arc Left / Right    camera sweeps a partial curve around
     360 Orbit           a full circle around the subject
     Lazy Susan          the subject turns, the camera holds
     Boom Down           camera descends, often following stairs
     Push to Glass       camera moves in until it meets a surface
     Through Object      camera passes through a gap, door or window

   ── BOLD MOVES — ⚠️ AT MOST ONE IN THE WHOLE FILM ──
     Crash Zoom In/Out   violent fast zoom, for one reaction or
                         reveal only
     Dolly Zoom (Vertigo) push in + zoom out together; subject fixed,
                         background warps. Dread, realisation.
     Bullet Time         time nearly frozen while the camera arcs
     Whip Pan            a blur-fast pan, usually hiding a cut
     Snorricam           camera locked to the body, world swings
     FPV Drone           fast flying path, dives and climbs
     YoYo Zoom           in then straight back out
     3D Rotation         the frame itself rotates

   ⚠️ THE DISCIPLINE THAT MATTERS MOST
   A bold move is a punch. Throw one and it lands. Throw six and the
   film is noise and nobody feels anything. Most shots in a good film
   are Static, Dolly, Truck, Pan or Follow. Reserve exactly ONE bold
   move for the single biggest beat, and write in the plan which shot
   gets it and why.

   NEVER stack two moves. "Orbit while zooming and craning up" gives
   you mush — the model picks one or blends them into drift. One
   named move, one direction, one speed.

   Every shot's move line is written like this:
       MOVE: Dolly In · slow · 1 metre of travel
       MOVE: Truck Right · matching her walking pace
       MOVE: Crash Zoom In · fast · on her eyes      ← the one bold
       MOVE: Static · locked off, she crosses the frame

   And the speed word is never optional. "Slow", "steady",
   "matching her pace", "fast" — a move with no speed renders at the
   model's default, which is usually too fast and reads as cheap.

⑨ SPEED RAMPS — where the film breathes
   Real action films do not run at one speed. They ramp: normal
   speed into slow motion at the peak, then back. Choose the ONE
   moment in this film that slows down, and state it:
       "Shot 11 ramps: normal speed for 0-2s, then slows to 40% for
        the turn, then returns to normal."
   One ramp per film. Two is a music video; three is a mess.

⑩ GENRE MODE — set it once, obey it everywhere
   A camera-control platform asks you to pick a genre before you
   generate anything, because genre decides pacing, light and how
   much the camera is allowed to move. Pick ONE for this film and
   write it at the top of the shot list. Then every shot obeys it.

     DRAMA    long takes, slow dolly, soft directional light,
              the camera waits on faces
     ACTION   shorter clips, handheld and follow moves, hard light,
              one crash zoom allowed
     EPIC     wide lenses, cranes and slow reveals, big landscape,
              low horizon
     NOIR     static and slow, hard single-source light, deep black
              shadow, the frame half dark
     INTIMATE close lenses, almost no camera movement, window light,
              faces fill the frame
     FOLK / DEVOTIONAL  steady unhurried moves, natural light only,
              real materials, the camera respectful and never showy

   Write: "GENRE: Drama. Clips run 4-6s, moves are slow dolly and
   static, light is one soft source, the camera holds on faces."
   And remember: the EMOTION PHASES from the story (㉘) override the
   genre inside the phase they belong to.

⑪ EFFECTS — THE SMALL ONE IN EVERY SHOT, AND THE ONE BIG ONE
   Effect-heavy platform footage looks rich for two separate
   reasons, and most prompts only ever get the first one.

   ① THE SMALL EFFECT — one in every single shot.
   Physical things in the air catching the light. Every shot names
   ONE element in motion:

     dust lifting from under a heel · smoke drifting across a beam ·
     water droplets thrown off a turn · cloth snapping in wind ·
     dry leaves skidding over stone · sand scuffing outward ·
     river spray · embers from a fire · rain on a shoulder ·
     a flock lifting off in the background · steam off hot metal

   Each is a real object with weight that light falls on. They add
   depth and motion and they cost nothing in realism.
   ⭐ And every one of these carries a NUMBER from ⑲. "Smoke drifts"
   is not enough; "smoke level 2, two wisps at chest height drifting
   left to right at walking pace" is.

   ② ⭐ THE BIG EFFECT — EXACTLY ONE SHOT IN THE FILM.
   This is the shot people remember and the shot they send to
   someone else. A film with no spectacle shot is a film nobody
   forwards, however beautiful the rest of it is. Plan ONE, on the
   story's peak, on the same beat as the one bold camera move.

   Choose one, and take it from the story — never decoration:

     FIRE        a wall of flame going up, sparks carried sideways,
                 heat shimmer bending the air behind it
     LIGHTNING   a strike that throws hard white light across her
                 face for a fraction of a second, then darkness
     WATER       a river surging, a wave breaking over stone, water
                 thrown upward in a sheet, rain starting all at once
     WIND        a storm front hitting, cloth and hair pulled flat
                 in one direction, leaves and grit driven past
     EARTH       stone cracking, ground splitting, a shockwave of
                 dust rolling outward along the floor
     SCALE       the camera pulls back and she is tiny against
                 something enormous that was there all along
     TIME        everything in the air freezes mid-fall and the
                 camera moves through it, then it drops
     SKY         clouds moving at impossible speed above a still
                 figure, the light on the ground racing with them
     RISE        ash, petals or embers lifting UPWARD off the ground
                 all at once, against gravity

   ⭐ HOW TO WRITE IT SO IT DOES NOT COME OUT AS A CARTOON. Never
   describe the effect as an effect. Describe it as something that
   happened in front of a real camera, and spend most of the words
   on its CONSEQUENCES on the person:

     ❌ "a magical divine energy blast with glowing particles"
     ✅ "the water goes up in a solid sheet two metres high and
        falls back; the spray hits the left side of her face and
        her eye on that side closes against it; her wet hair is
        pulled flat to her cheek; the light off the water throws a
        moving bright patch across her jaw; the ground around her
        darkens as it lands"

   Four things make an effect photographic, and all four must be
   in the prompt:
     • WHAT IT DOES TO THE LIGHT on her face, and from which side
     • WHAT IT MOVES on her — hair, cloth, eyelids, her balance
     • WHAT IT LEAVES BEHIND — wet stone, scattered dust, a mark
     • HER FACE RECEIVING IT — she flinches, squints, braces. An
       effect nobody reacts to reads as pasted on.

   And give it room: the effect shot is usually the longest clip in
   the film and almost always needs a reaction shot straight after.

   ⚠️ What is still NOT allowed, ever: glow, aura, halo, god rays,
   light shafts, golden mist, lens flare, bloom, sparkle, floating
   luminous particles, divine energy. Those are the calendar-art
   tells. The difference is simple — an EVENT is allowed, a GLOW is
   not. Fire is an event. Lightning is an event. A shining aura is
   not an event, it is a filter. And if a face is behind the effect,
   read ⑳ before you write it.

⑫ THE MOTION PLAN — this is what stops it looking like a slideshow.
   Never write a shot as a POSE. A pose does not move; two poses in
   a row is a swap, not a film.
     ❌ "She kneels at the water, praying."
     ✅ "Already kneeling, she lowers the last flower onto the water,
        her hand staying out over the surface as the ripples spread."
   Three rules for every shot:
     1. START MID-MOTION. The first frame is already moving.
        "already lowering…", "still rising…". Never "she stands".
     2. END MID-MOTION. Never let a clip settle and stop — that full
        stop is exactly what the eye reads as a cut.
     3. THE HANDOVER. The movement at the end of shot N must be the
        SAME movement continuing at the start of shot N+1, seen from
        a new angle. The eye completes it across the cut and the
        join disappears.
          N   ends : "weight shifting onto her right foot, knee
                     beginning to straighten"
          N+1 starts: "from lower, the same knee still straightening"
   Build a handover table: for each shot, the motion at its first
   frame, the motion at its last frame, and that same motion restated
   as the next shot's first frame. The last-frame motion you write
   here becomes BLOCK 3 (the end state) on that shot's card, and the
   next shot's BLOCK 2 must open on that same movement. They must
   match word for word — that is what makes the two clips read as
   one piece. If the motion does not literally
   reappear, that join will look like a swap — rewrite the pair.

   If a join still feels wrong, NEVER add a filler clip. Fix the two
   shots: extend the action so they overlap · re-angle further ·
   move the cut into the busiest part of the movement · let the
   camera keep travelling in the same direction across the cut ·
   carry the ambient sound across.

⑬ THE BEAT TIMELINE — the cure for "it starts right then goes wrong".
   A video tool fills every second it is given. If your prompt runs
   out of instructions at second 3 of a 6-second clip, the tool
   invents seconds 4, 5 and 6 by itself — a second action, a head
   turn nobody asked for, a hand that wanders, a face that changes.
   The clip did not drift. It ran out of script.

   So for every shot, book every single second before writing the
   card. One line per second, and the number of lines must equal
   the clip length exactly:
       4-second clip = 4 lines
       6-second clip = 6 lines
       8-second clip = 8 lines
   Each line is one small physical fact — a hand, a foot, an eye,
   a breath, a shift of weight. Not a feeling. Not a repeat.

   ⭐ AND IF THE SHOT IS A TRAVELLING SHOT, EVERY LINE MUST ALSO SAY
   HOW FAR SHE HAS GOT. This is the difference between a person
   walking and a person walking on the spot.

   A model given only body facts — "her foot lifts", "her braid
   swings", "her arm swings" — will animate a perfect walk that
   covers no ground at all. The legs move, the hips move, and she
   stays exactly where she started, like a treadmill. Everything you
   wrote happened. You just never said she got anywhere.

   So on a travelling shot, every beat names a LANDMARK she reaches,
   passes or leaves behind:

   ❌ WALKING ON THE SPOT — body facts only:
       0-1s: her rear foot pushes off the ground
       1-2s: her braid swings across her shoulder
       2-3s: her lehenga hem sways with her stride
       3-4s: her arm swings forward again
     Every line is true and the clip will go nowhere.

   ✅ ACTUALLY TRAVELLING — distance in every line:
       0-1s: she starts level with the blue door and moves off,
             the door sliding behind her right shoulder
       1-2s: she passes the stacked clay pots, which leave the
             frame behind her
       2-3s: the tethered cow that was far ahead is now beside her
             and falls back out of frame
       3-4s: the neem tree that was a small shape ahead now fills
             the left of frame as she reaches it
     Same walk. Now she has crossed a real piece of ground.

   THE TEST: read your beats and ask "where is she at 0 seconds, and
   where is she at the end?" If the honest answer is "the same place",
   rewrite every line with landmarks.

   HOW TO WRITE IT, three parts per travelling beat:
     1. WHAT SHE PASSES — something in the world going by
     2. WHERE IT GOES — behind her, out of frame left, past her
        shoulder. Things she passes must LEAVE the frame.
     3. WHAT GROWS AHEAD — the thing she is walking toward getting
        bigger, nearer, more detailed

   Also state it once in the prompt, in plain words:
     "She covers real ground — roughly [n] metres across this clip.
      She does not walk on the spot. The background slides past her
      and does not repeat."

   And add to the negatives of every walking clip (in the separate
   negative box if the tool has one, as positives if it does not):
     "no walking on the spot, no treadmill walk, no foot sliding, no
      repeating background, no looping scenery, the subject must
      change position relative to the background"

   Worked example, a 4-second clip that is NOT travelling — here
   body facts alone are correct, because she stays put on purpose:
       0-1s: her fingers close around the door handle
       1-2s: her wrist turns and the latch gives
       2-3s: the door starts to swing inward, her weight follows it
       3-4s: her front foot crosses the threshold, still moving
   Now the tool has nothing to invent.

   ⚠️ If you cannot fill every second with something real, the clip
   is too long for that action. Shorten it. A 3-second clip that is
   fully booked beats an 8-second clip that is half empty. Long
   clips with thin instructions are where drift lives.

⑭ THE CONSTANTS AND THE VARIABLES — per shot, two short lists.
   The tool changes whatever you left unstated, so state it.

   CONSTANTS — must be identical at the first frame and the last:
     the face and its bone structure · hairstyle and how it is tied ·
     every garment and its exact state (pallu on the head or off the
     shoulder — say which) · every piece of jewellery · what each
     hand is holding · the light direction · the time of day
   VARIABLES — the only things allowed to change in this clip:
     usually just the one action, the expression moving one step,
     and the single camera move.

   Write the constants into the card so the lock line can name them.
   ⚠️ If something must genuinely change during the clip — a dupatta
   slipping, a lamp being set down — it goes in the VARIABLES list
   AND in the beat timeline at the exact second it happens. Anything
   that changes without being written in one of those two places is
   a defect.
   ⭐ And every garment state is also carried shot to shot — see ㉗.

⑮ THE THREAD — five things that make it one film, not clips:
   ONE TRAVELLING OBJECT in 5+ shots, changing as the story changes ·
   ONE REPEATING FRAME, ideally the first and last shot ·
   LIGHT MOVING ONE WAY ONLY, never backwards ·
   ONE AMBIENT SOUND in every shot so audio never resets ·
   ONE COLOUR ACCENT only on things that matter.
   State all five, then honour them in every shot.

⑯ THE JOIN PLAN — do this for EVERY pair of neighbouring shots.
   This is the single biggest reason films feel like stitched
   clips instead of one piece. Make a table, one row per join:

     JOIN 01→02 · 02→03 · 03→04 ... one row for every pair.
     Columns:
       • LAST THING MOVING in shot A (the exact body part or object
         and which direction it is travelling)
       • FIRST THING MOVING in shot B (must be the SAME movement,
         continuing, seen from a new angle — or the direct physical
         result of it)
       • WHAT CARRIES ACROSS — tick at least TWO of:
           the same movement continuing
           the same object, still in the same hand
           the same sound running underneath
           the same light direction
           an eyeline in shot A answered by what we see in shot B
           a shape or colour in the same screen position
       • JOIN TYPE: CHAIN (continuous time) or CUT (new time/place)
       • If CUT: which THRESHOLD shot sits between them

   ⚠️ THE ARRIVAL LAW — no scene may simply begin.
   Every new place must be ENTERED on screen by a person. If shot 07
   is the first shot in a new place, then shot 06 or 07 must contain
   the entering — a door pushed, a gate opened, feet stepping in, a
   curtain moved aside, a body walking into frame from the edge.
   Never open a new place on a static waiting frame. The audience
   must feel they walked in with the character.

   ⚠️ THE EXIT LAW — no scene may simply stop.
   Before leaving a place, we see the character begin to leave:
   standing up, turning away, walking out of frame, reaching for
   the way out. The film must never blink from place to place.

   Worked example of a correct join chain:
     shot 4: she fastens her earring, then her eyes flick to the door
     shot 5: her hand closes on the door handle, pulls  ← her look in
             4 is answered by 5; same room, same light
     shot 6: from outside, the door opens and she steps through  ←
             the same door movement, new angle: THRESHOLD
     shot 7: her feet come down onto the road, camera rising with her
             ← the same step continuing
     shot 8: the road opens ahead of her, she stops, breathes  ←
             arrival completed, now the new scene may start
   Five shots to walk out of a house. That is correct. That is what
   makes it feel like one film.

   Fill the join table completely before writing any card. Any row
   where you cannot tick two "carries across" boxes is a broken
   join — fix it by re-angling, by overlapping the action, or by
   adding the missing threshold shot, then renumber.
   ⭐ And between two SCENES, plan the merge shot from ㉓.

⑰ THE OBJECT HANDOFF — the best join there is, and it is free.
   The joins above are good. This one is the one people replay and
   send to somebody. It works like this:

     A glass slips out of a hand. We cut while it is still falling.
     It is STILL FALLING in the next shot — but that shot is a
     different place, a different year, a different scene. The
     glass carried us there.

   One object in motion is the only thing that matches across the
   cut. Everything else changes. The eye follows the object, does
   not notice the change of world, and the two places become one
   piece of film. Nothing else joins two places this strongly.

   PLAN AT LEAST TWO OF THESE PER FILM, and one of them at the
   hardest join — wherever the story jumps place or time.

   WHAT CAN CARRY A HANDOFF:
     something FALLING    a glass, a cloth, a coin, a sword, ash,
                          water poured, a body going down
     something THROWN     a stone, a lamp, a spear, a bundle
     something PASSING    a horse, a cart, a crowd, a bird, a hand
                          sweeping through the near foreground
     something SWALLOWING dust rolling in, smoke, water filling the
                          frame, a cloak crossing the lens, a door
                          swinging shut into the camera
     something TURNING    a wheel, a grinding stone, a spinning
                          coin, a head turning — cut on the turn

   HOW TO WRITE IT, AND IT MUST BE WRITTEN IN BOTH SHOTS:
     • Shot A's END STATE: name the object, which direction it is
       travelling, how fast, and where it sits in the frame at the
       final frame. "The glass has left his fingers and is falling
       through the lower right of the frame, turning slowly."
     • Shot B's FIRST frame: the SAME object, SAME direction, SAME
       speed, SAME part of the frame — but the world behind it is
       the new place. "A falling glass turns slowly through the
       lower right of the frame; behind it, a stone courtyard at
       night instead of the hall."
     • Four things must match or the trick collapses: the object,
       the direction of travel, the speed, and its position in the
       frame. Nothing else needs to match. Nothing else should.

   ⚠️ DO NOT try to make one clip contain both places. That is the
   mush problem again. Generate TWO clips and cut between them in
   the editor, on the frame where the object is in the same spot in
   both. The cut is free; the effect is not.

   ⚠️ And the object must be in the story. A falling glass only
   works if somebody was drinking. An object inserted purely to
   make a clever cut reads as a trick.

⑱ THE SAFE SHOT LADDER — where generators never fail.
   There are shots a generator gets right every single time, and
   shots it gets wrong every single time. Most of a convincing AI
   film is built out of the first kind. This is not a limitation to
   work around; it is the actual craft.

   ALWAYS RIGHT — build the film out of these:
     • HANDS doing one thing — closing, releasing, tying, pouring,
       gripping a hilt, trembling. A hand carries emotion and has
       no face to get wrong.
     • EYES ONLY — an extreme close-up at the level of the eyes.
     • A DETAIL OF AN OBJECT — engraving, a buckle, a bead of water,
       a blade's edge, a thread pulling loose. (But every detail shot
       must do TWO jobs — see ㉖.)
     • A FULL SILHOUETTE against a bright sky or doorway.
     • FEET on ground — stepping, sliding, stopping.
     • SOMETHING FALLING or BLOWING past the lens.
     • THE BACK OF A HEAD with the world in front of it.

   USUALLY WRONG — use rarely and keep them short:
     • a full body walking at medium distance, seen head to toe
     • two people interacting in the same frame
     • any crowd where faces are visible
     • hands doing something complicated — writing, counting
     • a face talking

   ⭐ THE RULE THAT FOLLOWS: for every one wide shot, give the film
   three detail shots. Then the wide shot only has to survive two
   seconds, and the close work carries the emotion. A film built
   out of hands, eyes, objects and silhouettes reads as expensive
   and controlled. A film built out of people walking around in
   full view reads as AI, no matter how good the prompt is.

   And it solves the travel problem too. The FILM must travel —
   through cuts, new places and changing backgrounds. Every SHOT
   must have motion. But motion does not have to mean a full body
   walking: a hand opening, a horse rearing in silhouette, ash
   lifting, a head turning — all of those are motion, and all of
   them are safe.

⑲ ATMOSPHERE NUMBER SYSTEM ⭐ (kitna smoke? kitna fog? — andaza nahi)
   "Some smoke" and "light fog" mean NOTHING to a video model. It
   guesses, and the guess is always too much smoke, too weak a face,
   and a new face invented behind the smoke. So every effect gets a
   NUMBER, and the number means exactly the same thing in every shot
   of this film. The story already chose these numbers in PROMPT 1 —
   your job here is to write the number on every card.

   ⭐ SMOKE LEVEL — the number, then the look, then the direction

    0 — no smoke at all.
    1 — one wisp thinner than a wrist, one tenth of the frame at
        most. The face is completely clean and sharp. Safe for every
        face shot.
    2 — two or three wisps at chest height, drifting at walking pace.
        The face stays clean; only the outline of the shoulders
        softens.  ✅ THIS IS THE HEAVIEST SMOKE ALLOWED IN ANY SHOT
        THAT CONTAINS A READABLE FACE.
    3 — a low band up to the waist, plus two moving columns. Eyes and
        mouth staying sharp; the chin edge may soften.
    4 — smoke up to the neck, columns crossing the face. Eyes and
        brow only. Never in a dialogue shot, never in a face close-up.
    5 — the whole frame filled, the person is a silhouette. No face
        detail of any kind.

   ⭐ FOG LEVEL — the number is the height of the fog ON THE BODY

    0 — none; ground and distance readable.
    1 — ankle height; the feet disappear, the ground still readable.
    2 — knee height; the legs vanish, the body fully clear.
        ✅ Faces must stay at 0, 1 or 2 in fog.
    3 — waist height; faces in the middle distance start to go soft.
    4 — chest height; the person's own face goes soft. Silhouettes
        and wides only.
    5 — full frame; no ground, no distance, no face. A dreamy or lost
        moment only, never a plot action.

   ⭐ DUST / HAZE  1 motes in a beam · 2 dust off the ground under
   footsteps · 3 a beam thick enough to see the light itself · 4 the
   air greys out and the distance is lost.

   ⭐ RAIN  1 a few slow drops on skin, visible only against dark
   cloth · 2 steady drops, visible against dark backgrounds · 3 heavy;
   water standing on the ground, splash at the feet · 4 downpour;
   faces not usable.

   ⭐ FIRE / FLAME — the count, the height in centimetres, and where
   each one is: "two oil lamps, flame six centimetres, one left of
   frame at chest height, one behind her at knee height."

   ⭐ Every effect needs a SOURCE that is in the frame, or was in the
   frame a moment ago: the fire that makes the smoke, the window that
   makes the beam, the cloud that makes the rain, the breath that
   makes the steam. An effect with no source reads as a computer
   mistake.

   ⭐ And the atmosphere is CONTINUOUS: every shot starts at the
   level the previous shot ended on. A change is allowed, but never
   more than one step between two shots, unless something inside the
   shot causes it — and then write the cause: the door opens, the
   fire is thrown, the wind turns.

⑳ FACE REVEAL LAW ⭐ (chehra kab dikhega, kitna dikhega)
   A face hidden behind smoke is where identity dies: the model
   invents new features every time it has to guess. So:

   1. Any shot where the audience must READ the face: smoke 0-2,
      fog 0-2, rain 0-2, dust 0-2. Write the numbers.
   2. The heavy levels (smoke 4-5, fog 4-5, rain 4) are for
      silhouettes, wides and object shots ONLY.
   3. A REVEAL is written as a MOVEMENT, with a direction and a
      duration: "the smoke column drifts to the left and clears her
      eyes and mouth within two seconds, and stays clear for the rest
      of the shot." Never write "the smoke clears" with no direction
      and no time.
   4. The reveal shot gets ONE light source at forty-five degrees
      from the front, with its distance written: "a warm oil lamp,
      chest height, left of camera, one and a half metres away." A
      face revealed against the light stays a shadow, and the model
      fills that shadow with a different face.
   5. AFTER a reveal, the next two shots stay at smoke 0-1, so the
      face is locked in the audience's memory. Never reveal and
      re-cover at once.

㉑ THE TRACE LAW — already stated at the top; this is how it lands
   on the cards.
   • Every card opens with STORY LINE, quoted from my story.
   • Every item of MERI CHEEZEIN from PROMPT 1 must point to a shot
     number. Print that coverage table at the end, like this:

     | MERI CHEEZEIN item | Shot where it appears |
        the flood         | 09, 10, 11
        her silver payal  | 03, 07 (close), 18
        ...               | —

     Any item with no shot is a missing piece — add it now, or write
     one line saying why it cannot be filmed.
   • And the invented table from the top of this prompt, filled.

㉒ NOTHING ADDED ⭐ (apni marzi se cheezein jodna band)
   What is not in my story, my STORY LOCK or my inventory does not go
   into the frame. Not a prop, not an animal, not a visitor, not
   weather, not a crowd, not a decoration in the corner to "fill the
   frame".

   Birth, death, wedding, farewell and other ritual steps: use only
   the steps I actually described. Never add a ritual step that I did
   not write, and never invent a religious object that I did not
   mention.

   If something is genuinely missing and the shot cannot be built
   without it, do NOT invent it silently. Do this instead:

     1. Build the shot using the most plain, most neutral choice
        possible.
     2. Write that choice at the end, in a block called MY ASSUMPTIONS,
        one line each:
          MY ASSUMPTIONS — 1. she is standing, not sitting (story did
          not say). 2. the doorway is wooden (the location DNA did
          not say).
     3. Keep that block at the very end of your answer, after the
        cards and after the tables.

   I will read it and correct it. A written assumption I can fix in
   one minute. A silent invention costs me a whole day of failed
   generations.

㉓ MERGE SHOTS — the short shot that joins two scenes ⭐
   (Tumhara "beech beech ke seenshots" — ye device hai.)
   Between two scenes, and between two big emotions inside one scene,
   insert ONE short shot, about 4 to 6 seconds, that belongs to both
   sides. It must contain two of these at the same time:

     · the same movement continuing (a turn that becomes another turn)
     · the same shape (a circle, a line, a curve repeating)
     · the same colour passing from one object to another
     · the same action done by two different people
     · the same eyeline handed over
     · the same emotion changing owner

   Say in the card: JOINS Scene 2 → Scene 3. Name which two of the
   six devices it uses. If it does not join two things, it is not a
   merge shot — cut it.

㉔ HOLD, DRAPE AND WEAR MECHANICS ⭐
   (Tumhara "kab kaise kisko hold krna hai kaise odhna hai" — yahi hai.)
   This is where AI clips fall apart. Never write "they embrace" or
   "she adjusts her dupatta". Write the physical steps, in order, with
   the limbs named.

   HOLDING: which hand, where it grips, how hard, and what the other
            hand does at the same time.
            "Her left hand closes on his right forearm just below the
            elbow, thumb on the outside; her right hand stays at her
            side."
   DRAPING: lift → travel → land. Three steps, three timestamps.
            "She lifts the cloth with her right hand to shoulder
            height, carries it across her chest, and lays it over the
            left shoulder; it settles and stays."
   WEARING: what moves when the body moves. Sleeve, hem, veil, hair.
            "When she turns, the hem sweeps a quarter turn behind her
            and settles."
   TOUCHING: contact must be visible for at least one full second,
            and the second person must react to it — a breath, a
            blink, a small change of weight.

   Never let a cloth, a hand or a garland pass through a body, and
   never let it change from one shoulder to the other between shots.

㉕ CAMERA COVERAGE — 4 TO 6 SETUPS, VERIFIED ⭐
   Choose 4 to 6 setups for the whole film and never invent a seventh.

     A — WIDE / master: the whole place, tiny people, one landmark
     B — MEDIUM: waist-up, two people if they are together
     C — CLOSE: face, or hands doing the important thing
     D — OVER-SHOULDER: we look past one person at the other
     E — HIGH or TOP-DOWN: the ground, the spread, the arrangement
     F — LOW: from knee height looking up, for power moments only

   Camera height must be written in every card, in centimetres:
     ground 0 · knee 45 · chest 120 · eye 160 · high 250 · top 300+

   Laws:
     · Every scene must be covered from at least FOUR of the setups.
     · Never two shots in a row from the same setup, unless the second
       is a deliberate hold on the same face.
     · Exactly ONE bold camera move in the entire film (see ⑧).
     · After a bold move, the next shot is locked or nearly locked.
     · Print a small coverage table at the end: setup letter × how
       many shots use it. Any setup used fewer than four times, or a
       scene sitting in one setup, is a defect — fix it.

㉖ A DETAIL SHOT MUST DO TWO JOBS ⭐
   Every close-up of an object must (a) be the RESULT of the previous
   shot and (b) SET UP the next one. Write both jobs on the card:

     DOES:  the knot is already tightening because of Shot 05.
     SETS:  the same rope is what Shot 06 pulls.

   A pretty object with no before and no after is filler. Delete it.

㉗ THE COSTUME STATE LINE ⭐ (kapda actor hai, lock nahi)
   Never write "she wears the same clothes as before". Write what the
   clothing is DOING in this shot, and keep the state continuous:

     · at the start of the shot, the state it entered with (wet hem,
       loose sash, hair forward on the left, pallu on the head)
     · what it does during the shot
     · the exact state it leaves in, so the next card can start there

   Print it on every card as: CLOTH STATE — starts / changes / ends.
   If two neighbouring cards disagree about a garment's state, that
   is a defect — fix the pair.

㉘ THE PHASE ENGINE ⭐ (quality, VFX aur look story ke emotion se
   automatically badle — tumhara main point)
   The film has 3 to 5 EMOTION PHASES, already named in the STORY
   LOCK. They are law. Every card states its phase, and the phase
   chooses the look for you — I do not want to decide it shot by shot:

     peace:  wide, soft light, greens and cream, slow drift, no VFX
     want:   closer, warmer, one loud colour, light from one side,
             small natural VFX already building (dust, drifting petals,
             heat)
     test:   tighter, cooler, harder shadow, faster drift, weather VFX
     break:  closest, darkest, hand-held, the ONE big VFX event lands
             HERE
     grace:  opens wide again, bright clean light, the loudest colour
             of the film, calm locked frame

   Rules:
     · The phase overrides the genre mode: inside "break", the light
       is harder and the moves are shorter even if the genre is Drama.
     · The look must stay consistent INSIDE a phase, so the audience
       feels the change when the phase changes.
     · A phase change lands on a shot, not in the middle of one, unless
       the change is caused by something inside that shot — and then
       write the cause.
     · Print the phase on every card, and print a small phase table
       before the cards: phase · shots · look · effects.

㉙ FRAME INVENTORY ⭐ (ginti likho, warna model bana dega)
   Every card carries a count. What is not counted does not exist in
   the frame, and if the model wants something, it must ask me first.

     PEOPLE   — how many, and where each one stands (left / centre /
                right, front / middle / back), with the distance in
                metres.
     LIGHT    — how many sources, what each one is, where it is, and
                its distance in metres.
     OBJECTS  — every object, with its number.
     CLOTH    — how many pieces on each person, and in what state.
     DISTANCE — how far the camera is from the subject, in metres.
     HEIGHT   — which part of the body sits at the centre of the frame.

   Write it straight onto the card:
     FRAME INVENTORY — 2 people (her, centre, 1.5 m; him, left, 3 m) ·
     2 lights (lamp, left, 1 m; fire, back right, 4 m) · 3 objects
     (one brass thali, one folded cloth, one rope) · smoke level 2

   AND THE RULE THAT MAKES IT WORK: whatever the Image prompt says
   must already be in this inventory. If the image prompt mentions an
   object that is not counted here, the card is wrong — either count
   it, or take it out of the prompt.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
NOW WRITE ONE CARD PER SHOT, IN THIS EXACT FORMAT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

I will follow these cards literally. Everything I need must be on
the card. Never write "same as above". Never abbreviate.

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  SHOT 04 of 20        3 seconds        CHAIN from 03
  ⭐ STORY LINE (mine, quoted): "her fingers close on the cloth
     and she does not let go"   ← the only reason this shot exists
  PHASE: 2 "chaah"      SHOT TYPE: hands + close        SETUP: C
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  JOINS FROM: [what was still moving at the end of shot 03,
  and how this shot catches that same movement — e.g.
  "her hand was closing on the door handle; this shot
  opens on that same hand, from outside, still pulling"]
  HANDS TO: [what is still moving as this shot ends, so
  shot 05 can pick it up — e.g. "the door is swinging
  inward and her weight is already shifting forward"]

  🎥 MOVE: [the exact named move from ⑧, then direction, then
  speed. e.g. "Dolly In · slow · 1 metre" / "Truck Right · matching
  her pace" / "Static · locked off". One move only, never two. Mark
  it ⭐ if this is the film's single bold move.]
  💨 ATMOSPHERE (start → end): [the numbers from ⑲, then the look,
  then the direction — e.g. "smoke 2 → smoke 1: two wisps at chest
  height drifting left to right at walking pace, lit from behind by
  the lamp; ends as the column thins and clears her eyes and mouth"]
  ✨ PRACTICAL ELEMENT: [one physical thing moving in the air or on
  the ground in this shot — dust, smoke, spray, cloth, leaves. One
  only. Never a glow or a light ray.]
  🔗 HANDOFF: [if this shot hands over to the next one through a
  moving object, name the object, its direction, its speed and
  where it sits in the frame at the last frame — and write the
  same four things as the first frame of the next shot. Otherwise
  write "normal join".]
  🌋 BIG EFFECT: [only on the film's ONE effects shot — the physical
  event that happens in front of the camera, and what it does to
  her: the light it throws on her face, what it moves in her hair
  or cloth, what it displaces on the ground. Write "none" on every
  other shot.]
  👁 FACE REVEAL: [yes / no. If yes: which way the smoke or veil
  moves, how many seconds until the eyes and mouth are clear, and
  the one front light source with its distance. See ⑳.]
  👗 CLOTH STATE: [starts → changes → ends. Which garment, on which
  shoulder, wet or dry, how it moves during the shot, and the exact
  state it leaves in. See ㉗.]
  🎭 ACTING: [THOUGHT — one line of what she is thinking, in her own
  words. TURN — from what to what, and the second it turns. LEAK —
  the one involuntary thing. BUSY BODY — what her hands are doing
  while her face feels. Never one held expression.]

  🚶 MOVEMENT THROUGH THE FRAME: [one line — she enters frame left
  and exits frame right / she walks toward the lens and passes it /
  the camera pulls back and she shrinks. Never blank.]
  ENTERS FROM: [which edge]    EXITS AT: [which edge]
  🏞️ BEHIND HER: [what fills the background in this shot, and how it
  differs from the previous shot. If it is the same as the last two
  shots, this shot is stalling — move her on.]
  📏 DISTANCE COVERED: [for a travelling shot: where her body is at
  the first frame, where it is at the last frame, and roughly how
  many metres apart those are. Then name the landmarks she passes
  on the way. If the honest answer is "the same place", this shot
  is walking on the spot — rewrite it. Write "none, she stays put"
  only for a shot that is deliberately stationary.]
  📍 DESTINATION: [how far away the film's destination is now, and
  whether it is visible in this frame]
  📦 FRAME INVENTORY: [people (how many, where, how far) · lights
  (how many, what, where, how far) · objects (each, with its number)
  · cloth pieces · camera distance · frame centre height. Nothing
  may appear in the prompts below that is not counted here.]

  STAYS THE SAME ALL CLIP: [face, hairstyle, each garment and
  its exact state, each piece of jewellery, what each hand
  holds, light direction. These must be identical in the first
  and last frame.]
  ONLY THING THAT CHANGES: [the one action, plus the expression
  moving one step. Nothing else.]

  STORY: [one plain sentence — what is happening in the
  film right now and why it matters. A stranger must
  understand this line on its own.]

  JUST BEFORE: [the previous event]
  JUST AFTER:  [the next event]
  AUDIENCE FEELS: [what they feel watching this]
  ⛔ NOT ADDED: [nothing in this frame comes from your own
  imagination. Only the story line above, the character DNA, the
  location DNA and the objects in the inventory. Any small choice
  you had to make goes to MY ASSUMPTIONS at the end.]

──────── STEP 1 — MAKE THE FRAME ────────
  UPLOAD: CHAR-radha.png only — the upload portrait, nothing else.
  [Name only the characters actually visible in this shot. Never the
  world sheet, never the character sheet grid, never the style image.
  One portrait per visible character, and no more. Stacking
  references is what makes a face come out cartoonish.]
  SAVE AS:      SHOT-04.png

  PASTE THIS INTO THE IMAGE TOOL:
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  [The complete image prompt, written in THIS ORDER. The order is
   not decoration — an image model weights the words nearest the
   start most heavily. A realism instruction parked at the end
   arrives too late to stop the model rendering calendar art. So
   the capture comes FIRST and the description second.

   1. THE CAPTURE, first words of the prompt:
      "Photographed on a modern digital cinema camera, ARRI Alexa
       35, Cooke S7 anamorphic [n]mm lens at T2.8, [shot size],
       camera height [n] centimetres, [angle], clean and sharp with
       deep blacks."

   2. THE LIGHT, second — one source, named, with a dark side:
      "Lit by [one named source] from [exact direction], so the
       [named] side of the face is bright and the [named] side
       falls into real shadow. Colour temperature [warm low sun /
       cool overcast / warm lamplight]."
      ⚠️ ONE source only. Light arriving evenly from everywhere is
      the number one reason an image reads as a 3D render. There
      must be a bright side and a shadow side on every face.
      ⚠️ Never light a FACE only from behind. Backlight hides skin
      texture, and hidden texture is what makes skin look like
      plastic. If the sun is behind them, name the front fill too.
      ⭐ THE EXCEPTION — go all the way to a FULL SILHOUETTE. Let
      the figure go completely black against a bright sky or a
      bright doorway and backlight becomes the strongest shot a
      generator can make: no skin, no face, no detail left to get
      wrong, just a perfect black shape. One of these per film, on
      a big moment, is almost free quality. What fails is the
      half-measure — a backlit face that is still half-visible.
      Either light the face properly, or kill it completely.

   3. ⭐ the FULL DNA LINE for every character in this shot, pasted
      word for word from PROMPT 2. The whole line, every garment,
      every ornament. Never a shortened version, never "the same
      person as before". This is an IMAGE prompt — there is no
      picture for the tool to work from, so the complete line is
      what holds the face across the whole film.

   4. the exact number of people: "EXACTLY ONE PERSON IS IN THIS
      FRAME: [name]. No one else, no second figure, no duplicate,
      no reflection, nobody in the background."

   5. the figure size: "the figure fills about one-third of the
      frame height"

   6. THE FACIAL EXPRESSION AT FULL STRENGTH, written as muscles —
      the still must ALREADY carry the performance
   7. "a clear, strongly readable facial expression, not neutral,
      not a blank passport expression, the emotion visible in the
      eyes, brow and mouth at once"
   8. the exact body posture and what each hand holds
   9. the location DNA line, and the palette
  10. the ATMOSPHERE with its numbers, written as positive fact:
      "smoke level 2 — two thin wisps at chest height drifting
       left to right; her eyes and mouth completely clear"
  11. 9:16 vertical

  12. THE TEXTURE. A model left alone renders skin as smooth
      plastic. Texture is what makes it a photograph — but texture
      is not AGE. Never write pores, wrinkles, weathered, tarnish,
      faded, worn, slub or film grain for a young character; those
      words are what make a twenty-year-old come back looking fifty,
      and what makes a new film look like an old one. Include
      instead, word for word: "healthy skin with fine natural
      texture and a light sheen on the forehead and cheekbones,
      individually visible hair strands, fine soft hair at the
      hairline, crisp new fabric with a soft sheen where it catches
      the light, bright polished metal with deep sharp engraving,
      true depth of field falloff, clean modern digital capture,
      very deep blacks, clean bright highlights, strong contrast, a
      restrained palette sitting near grey with one colour allowed
      to be strong"

  13. ⚠️⚠️ THE CLOSING LINES — AND READ THIS WARNING FIRST.

      DO NOT WRITE A LIST OF "no this, no that" INTO THE PROMPT.

      This is the most damaging mistake in AI image work and almost
      every guide gets it wrong. Most tools people actually use —
      ChatGPT, Gemini and the rest — have NO separate negative box.
      There is only one text field. Whatever you type goes in as
      things to DRAW. These models cannot process "no", "not",
      "without" or "avoid"; the words simply register as content.

      Write "no god rays, no glowing skin, no floating petals" and
      you have just asked, in plain language, for god rays, glowing
      skin and floating petals. Ask for "a field with no horses" and
      you get horses. The longer your ban list, the more of the
      forbidden thing you are ordering.

      ✅ SO STATE EVERY CONSTRAINT AS A POSITIVE FACT INSTEAD.
        instead of "no plastic skin, no waxy skin"
          → "healthy skin with fine natural texture, a light sheen
             on the forehead and cheekbones"
        instead of "no god rays, no glow, no aura, no haze"
          → "clear dry air; every surface lit only by direct light
             and its own cast shadow; the darkest areas stay dark"
        instead of "no floating petals, no particles, no sparkles"
          → simply do not mention petals or particles at all.
        instead of "no duplicate person, no extra people"
          → "exactly one person is in this frame and the space
             behind is empty"
        instead of "no text, no watermark, no logo"
          → "every surface in the frame is blank and unmarked"

      So the prompt ENDS with this positive closing block, word for
      word, and with no ban list anywhere:

      "Healthy skin with fine natural texture, a light sheen on the
       forehead and cheekbones and soft fine hair at the hairline.
       Clear air; every surface lit only by direct light and its own
       cast shadow, and the shadow side stays genuinely dark. A
       restrained palette sitting near grey with one colour allowed
       to be strong, very deep blacks, clean bright highlights,
       strong contrast. Crisp new woven fabric falling in sharp
       folds with a soft sheen where it catches the light. Bright
       polished metal with deep sharp engraving. Clean modern
       digital capture, sharp through the eyes. Every surface in the
       frame is blank and unmarked."

      🔻 ONLY IF YOUR TOOL HAS A SEPARATE NEGATIVE BOX 🔻
      A few tools do have a real, separate negative field. If and
      only if you are using one, put a SHORT list in that box —
      never in the main prompt, and never more than about six terms:
        "3d render, cgi, illustration, calendar art, plastic skin,
         extra limbs"
      If you are pasting into ChatGPT or anything with a single text
      box, use nothing from this line.
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄

  ✅ CHECK BEFORE MOVING ON: cover the body and look only at the
  face. Can you name the emotion? If not, regenerate with stronger
  muscles. A beautiful frame with a blank face is a failed frame.
  [Also state what specifically to look for in this frame.]

──────── STEP 2 — MAKE THE VIDEO ────────
  UPLOAD: SHOT-04.png as the FIRST FRAME
          [ONLY if this shot and the next are in the SAME place with
           almost the same framing: SHOT-05.png as the LAST FRAME.
           If the next shot is in a different place, or the framing
           jumps, upload the first frame ONLY — see the rule below.]

  ⚠️ UPLOAD NOTHING ELSE. One image, or two if you pinned the last
  frame. Do not add the character sheet, the world sheet, the style
  image or the previous clip on top.

  ⭐ WHEN TO PIN A LAST FRAME — AND WHEN IT RUINS THE CLIP
  Many tools accept a first frame AND a last frame. Used correctly it
  is the strongest continuity tool there is. Used wrongly it is the
  reason a clip suddenly CUTS to a different scene halfway through.

  ⚠️ THE RULE: pin a last frame ONLY when the two stills are the same
  place, the same framing class and only a small change apart.

  A video model treats the two pinned frames as one continuous motion
  path. If they are far apart — different location, wide to extreme
  close-up, subject on the left then on the right — the model cannot
  travel between them in four seconds, so it does the only thing it
  can: it invents a camera move that does not exist, or it dissolves
  and morphs one scene into the other. On screen that reads as the
  clip changing scene in the middle. That is not the tool failing.
  That is two endpoints too far apart.

  ✅ PIN THE LAST FRAME when:
     • both stills are in the SAME location
     • the shot size changes by at most one step (medium → medium
       close, never wide → extreme close-up)
     • the subject stays on roughly the same side of the frame
     • the light direction and colour are identical
     • the whole change is something a body could really do in the
       clip's length

  ❌ DO NOT PIN A LAST FRAME when:
     • the next shot is in a DIFFERENT PLACE. This is the big one.
       Never pin across a location change. Let the clip end on its
       own end state and make a normal cut in the edit.
     • the framing jumps more than one step
     • the subject crosses to the other side of the frame
     • the light changes
     In all of those, give the tool the FIRST FRAME ONLY and write
     BLOCK 3, the end state, with real care.

  IF YOUR TOOL ONLY TAKES A FIRST FRAME:
   • Press EXTEND on the previous clip instead of starting fresh.
   • Failing that, take the last frame of the previous clip, upload it
     as this clip's first frame.
   • Then BLOCK 3 below must be written with real care, because the
     end state is the only destination the model is given.

  THE BEATS — one line per second, all of them filled:
    0-1s: [one small physical fact]
    1-2s: [one small physical fact]
    2-3s: [one small physical fact]
    [as many lines as the clip has seconds — never fewer]

  PASTE THIS INTO THE VIDEO TOOL:
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  [Write it in the blocks below, in this exact order, every time.
   Never skip a block.

   ⭐ THE LAW OF THIS BLOCK: THE STILL IS THE TRUTH.
   The uploaded still has already decided the face, the clothes, the
   place, the framing and the light. So the video prompt asks for ONE
   thing only: MOVEMENT. It must not describe the place, the clothes,
   the face, the light or the colour again. Those are in the still,
   and the model copies what it sees. Re-describing them is exactly
   how the face and the scene start to change.

   Write it in the SEVEN BLOCKS below.

   BLOCK 0 — THE IDENTITY LINE.
     Write the identity as a SHORT LOCK, not a full re-description:

       "The woman in the uploaded first frame. Her face, hair,
        lehenga, dupatta and every ornament stay exactly as they
        are in that frame."

     Then add ONLY the identity facts the still cannot show because
     they are out of frame or about to enter it — for a close-up of a
     face, name the braid and the anklets if the body enters later.

     WHEN TO PASTE THE FULL DNA LINE INSTEAD (three cases only):
       • the shot is a CUT to a new place, where you are NOT feeding
         the previous clip's last frame
       • the tool ignores the upload and invents its own person
       • the character is partly out of frame in the still and walks
         fully into view during the clip

   BLOCK 0B — MOVEMENT THROUGH THE FRAME, one line, from ⑥.
     "She enters frame left, crosses, and exits frame right."
     "She walks toward the camera and passes the lens."
     Never leave this out. A clip with no travel is a dead clip.

   BLOCK 1 — THE ONE ACTION, in five words or fewer.
     "She pulls the door open." "He lifts his head."
     One verb. If you need "and", it is two shots, not one.

   ⚠️ There is no word limit on this prompt. The old advice to keep
   video prompts to 25-45 words is WRONG for this workflow and it is
   what caused faces to change mid-film. Length spent on identity and
   on the second-by-second beats is never wasted; only vague words
   are wasted.

   BLOCK 2 — THE BEAT TIMELINE, one line per second of the clip.
     This is what stops the clip inventing a different action
     halfway. An unbooked second is a second the tool fills itself.
     ⭐ EVERY LINE HAS TWO HALVES: the BODY, then the FACE. A beat
     line with no face in it is a second of dead acting.
       0-1s: her fingers close on the handle — her eyes are still
             down, mouth closed, holding
       1-2s: her wrist turns, the door starts to swing — her chin
             lifts a little, her eyes come up to the gap
       2-3s: she steps forward through the opening — her brows
             pull together, her lips part slightly as she sees
     Every second of the clip length must have a line. A 4-second
     clip gets 4 lines. An 8-second clip gets 8.
     ⭐ On a travelling shot, every line also says HOW FAR she has
     got, with a named landmark (see ⑬).

   BLOCK 2B — THE PERFORMANCE. ⭐ THIS IS WHY ACTING LOOKS FAKE.
     A face that holds one expression for the whole clip is what a
     bad new actor does. A real actor's face CHANGES, and the
     change is the performance. Write all four, every clip:

     THE THOUGHT — one line of what is going through her head
       during this clip, in her own words. Not an emotion word, a
       thought. "He is going to say no and I already know it."

     THE TURN — the face must travel FROM one state TO another,
       and you must name the second it turns:
         "she begins almost blank, controlled; at 1.6 seconds her
          eyes narrow and her jaw sets and she stays there"
       Never the same expression at the first frame and the last.

     THE LEAK — one small involuntary thing the body does that
       she is not choosing: a swallow, a late blink, breath caught
       and released, nostrils widening, a lip pressed flat,
       fingers tightening on cloth, eyes going wet without a tear
       falling. One only.

     THE BUSY BODY — what her hands or body are doing while her
       face is feeling. A character who only emotes, with still
       hands, reads as generated.

     ⚠️ SUPPRESSION BEATS EXPRESSION. Wherever the emotion is big,
     write her TRYING TO HIDE IT and failing in one place.

     ⚠️ Never: smiling at the camera · looking into the lens ·
     a nod at the end · an expression that arrives and then holds
     for the rest of the clip.

   BLOCK 3 — THE END STATE. Say exactly where the body is at the
     final frame. This is the single biggest cause of a clip that
     starts right and goes wrong: the tool finishes what you
     described, finds it still has seconds left, and invents
     something to fill them. Booking the final frame stops that.
     ⚠️ END STATE does NOT mean "stops moving". The body must
     still be in motion at the last frame — but it must be THIS
     motion, at a stated point, not a new one:
       ✅ "at the final frame her front foot is landing on the road
           and her weight is still travelling forward"
       ✅ "the last frame catches the door still swinging inward,
           halfway open"
       ❌ "then she settles and holds still"  ← puts a full stop in
           the middle of your film and kills the join
     Only a shot that truly ends the film may come to rest.

   BLOCK 4 — ONE camera move, measurable, and nothing else.
     ⭐ Use the EXACT NAMED MOVE from ⑧, not a description of it.
       "Dolly In, slow, about one metre of travel"
       "Truck Right, matching her walking pace"
       "Static, camera locked off"
     Always name the move, then the direction, then the speed.
     Never two moves stacked.
     ⚠️ WHEN BOTH THE CAMERA AND THE SUBJECT MOVE, SAY HOW THEY
     RELATE: "the camera follows behind her at her walking pace,
     holding the same distance, while the lane slides past on both
     sides." Say what the camera does RELATIVE TO HER.

   BLOCK 5 — ONE environment motion, WITH ITS NUMBER from ⑲.
     "Smoke level 2 — two thin wisps at chest height, drifting left
      to right at walking pace, lit from behind by the lamp."
     "Rain level 1 — a few slow drops on her shoulder, visible
      against the dark cloth."
     One element. A real physical thing. Never a glow or a ray.

   BLOCK 6 — THE LOCK LINE, word for word, every single clip:
     "This is the same person as in the uploaded frame — the same
      bone structure, the same eyes, the same nose, the same lips,
      the same age, the same skin. Same clothes, same jewellery,
      same hair from the first frame to the last. Her EXPRESSION
      changes exactly as described above; her identity does not.
      She performs this one action and this action only. The camera
      makes this one move and holds it. One continuous unbroken
      take in real time; everything visible at the first frame is
      still there at the last."

   ⚠️⚠️ THE REASON ACTING LOOKS FAKE — READ THIS.
   The old version of this line said "the face holds exactly as it
   is in the uploaded frame for the whole clip". That line locks
   the PERFORMANCE, not just the identity. A tool given that line
   does the only thing it can: it holds one frozen expression for
   four seconds while the body moves underneath. On screen that is
   exactly a beginner actor holding a face and waiting for the
   director to say cut. Lock WHO the person is. Never lock what
   their face is doing.

   Write positively throughout. Never "don't move the camera" —
   write "the camera is locked and still".]
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  SAVE AS: CLIP-04.mp4

  ✅ CHECK: [what must be true in this clip]
  IF IT FAILS: [a simpler fallback prompt for this exact shot]

──────── THEN ────────
  ➡️ NEXT: go to SHOT 05.
  [If the next shot chains: "Screenshot the LAST FRAME of CLIP-04
   first — shot 05 needs it."]
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

━━━ RULES FOR THE CARDS ━━━
- One clip = ONE action. If a description needs "and" between two
  physical actions, split it into two shots. A clip asked to do
  three things will skip two.
- Clip length: 3-4s for a look, a blink, a turn, a reaction ·
  5s for walking or reaching · 8s for approaching or a full beat.
  Shorter clips drift less. Reactions are best at 3-4s.
- CHAIN inside a continuous moment. CUT only for a new time or
  place. Say which on every card.
- Body state at the end of a shot must equal the body state at the
  start of the next. If they differ, the change must be shown —
  that is a missing shot, add it to the chain and renumber.
- Nothing held may vanish or swap hands unexplained.
- No dialogue. The film must work on mute. Give ambient sound per
  shot instead.
- Everything must be supported by the research and the chain.
- EVERY CARD CARRIES ITS JOIN. On every card, under the header,
  write two lines:
     JOINS FROM: what was moving at the end of the previous shot,
     and how this shot picks that same movement up.
     HANDS TO: what is still moving as this shot ends, so the next
     shot can catch it.
  Shot 01 writes "JOINS FROM: film opens" and still writes HANDS TO.
- NO SHOT MAY START FROM STILL unless the previous shot ended with
  that same body already still. Movement at the cut is what makes
  two clips read as one continuous piece.
- A NEW PLACE IS ALWAYS ENTERED ON SCREEN. If this card is the first
  in a new location, the character must physically come into it in
  this shot or the one before. Never a waiting frame.
- THRESHOLD SHOTS ARE REAL SHOTS. Doors, gates, steps, paths,
  corners get their own card with their own image and video prompt.
  They are not optional and they are not filler — they are the
  glue. A person must be doing something physical in every one.
- EVERY SHOT CARRIES ITS PHASE from ㉘ and its numbers from ⑲.
  A card with no phase, or with "some smoke" instead of a level,
  is an unfinished card.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🔁 BEFORE YOU SHOW ME ANYTHING — RUN THIS LOOP
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You are not finished when the cards are written. You are finished
when they survive this. Run it yourself, silently. Do not show me a
draft and ask if it is right. Fix it, then show me.

PASS 0 — IS THIS STILL MY FILM?
  Put the STORY LOCK beside the cards. Same events, same order, same
  people, same feeling, same ending? Every difference is a defect and
  the lock always wins. You may not improve the story.

PASS 1 — READ IT AS A FILM
  Read only the STORY lines of every card, in order, as one
  paragraph. Does it read as ONE event a camera followed, or as
  separate pictures? Every place it reads as separate is a defect.

PASS 1B — THE TRACE AND THE ADDITIONS ⭐
  • Does every card open with a quoted STORY LINE? Any card without
    one is invented — delete it or mark it PURE TRANSITION.
  • Is the invented table printed and filled?
  • Is the MY CHEEZEIN coverage table printed, with every item
    pointing to a shot? Any item with no shot is a missing piece.
  • Is any object, person, animal, weather or ritual step in any
    card that is NOT in my story or the STORY LOCK? Cut it, or move
    it into MY ASSUMPTIONS.
  • Is MY ASSUMPTIONS printed at the very end, one line per choice?

PASS 2 — THE MOTION HANDOVER
  For each pair, put shot N's closing motion beside shot N+1's
  opening motion. Not merely compatible — the SAME movement from
  two angles. Any pair where it is not is a defect.

PASS 2B — THE JOINS AND THE THRESHOLDS
  Walk the film as a route, out loud, in one sentence:
  "she is HERE, then she goes THROUGH here, then she is HERE."
  If you cannot say it without the word "then suddenly", the film is
  not connected. Defects: any new location not physically ENTERED on
  screen · any place left without seeing her begin to leave · any
  A→B move with no threshold shot of a real physical action · any
  join row with fewer than two things carrying across · any
  threshold that is scenery with nobody in it.
  Fix by writing the missing threshold shot and renumbering.
  ⭐ And between two SCENES: is the merge shot from ㉓ there, with
  its two named devices?

PASS 2C — THE OPENING
  Look only at shot 01 and shot 02. Is something already moving at
  0.0s, or does the film warm up? Does shot 02 physically grow out
  of shot 01? A slow establishing wide, an empty landscape, a fade
  in, or a static frame waiting for someone to enter is a defect.

PASS 2D — HANDOFFS AND SAFE SHOTS
  • Read the SHOT TYPE column down. Count wide and full-body rows
    against detail rows (hands, eyes, objects, silhouettes, feet,
    backs of heads). If detail shots are not roughly three to one,
    rewrite until they are. This one ratio decides whether the film
    reads as expensive or as AI.
  • Is there at least one full silhouette, on a big moment?
  • Are there at least TWO object handoffs, one on the hardest jump
    in place or time? For each, do all four things match across the
    cut — the object, the direction, the speed, the position in
    frame? Is the handoff object already in the story?
  • Is any full-body walking shot longer than two seconds? Cut it
    down or replace it with feet, or with a silhouette.

PASS 3 — THE ACTING
  Does every face shot state what the character wants, what the face
  shows, what it hides, and one tell? Is the expression written as
  muscles at full strength, so the still already carries it? Any
  named emotion left standing alone is a defect.

PASS 3A — THE PERFORMANCE SWEEP
  Every shot with a face, check all six:
  • Does the face CHANGE during the clip, with a named second where
    it turns? One held expression across a whole clip is the single
    biggest reason acting reads as AI.
  • Is there a THOUGHT written in the character's own words?
  • Is there exactly one LEAK? Not three.
  • Are the hands and body doing something while the face feels?
  • Is the big emotion HELD DOWN rather than performed?
  • Does any video prompt still say the face holds exactly as it is
    in the uploaded frame? Delete that wording now.

PASS 3B — THE CALENDAR ART SWEEP
  • Does every image prompt OPEN with the capture — camera, lens,
    aperture, shot size, camera height? If the realism wording is
    parked at the end, move it to the front.
  • Is the word "photorealistic" used as a magic word? Delete it.
  • Is there ONE named light source with a direction, a bright side
    AND a shadow side? Is anyone lit only from behind and still
    half-visible? Either add a named front fill, or push it all the
    way to a full black silhouette.
  • Does the film have ONE full silhouette shot on a big moment?
  • Search every prompt and delete: glowing, luminous, radiant,
    aura, halo, god rays, light shafts, sunbeams, golden mist,
    golden haze, divine light, ethereal, dreamlike, otherworldly,
    magical, shimmering, sparkling, floating petals, floating
    particles, bloom, lens flare, backlit glow, rim glow, heavenly.
  • Divine characters: is the divinity carried by MATERIALS (heavy
    silk, worked gold, uncut gems, fresh flowers) and not by light?
  • Is the TEXTURE there — fine skin texture, a sheen on the
    cheekbone, separate hair strands, real fabric?
  • ⚠️ Is any AGEING language there — pores, wrinkles, weathered,
    tarnish, faded, worn, slub, film grain, muted? On a young
    character every one of those is a defect.
  • ⚠️⚠️ Is there a BAN LIST in any prompt? Search for "no ", "not ",
    "without", "avoid". Every one is a defect. Rewrite each as a
    positive fact. The only exception is a real separate negative
    box, no more than six terms, in that box only.
  • ⚠️ Does any prompt contain a DEITY'S NAME — Krishna, Radha,
    Shiva, Ram, Hanuman, Durga — or Lord, Shri, deity, divine, God?
    Delete every one and write the physical description instead.
  • Any poster buzzword — 8K, 4K, ultra-realistic, masterpiece,
    premium, devotional, majestic, epic, breathtaking, cinematic,
    poster, artwork, rendering? Delete and put a lens or a light in
    its place.
  • Is the colour restrained, or is everything saturated gold?

PASS 3C — THE CAMERA SWEEP
  • Does every shot name an EXACT move from ⑧, with a DIRECTION and
    a SPEED?
  • Count the bold moves — exactly ONE in the whole film.
  • Is any shot stacking two moves? Do two neighbouring shots use
    the same move AND the same size?
  • Does the film keep to 4-6 setups (㉕), each used at least four
    times, and is any scene stuck in one setup?
  • Is the GENRE MODE stated, and does every clip length, move and
    light choice obey it — with the PHASES (㉘) overriding inside
    each phase?
  • Is there exactly one speed ramp, on the peak?
  • Does every shot name ONE practical element, and is it a real
    physical thing rather than a glow or ray?

PASS 3D — MODERN AND BIG
  • Does every character DNA line open with an AGE IN YEARS?
  • Search every prompt for: pores, wrinkles, lines, weathered,
    worn, faded, tarnish, slub, grain, muted, aged, dusty, peeling,
    cracked. On a young character or a present-day setting, every
    one is a defect.
  • Is the capture line modern — digital cinema camera, clean and
    sharp — rather than film stock and grain?
  • Exactly ONE big effect shot, on the peak. Not zero. Not three.
    Is it written as a physical event with its light on her face,
    what it moves on her, what it leaves behind, and her face
    receiving it? Does a reaction shot follow it?
  • Would someone scrolling stop on this? If no single frame is
    worth stopping for, build the peak.

PASS 3E — THE ATMOSPHERE AND FACE SWEEP ⭐
  • Does EVERY effect carry a number — smoke 0-5, fog 0-5, dust
    0-4, rain 0-4, and the flame count for every fire? Any shot
    left saying "some smoke" or "light fog" is a defect.
  • Does any shot with a readable face sit above smoke 2, fog 2 or
    rain 2? Bring it down.
  • Does every face reveal have a direction, a movement and a
    duration, plus one front light source with its distance? Are the
    two shots after a reveal at smoke 0-1?
  • Does every shot start at the exact level the previous shot ended
    on, and does any change of more than one step have a cause?

PASS 3F — THE MECHANICS SWEEP ⭐
  • Does every hold, drape or touch have named hands and the three
    steps (lift → travel → land)? Does contact last a full second,
    with the other person reacting?
  • Does any cloth, hand or garland pass through a body, or change
    shoulder between shots?
  • Does every detail shot have DOES and SETS written?
  • Does every card carry a CLOTH STATE (starts → changes → ends),
    and do neighbouring cards agree?
  • Does every card carry a FRAME INVENTORY with real counts, and is
    everything in the prompts counted there?

PASS 4 — THE REST CHECK
  Any shot that begins or ends at rest is a defect — apart from the
  very first and the very last shot of the film.

PASS 4B — THE SECOND-BY-SECOND AUDIT
  • Count the beat lines. Does the count equal the clip length in
    seconds? Fill them or shorten the clip.
  • Is any second empty, vague, or a repeat of the one before?
  • Does BLOCK 3 state exactly where the body is at the final frame,
    still in motion?
  • Does any beat line contain a SECOND action? Split it.
  • Does the STAYS THE SAME list name the face, hair, every garment
    with its state, every piece of jewellery and what each hand
    holds?
  • Does anything change in the beats that is not in the ONLY THING
    THAT CHANGES list?
  • ⭐ Does the video prompt re-describe her face, clothes or the
    place? Delete those words. That is the "still is the truth"
    law — re-describing is what makes the face change mid-clip.
  • Is more than one camera move asked for? Is the clip longer than
    the action needs?

PASS 4C — THE DEAD SHOT SWEEP
  Does the FRAME change during each clip, or is a person standing in
  one spot while a small part of them moves? Any shot whose only
  movement is a hand, an earring, hair, cloth or a blink, with no
  travel, is a DEAD SHOT — rewrite it. Count the still shots: more
  than one in the film is a defect. Does every card have a MOVEMENT
  THROUGH THE FRAME line, an ENTERS FROM and an EXITS AT? Does the
  travel direction hold across the film? Any two neighbouring camera
  positions less than 30 degrees apart is a jump cut.

PASS 4D — THE TREADMILL SWEEP
  For every travelling card: where is her body at 0 seconds, and
  where at the last second? If it is the same place, rewrite it.
  Does the card state FROM, TO and metres? Does something LEAVE the
  frame behind her, named, with the second it goes? Does something
  GROW ahead? Do the beats name landmarks rather than only body
  parts? Does her size or position change — or, if the camera
  matches her pace, does the background clearly slide past? Does the
  prompt state the camera-to-subject relationship?

PASS 4E — THE PROGRESS SWEEP
  Read only the BEHIND HER column, top to bottom. Does the backdrop
  change at least every two shots? Is the destination visible and
  getting closer? Does the ground underfoot change? Could any two
  shots be swapped without anyone noticing — if yes, rewrite both.
  Do two neighbouring shots share both size and move?

PASS 4F — THE HANDOFF SWEEP
  For every pair: is shot N's last frame the same picture as shot
  N+1's first frame? If the tool takes a last frame, is it said
  explicitly on the card? Does every still work as the previous
  shot's destination — same light direction, same distance class,
  body caught mid-movement? Does any video prompt describe the
  endpoints instead of the journey?

PASS 4G — THE DNA SWEEP
  IMAGE prompts: full DNA line, word for word, every time; never
  shortened to "the same person as before"; full location DNA line.
  VIDEO prompts: SHORT LOCK, not a full re-description — with the
  full line only in the three justified cases. Does every video
  prompt end with the face lock line? Getting this split wrong in
  either direction is a defect.

PASS 4H — THE UPLOAD SWEEP
  Does any card ask me to upload more than one portrait per visible
  character? Does any card ask for a character sheet grid, a world
  sheet or a style image? Remove them from every upload line.
