──────── STEP 1 — MAKE THE FRAME ────────
  UPLOAD: CHAR-radha.png only — the upload portrait, nothing else.
  [Name only the characters actually visible in this shot. Never the
  world sheet, never the character sheet grid, never the style image.
  One portrait per visible character, and no more. Stacking
  references is what makes a face come out cartoonish.]
  SAVE AS:      SHOT-04.png

  PASTE THIS INTO THE IMAGE TOOL:
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  [The complete image prompt, written in THIS ORDER. The order is
   not decoration — an image model weights the words nearest the
   start most heavily. A realism instruction parked at the end
   arrives too late to stop the model rendering calendar art. So
   the capture comes FIRST and the description second.

   1. THE CAPTURE, first words of the prompt:
      "Photographed on a modern digital cinema camera, ARRI Alexa
       35, Cooke S7 anamorphic [n]mm lens at T2.8, [shot size],
       camera height [n] centimetres, [angle], clean and sharp with
       deep blacks."

   2. THE LIGHT, second — one source, named, with a dark side:
      "Lit by [one named source] from [exact direction], so the
       [named] side of the face is bright and the [named] side
       falls into real shadow. Colour temperature [warm low sun /
       cool overcast / warm lamplight]."
      ⚠️ ONE source only. Light arriving evenly from everywhere is
      the number one reason an image reads as a 3D render. There
      must be a bright side and a shadow side on every face.
      ⚠️ Never light a FACE only from behind. Backlight hides skin
      texture, and hidden texture is what makes skin look like
      plastic. If the sun is behind them, name the front fill too.
      ⭐ THE EXCEPTION — go all the way to a FULL SILHOUETTE. Let
      the figure go completely black against a bright sky or a
      bright doorway and backlight becomes the strongest shot a
      generator can make: no skin, no face, no detail left to get
      wrong, just a perfect black shape. One of these per film, on
      a big moment, is almost free quality. What fails is the
      half-measure — a backlit face that is still half-visible.
      Either light the face properly, or kill it completely.

   3. ⭐ the FULL DNA LINE for every character in this shot, pasted
      word for word from PROMPT 2. The whole line, every garment,
      every ornament. Never a shortened version, never "the same
      person as before". This is an IMAGE prompt — there is no
      picture for the tool to work from, so the complete line is
      what holds the face across the whole film.

   4. the exact number of people: "EXACTLY ONE PERSON IS IN THIS
      FRAME: [name]. No one else, no second figure, no duplicate,
      no reflection, nobody in the background."

   5. the figure size: "the figure fills about one-third of the
      frame height"

   6. THE FACIAL EXPRESSION AT FULL STRENGTH, written as muscles —
      the still must ALREADY carry the performance
   7. "a clear, strongly readable facial expression, not neutral,
      not a blank passport expression, the emotion visible in the
      eyes, brow and mouth at once"
   8. the exact body posture and what each hand holds
   9. the location DNA line, and the palette
  10. the ATMOSPHERE with its numbers, written as positive fact:
      "smoke level 2 — two thin wisps at chest height drifting
       left to right; her eyes and mouth completely clear"
  11. 9:16 vertical

  12. THE TEXTURE. A model left alone renders skin as smooth
      plastic. Texture is what makes it a photograph — but texture
      is not AGE. Never write pores, wrinkles, weathered, tarnish,
      faded, worn, slub or film grain for a young character; those
      words are what make a twenty-year-old come back looking fifty,
      and what makes a new film look like an old one. Include
      instead, word for word: "healthy skin with fine natural
      texture and a light sheen on the forehead and cheekbones,
      individually visible hair strands, fine soft hair at the
      hairline, crisp new fabric with a soft sheen where it catches
      the light, bright polished metal with deep sharp engraving,
      true depth of field falloff, clean modern digital capture,
      very deep blacks, clean bright highlights, strong contrast, a
      restrained palette sitting near grey with one colour allowed
      to be strong"

  13. ⚠️⚠️ THE CLOSING LINES — AND READ THIS WARNING FIRST.

      DO NOT WRITE A LIST OF "no this, no that" INTO THE PROMPT.

      This is the most damaging mistake in AI image work and almost
      every guide gets it wrong. Most tools people actually use —
      ChatGPT, Gemini and the rest — have NO separate negative box.
      There is only one text field. Whatever you type goes in as
      things to DRAW. These models cannot process "no", "not",
      "without" or "avoid"; the words simply register as content.

      Write "no god rays, no glowing skin, no floating petals" and
      you have just asked, in plain language, for god rays, glowing
      skin and floating petals. Ask for "a field with no horses" and
      you get horses. The longer your ban list, the more of the
      forbidden thing you are ordering.

      ✅ SO STATE EVERY CONSTRAINT AS A POSITIVE FACT INSTEAD.
        instead of "no plastic skin, no waxy skin"
          → "healthy skin with fine natural texture, a light sheen
             on the forehead and cheekbones"
        instead of "no god rays, no glow, no aura, no haze"
          → "clear dry air; every surface lit only by direct light
             and its own cast shadow; the darkest areas stay dark"
        instead of "no floating petals, no particles, no sparkles"
          → simply do not mention petals or particles at all.
        instead of "no duplicate person, no extra people"
          → "exactly one person is in this frame and the space
             behind is empty"
        instead of "no text, no watermark, no logo"
          → "every surface in the frame is blank and unmarked"

      So the prompt ENDS with this positive closing block, word for
      word, and with no ban list anywhere:

      "Healthy skin with fine natural texture, a light sheen on the
       forehead and cheekbones and soft fine hair at the hairline.
       Clear air; every surface lit only by direct light and its own
       cast shadow, and the shadow side stays genuinely dark. A
       restrained palette sitting near grey with one colour allowed
       to be strong, very deep blacks, clean bright highlights,
       strong contrast. Crisp new woven fabric falling in sharp
       folds with a soft sheen where it catches the light. Bright
       polished metal with deep sharp engraving. Clean modern
       digital capture, sharp through the eyes. Every surface in the
       frame is blank and unmarked."

      🔻 ONLY IF YOUR TOOL HAS A SEPARATE NEGATIVE BOX 🔻
      A few tools do have a real, separate negative field. If and
      only if you are using one, put a SHORT list in that box —
      never in the main prompt, and never more than about six terms:
        "3d render, cgi, illustration, calendar art, plastic skin,
         extra limbs"
      If you are pasting into ChatGPT or anything with a single text
      box, use nothing from this line.
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄

  ✅ CHECK BEFORE MOVING ON: cover the body and look only at the
  face. Can you name the emotion? If not, regenerate with stronger
  muscles. A beautiful frame with a blank face is a failed frame.
  [Also state what specifically to look for in this frame.]

──────── STEP 2 — MAKE THE VIDEO ────────
  UPLOAD: SHOT-04.png as the FIRST FRAME
          [ONLY if this shot and the next are in the SAME place with
           almost the same framing: SHOT-05.png as the LAST FRAME.
           If the next shot is in a different place, or the framing
           jumps, upload the first frame ONLY — see the rule below.]

  ⚠️ UPLOAD NOTHING ELSE. One image, or two if you pinned the last
  frame. Do not add the character sheet, the world sheet, the style
  image or the previous clip on top.

  ⭐ WHEN TO PIN A LAST FRAME — AND WHEN IT RUINS THE CLIP
  Many tools accept a first frame AND a last frame. Used correctly it
  is the strongest continuity tool there is. Used wrongly it is the
  reason a clip suddenly CUTS to a different scene halfway through.

  ⚠️ THE RULE: pin a last frame ONLY when the two stills are the same
  place, the same framing class and only a small change apart.

  A video model treats the two pinned frames as one continuous motion
  path. If they are far apart — different location, wide to extreme
  close-up, subject on the left then on the right — the model cannot
  travel between them in four seconds, so it does the only thing it
  can: it invents a camera move that does not exist, or it dissolves
  and morphs one scene into the other. On screen that reads as the
  clip changing scene in the middle. That is not the tool failing.
  That is two endpoints too far apart.

  ✅ PIN THE LAST FRAME when:
     • both stills are in the SAME location
     • the shot size changes by at most one step (medium → medium
       close, never wide → extreme close-up)
     • the subject stays on roughly the same side of the frame
     • the light direction and colour are identical
     • the whole change is something a body could really do in the
       clip's length

  ❌ DO NOT PIN A LAST FRAME when:
     • the next shot is in a DIFFERENT PLACE. This is the big one.
       Never pin across a location change. Let the clip end on its
       own end state and make a normal cut in the edit.
     • the framing jumps more than one step
     • the subject crosses to the other side of the frame
     • the light changes
     In all of those, give the tool the FIRST FRAME ONLY and write
     BLOCK 3, the end state, with real care.

  IF YOUR TOOL ONLY TAKES A FIRST FRAME:
   • Press EXTEND on the previous clip instead of starting fresh.
   • Failing that, take the last frame of the previous clip, upload it
     as this clip's first frame.
   • Then BLOCK 3 below must be written with real care, because the
     end state is the only destination the model is given.

  THE BEATS — one line per second, all of them filled:
    0-1s: [one small physical fact]
    1-2s: [one small physical fact]
    2-3s: [one small physical fact]
    [as many lines as the clip has seconds — never fewer]

  PASTE THIS INTO THE VIDEO TOOL:
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  [Write it in the blocks below, in this exact order, every time.
   Never skip a block.

   ⭐ THE LAW OF THIS BLOCK: THE STILL IS THE TRUTH.
   The uploaded still has already decided the face, the clothes, the
   place, the framing and the light. So the video prompt asks for ONE
   thing only: MOVEMENT. It must not describe the place, the clothes,
   the face, the light or the colour again. Those are in the still,
   and the model copies what it sees. Re-describing them is exactly
   how the face and the scene start to change.

   Write it in the SEVEN BLOCKS below.

   BLOCK 0 — THE IDENTITY LINE.
     Write the identity as a SHORT LOCK, not a full re-description:

       "The woman in the uploaded first frame. Her face, hair,
        lehenga, dupatta and every ornament stay exactly as they
        are in that frame."

     Then add ONLY the identity facts the still cannot show because
     they are out of frame or about to enter it — for a close-up of a
     face, name the braid and the anklets if the body enters later.

     WHEN TO PASTE THE FULL DNA LINE INSTEAD (three cases only):
       • the shot is a CUT to a new place, where you are NOT feeding
         the previous clip's last frame
       • the tool ignores the upload and invents its own person
       • the character is partly out of frame in the still and walks
         fully into view during the clip

   BLOCK 0B — MOVEMENT THROUGH THE FRAME, one line, from ⑥.
     "She enters frame left, crosses, and exits frame right."
     "She walks toward the camera and passes the lens."
     Never leave this out. A clip with no travel is a dead clip.

   BLOCK 1 — THE ONE ACTION, in five words or fewer.
     "She pulls the door open." "He lifts his head."
     One verb. If you need "and", it is two shots, not one.

   ⚠️ There is no word limit on this prompt. The old advice to keep
   video prompts to 25-45 words is WRONG for this workflow and it is
   what caused faces to change mid-film. Length spent on identity and
   on the second-by-second beats is never wasted; only vague words
   are wasted.

   BLOCK 2 — THE BEAT TIMELINE, one line per second of the clip.
     This is what stops the clip inventing a different action
     halfway. An unbooked second is a second the tool fills itself.
     ⭐ EVERY LINE HAS TWO HALVES: the BODY, then the FACE. A beat
     line with no face in it is a second of dead acting.
       0-1s: her fingers close on the handle — her eyes are still
             down, mouth closed, holding
       1-2s: her wrist turns, the door starts to swing — her chin
             lifts a little, her eyes come up to the gap
       2-3s: she steps forward through the opening — her brows
             pull together, her lips part slightly as she sees
     Every second of the clip length must have a line. A 4-second
     clip gets 4 lines. An 8-second clip gets 8.
     ⭐ On a travelling shot, every line also says HOW FAR she has
     got, with a named landmark (see ⑬).

   BLOCK 2B — THE PERFORMANCE. ⭐ THIS IS WHY ACTING LOOKS FAKE.
     A face that holds one expression for the whole clip is what a
     bad new actor does. A real actor's face CHANGES, and the
     change is the performance. Write all four, every clip:

     THE THOUGHT — one line of what is going through her head
       during this clip, in her own words. Not an emotion word, a
       thought. "He is going to say no and I already know it."

     THE TURN — the face must travel FROM one state TO another,
       and you must name the second it turns:
         "she begins almost blank, controlled; at 1.6 seconds her
          eyes narrow and her jaw sets and she stays there"
       Never the same expression at the first frame and the last.

     THE LEAK — one small involuntary thing the body does that
       she is not choosing: a swallow, a late blink, breath caught
       and released, nostrils widening, a lip pressed flat,
       fingers tightening on cloth, eyes going wet without a tear
       falling. One only.

     THE BUSY BODY — what her hands or body are doing while her
       face is feeling. A character who only emotes, with still
       hands, reads as generated.

     ⚠️ SUPPRESSION BEATS EXPRESSION. Wherever the emotion is big,
     write her TRYING TO HIDE IT and failing in one place.

     ⚠️ Never: smiling at the camera · looking into the lens ·
     a nod at the end · an expression that arrives and then holds
     for the rest of the clip.

   BLOCK 3 — THE END STATE. Say exactly where the body is at the
     final frame. This is the single biggest cause of a clip that
     starts right and goes wrong: the tool finishes what you
     described, finds it still has seconds left, and invents
     something to fill them. Booking the final frame stops that.
     ⚠️ END STATE does NOT mean "stops moving". The body must
     still be in motion at the last frame — but it must be THIS
     motion, at a stated point, not a new one:
       ✅ "at the final frame her front foot is landing on the road
           and her weight is still travelling forward"
       ✅ "the last frame catches the door still swinging inward,
           halfway open"
       ❌ "then she settles and holds still"  ← puts a full stop in
           the middle of your film and kills the join
     Only a shot that truly ends the film may come to rest.

   BLOCK 4 — ONE camera move, measurable, and nothing else.
     ⭐ Use the EXACT NAMED MOVE from ⑧, not a description of it.
       "Dolly In, slow, about one metre of travel"
       "Truck Right, matching her walking pace"
       "Static, camera locked off"
     Always name the move, then the direction, then the speed.
     Never two moves stacked.
     ⚠️ WHEN BOTH THE CAMERA AND THE SUBJECT MOVE, SAY HOW THEY
     RELATE: "the camera follows behind her at her walking pace,
     holding the same distance, while the lane slides past on both
     sides." Say what the camera does RELATIVE TO HER.

   BLOCK 5 — ONE environment motion, WITH ITS NUMBER from ⑲.
     "Smoke level 2 — two thin wisps at chest height, drifting left
      to right at walking pace, lit from behind by the lamp."
     "Rain level 1 — a few slow drops on her shoulder, visible
      against the dark cloth."
     One element. A real physical thing. Never a glow or a ray.

   BLOCK 6 — THE LOCK LINE, word for word, every single clip:
     "This is the same person as in the uploaded frame — the same
      bone structure, the same eyes, the same nose, the same lips,
      the same age, the same skin. Same clothes, same jewellery,
      same hair from the first frame to the last. Her EXPRESSION
      changes exactly as described above; her identity does not.
      She performs this one action and this action only. The camera
      makes this one move and holds it. One continuous unbroken
      take in real time; everything visible at the first frame is
      still there at the last."

   ⚠️⚠️ THE REASON ACTING LOOKS FAKE — READ THIS.
   The old version of this line said "the face holds exactly as it
   is in the uploaded frame for the whole clip". That line locks
   the PERFORMANCE, not just the identity. A tool given that line
   does the only thing it can: it holds one frozen expression for
   four seconds while the body moves underneath. On screen that is
   exactly a beginner actor holding a face and waiting for the
   director to say cut. Lock WHO the person is. Never lock what
   their face is doing.

   Write positively throughout. Never "don't move the camera" —
   write "the camera is locked and still".]
  ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄
  SAVE AS: CLIP-04.mp4

  ✅ CHECK: [what must be true in this clip]
  IF IT FAILS: [a simpler fallback prompt for this exact shot]

──────── THEN ────────
  ➡️ NEXT: go to SHOT 05.
  [If the next shot chains: "Screenshot the LAST FRAME of CLIP-04
   first — shot 05 needs it."]
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

━━━ RULES FOR THE CARDS ━━━
- One clip = ONE action. If a description needs "and" between two
  physical actions, split it into two shots. A clip asked to do
  three things will skip two.
- Clip length: 3-4s for a look, a blink, a turn, a reaction ·
  5s for walking or reaching · 8s for approaching or a full beat.
  Shorter clips drift less. Reactions are best at 3-4s.
- CHAIN inside a continuous moment. CUT only for a new time or
  place. Say which on every card.
- Body state at the end of a shot must equal the body state at the
  start of the next. If they differ, the change must be shown —
  that is a missing shot, add it to the chain and renumber.
- Nothing held may vanish or swap hands unexplained.
- No dialogue. The film must work on mute. Give ambient sound per
  shot instead.
- Everything must be supported by the research and the chain.
- EVERY CARD CARRIES ITS JOIN. On every card, under the header,
  write two lines:
     JOINS FROM: what was moving at the end of the previous shot,
     and how this shot picks that same movement up.
     HANDS TO: what is still moving as this shot ends, so the next
     shot can catch it.
  Shot 01 writes "JOINS FROM: film opens" and still writes HANDS TO.
- NO SHOT MAY START FROM STILL unless the previous shot ended with
  that same body already still. Movement at the cut is what makes
  two clips read as one continuous piece.
- A NEW PLACE IS ALWAYS ENTERED ON SCREEN. If this card is the first
  in a new location, the character must physically come into it in
  this shot or the one before. Never a waiting frame.
- THRESHOLD SHOTS ARE REAL SHOTS. Doors, gates, steps, paths,
  corners get their own card with their own image and video prompt.
  They are not optional and they are not filler — they are the
  glue. A person must be doing something physical in every one.
- EVERY SHOT CARRIES ITS PHASE from ㉘ and its numbers from ⑲.
  A card with no phase, or with "some smoke" instead of a level,
  is an unfinished card.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🔁 BEFORE YOU SHOW ME ANYTHING — RUN THIS LOOP
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You are not finished when the cards are written. You are finished
when they survive this. Run it yourself, silently. Do not show me a
draft and ask if it is right. Fix it, then show me.

PASS 0 — IS THIS STILL MY FILM?
  Put the STORY LOCK beside the cards. Same events, same order, same
  people, same feeling, same ending? Every difference is a defect and
  the lock always wins. You may not improve the story.

PASS 1 — READ IT AS A FILM
  Read only the STORY lines of every card, in order, as one
  paragraph. Does it read as ONE event a camera followed, or as
  separate pictures? Every place it reads as separate is a defect.

PASS 1B — THE TRACE AND THE ADDITIONS ⭐
  • Does every card open with a quoted STORY LINE? Any card without
    one is invented — delete it or mark it PURE TRANSITION.
  • Is the invented table printed and filled?
  • Is the MY CHEEZEIN coverage table printed, with every item
    pointing to a shot? Any item with no shot is a missing piece.
  • Is any object, person, animal, weather or ritual step in any
    card that is NOT in my story or the STORY LOCK? Cut it, or move
    it into MY ASSUMPTIONS.
  • Is MY ASSUMPTIONS printed at the very end, one line per choice?

PASS 2 — THE MOTION HANDOVER
  For each pair, put shot N's closing motion beside shot N+1's
  opening motion. Not merely compatible — the SAME movement from
  two angles. Any pair where it is not is a defect.

PASS 2B — THE JOINS AND THE THRESHOLDS
  Walk the film as a route, out loud, in one sentence:
  "she is HERE, then she goes THROUGH here, then she is HERE."
  If you cannot say it without the word "then suddenly", the film is
  not connected. Defects: any new location not physically ENTERED on
  screen · any place left without seeing her begin to leave · any
  A→B move with no threshold shot of a real physical action · any
  join row with fewer than two things carrying across · any
  threshold that is scenery with nobody in it.
  Fix by writing the missing threshold shot and renumbering.
  ⭐ And between two SCENES: is the merge shot from ㉓ there, with
  its two named devices?

PASS 2C — THE OPENING
  Look only at shot 01 and shot 02. Is something already moving at
  0.0s, or does the film warm up? Does shot 02 physically grow out
  of shot 01? A slow establishing wide, an empty landscape, a fade
  in, or a static frame waiting for someone to enter is a defect.

PASS 2D — HANDOFFS AND SAFE SHOTS
  • Read the SHOT TYPE column down. Count wide and full-body rows
    against detail rows (hands, eyes, objects, silhouettes, feet,
    backs of heads). If detail shots are not roughly three to one,
    rewrite until they are. This one ratio decides whether the film
    reads as expensive or as AI.
  • Is there at least one full silhouette, on a big moment?
  • Are there at least TWO object handoffs, one on the hardest jump
    in place or time? For each, do all four things match across the
    cut — the object, the direction, the speed, the position in
    frame? Is the handoff object already in the story?
  • Is any full-body walking shot longer than two seconds? Cut it
    down or replace it with feet, or with a silhouette.

PASS 3 — THE ACTING
  Does every face shot state what the character wants, what the face
  shows, what it hides, and one tell? Is the expression written as
  muscles at full strength, so the still already carries it? Any
  named emotion left standing alone is a defect.

PASS 3A — THE PERFORMANCE SWEEP
  Every shot with a face, check all six:
  • Does the face CHANGE during the clip, with a named second where
    it turns? One held expression across a whole clip is the single
    biggest reason acting reads as AI.
  • Is there a THOUGHT written in the character's own words?
  • Is there exactly one LEAK? Not three.
  • Are the hands and body doing something while the face feels?
  • Is the big emotion HELD DOWN rather than performed?
  • Does any video prompt still say the face holds exactly as it is
    in the uploaded frame? Delete that wording now.

PASS 3B — THE CALENDAR ART SWEEP
  • Does every image prompt OPEN with the capture — camera, lens,
    aperture, shot size, camera height? If the realism wording is
    parked at the end, move it to the front.
  • Is the word "photorealistic" used as a magic word? Delete it.
  • Is there ONE named light source with a direction, a bright side
    AND a shadow side? Is anyone lit only from behind and still
    half-visible? Either add a named front fill, or push it all the
    way to a full black silhouette.
  • Does the film have ONE full silhouette shot on a big moment?
  • Search every prompt and delete: glowing, luminous, radiant,
    aura, halo, god rays, light shafts, sunbeams, golden mist,
    golden haze, divine light, ethereal, dreamlike, otherworldly,
    magical, shimmering, sparkling, floating petals, floating
    particles, bloom, lens flare, backlit glow, rim glow, heavenly.
  • Divine characters: is the divinity carried by MATERIALS (heavy
    silk, worked gold, uncut gems, fresh flowers) and not by light?
  • Is the TEXTURE there — fine skin texture, a sheen on the
    cheekbone, separate hair strands, real fabric?
  • ⚠️ Is any AGEING language there — pores, wrinkles, weathered,
    tarnish, faded, worn, slub, film grain, muted? On a young
    character every one of those is a defect.
  • ⚠️⚠️ Is there a BAN LIST in any prompt? Search for "no ", "not ",
    "without", "avoid". Every one is a defect. Rewrite each as a
    positive fact. The only exception is a real separate negative
    box, no more than six terms, in that box only.
  • ⚠️ Does any prompt contain a DEITY'S NAME — Krishna, Radha,
    Shiva, Ram, Hanuman, Durga — or Lord, Shri, deity, divine, God?
    Delete every one and write the physical description instead.
  • Any poster buzzword — 8K, 4K, ultra-realistic, masterpiece,
    premium, devotional, majestic, epic, breathtaking, cinematic,
    poster, artwork, rendering? Delete and put a lens or a light in
    its place.
  • Is the colour restrained, or is everything saturated gold?

PASS 3C — THE CAMERA SWEEP
  • Does every shot name an EXACT move from ⑧, with a DIRECTION and
    a SPEED?
  • Count the bold moves — exactly ONE in the whole film.
  • Is any shot stacking two moves? Do two neighbouring shots use
    the same move AND the same size?
  • Does the film keep to 4-6 setups (㉕), each used at least four
    times, and is any scene stuck in one setup?
  • Is the GENRE MODE stated, and does every clip length, move and
    light choice obey it — with the PHASES (㉘) overriding inside
    each phase?
  • Is there exactly one speed ramp, on the peak?
  • Does every shot name ONE practical element, and is it a real
    physical thing rather than a glow or ray?

PASS 3D — MODERN AND BIG
  • Does every character DNA line open with an AGE IN YEARS?
  • Search every prompt for: pores, wrinkles, lines, weathered,
    worn, faded, tarnish, slub, grain, muted, aged, dusty, peeling,
    cracked. On a young character or a present-day setting, every
    one is a defect.
  • Is the capture line modern — digital cinema camera, clean and
    sharp — rather than film stock and grain?
  • Exactly ONE big effect shot, on the peak. Not zero. Not three.
    Is it written as a physical event with its light on her face,
    what it moves on her, what it leaves behind, and her face
    receiving it? Does a reaction shot follow it?
  • Would someone scrolling stop on this? If no single frame is
    worth stopping for, build the peak.

PASS 3E — THE ATMOSPHERE AND FACE SWEEP ⭐
  • Does EVERY effect carry a number — smoke 0-5, fog 0-5, dust
    0-4, rain 0-4, and the flame count for every fire? Any shot
    left saying "some smoke" or "light fog" is a defect.
  • Does any shot with a readable face sit above smoke 2, fog 2 or
    rain 2? Bring it down.
  • Does every face reveal have a direction, a movement and a
    duration, plus one front light source with its distance? Are the
    two shots after a reveal at smoke 0-1?
  • Does every shot start at the exact level the previous shot ended
    on, and does any change of more than one step have a cause?

PASS 3F — THE MECHANICS SWEEP ⭐
  • Does every hold, drape or touch have named hands and the three
    steps (lift → travel → land)? Does contact last a full second,
    with the other person reacting?
  • Does any cloth, hand or garland pass through a body, or change
    shoulder between shots?
  • Does every detail shot have DOES and SETS written?
  • Does every card carry a CLOTH STATE (starts → changes → ends),
    and do neighbouring cards agree?
  • Does every card carry a FRAME INVENTORY with real counts, and is
    everything in the prompts counted there?

PASS 4 — THE REST CHECK
  Any shot that begins or ends at rest is a defect — apart from the
  very first and the very last shot of the film.

PASS 4B — THE SECOND-BY-SECOND AUDIT
  • Count the beat lines. Does the count equal the clip length in
    seconds? Fill them or shorten the clip.
  • Is any second empty, vague, or a repeat of the one before?
  • Does BLOCK 3 state exactly where the body is at the final frame,
    still in motion?
  • Does any beat line contain a SECOND action? Split it.
  • Does the STAYS THE SAME list name the face, hair, every garment
    with its state, every piece of jewellery and what each hand
    holds?
  • Does anything change in the beats that is not in the ONLY THING
    THAT CHANGES list?
  • ⭐ Does the video prompt re-describe her face, clothes or the
    place? Delete those words. That is the "still is the truth"
    law — re-describing is what makes the face change mid-clip.
  • Is more than one camera move asked for? Is the clip longer than
    the action needs?

PASS 4C — THE DEAD SHOT SWEEP
  Does the FRAME change during each clip, or is a person standing in
  one spot while a small part of them moves? Any shot whose only
  movement is a hand, an earring, hair, cloth or a blink, with no
  travel, is a DEAD SHOT — rewrite it. Count the still shots: more
  than one in the film is a defect. Does every card have a MOVEMENT
  THROUGH THE FRAME line, an ENTERS FROM and an EXITS AT? Does the
  travel direction hold across the film? Any two neighbouring camera
  positions less than 30 degrees apart is a jump cut.

PASS 4D — THE TREADMILL SWEEP
  For every travelling card: where is her body at 0 seconds, and
  where at the last second? If it is the same place, rewrite it.
  Does the card state FROM, TO and metres? Does something LEAVE the
  frame behind her, named, with the second it goes? Does something
  GROW ahead? Do the beats name landmarks rather than only body
  parts? Does her size or position change — or, if the camera
  matches her pace, does the background clearly slide past? Does the
  prompt state the camera-to-subject relationship?

PASS 4E — THE PROGRESS SWEEP
  Read only the BEHIND HER column, top to bottom. Does the backdrop
  change at least every two shots? Is the destination visible and
  getting closer? Does the ground underfoot change? Could any two
  shots be swapped without anyone noticing — if yes, rewrite both.
  Do two neighbouring shots share both size and move?

PASS 4F — THE HANDOFF SWEEP
  For every pair: is shot N's last frame the same picture as shot
  N+1's first frame? If the tool takes a last frame, is it said
  explicitly on the card? Does every still work as the previous
  shot's destination — same light direction, same distance class,
  body caught mid-movement? Does any video prompt describe the
  endpoints instead of the journey?

PASS 4G — THE DNA SWEEP
  IMAGE prompts: full DNA line, word for word, every time; never
  shortened to "the same person as before"; full location DNA line.
  VIDEO prompts: SHORT LOCK, not a full re-description — with the
  full line only in the three justified cases. Does every video
  prompt end with the face lock line? Getting this split wrong in
  either direction is a defect.

PASS 4H — THE UPLOAD SWEEP
  Does any card ask me to upload more than one portrait per visible
  character? Does any card ask for a character sheet grid, a world
  sheet or a style image? Remove them from every upload line.
  Does every video card upload the shot still and nothing else,
  except a legitimately pinned last frame? Is the total number of
  images I must make as small as it can be?

PASS 4I — THE LAST FRAME SWEEP
  For every card that pins a last frame: same location? framing
  within one step? subject on the same side? same light? a change a
  real body could make in this clip's length? Any "no" means unpin
  it and write the end state properly instead.

PASS 5 — THE FULL AUDIT
  STORY: every character arrives and leaves on screen · nobody
  appears before their ARRIVES event · no action happens twice ·
  every object tracked · a stranger could never ask "how did they
  get there?"
  CONTINUITY: body state, hands, wardrobe and light checked shot to
  shot · light only moves forward.
  CAMERA: 180-degree line held · screen direction held · eyelines
  match · every angle given an emotional reason · camera height
  stated everywhere.
  FRAMING: every shot states the headcount and rules out duplicates ·
  every shot states the figure size.
  PERFORMANCE: every event has a reaction shot · no two consecutive
  shots hold the same expression · every character's face is
  different at the end than at the beginning.
  CLIP DISCIPLINE: every second booked · constants named · stated
  final frame.
  ROUTE: film travels one way · every place entered and left on
  screen · no place holds more than 3 shots in a row.
  OPENING: opens mid-movement, no warming up.
  THREAD: travelling object in 5+ shots · repeating frame · one
  ambient sound · colour accent.

PASS 6 — FIX
  Rewrite the offending shots. Fix by rewriting the pair — extend
  the action, re-angle, move the cut into the motion, carry the
  camera, carry the sound. Never by inserting filler clips.

PASS 7 — RUN AGAIN
  Go back to PASS 0. Keep looping until a full pass finds ZERO
  defects. Three or four rounds is normal. Do the work.

Then print at the top of your output:
  CORRECTION LOOP: [n] rounds.
  Fixed: [one line each]
  Final pass: 0 defects.
  This film plays as: [one sentence — the single continuous event
  the viewer experiences]

If you cannot honestly write "0 defects", you are not finished.

━━━ AFTER THE CARDS ━━━
Print, in this order:
  1. THE TABLES — invented table · MY CHEEZEIN coverage · camera
     coverage (setup × count) · phase table · join table summary.
  2. THE ORDER OF WORK — make every frame first, then every clip.
     Never alternate. List the exact sequence of files.
  3. TRIM PLAN — clips × 8s versus target runtime, and how many
     seconds to keep from each clip. Mark the 3 shots that stay
     long. ⚠️ NEVER TRIM AWAY A THRESHOLD SHOT. When the film runs
     long, shorten the long middle shots — never delete the door,
     the gate, the steps or the entrance. A threshold may be cut to
     1 second, but it may not be removed. Confirm the opening still
     begins mid-movement after the trim.
  4. SOUND — ambient per shot, and one score prompt for Suno or Lyria.
  5. EDIT SETTINGS — grain 8-15%, saturation −10 to −15%, warm
     shadows, cool highlights, vignette, 24fps, 0.3s dissolves.
  6. MY ASSUMPTIONS — the full list, at the very end.
  7. FINAL CHECK — a short list of what must be true before posting.

baaki sab — isi order me (6 parts)

| 22 | Action attach nahi lagta | **MOTION HANDOVER** — N ka aakhri movement = N+1 ka pehla, word for word | P3 ⑫ |
| 23 | Keyframe pinning se clip beech me scene badal jaata | **LAST FRAME RULE** — sirf same jagah, same framing par pin karo | P3 STEP 2 |
| 24 | Positives vs negative list ka confusion | **POSITIVE ONLY** — "no" list kabhi nahi; tool ke alag negative box me sirf 6 terms | P3 3B |
🗺️ FILM BANANE KA ORDER
STEP 5 · block · 63 words
dekho
1. PROMPT 1 paste → STORY LOCK milega
2. PROMPT 2 paste → style test, phir CHAR-naam.png portraits
3. PROMPT 3 paste → saare shot cards
4. Saari IMAGES banao (SHOT-01 → last)     ← beech me video mat banao
5. Saare CLIPS banao (CLIP-01 → last)
6. Trim → edit → grade → audio
7. PROMPT 4 → gaana + caption + timing
✅ FILE KHATAM — AB YE KARO
STEP 10 · block · 69 words
dekho
1. PROMPT 1 → STORY LOCK                  (kahani likhna mat bhoolna)
2. PROMPT 2 → style test, phir portraits  (video prompt yahan NAHI)
3. PROMPT 3 → saare shot cards
4. Saari IMAGES   (SHOT-01 se last tak)   ← ek saath

# 🎬 AI-FILM-PROMPTS — MASTER v10
### 4 prompt · story-first · real acting · har frame connected · Veo + image tool

> **Ye tumhare 4-prompt system ka fixed version hai.** Wahi 4 prompt hain — PROMPT 1, 2, 3, 4.
> Nayi system nahi, naya tarika nahi. Sirf wahi saari galtiyan theek ki hui jo shuru se lekar abhi tak bani hain.
>
> **Kaam ka tarika:** 4 prompt hain. Ek ke baad ek paste karo. Bas.

---

## ⚡ 2 MINUTE ME SAMJHO

| Step | Kya paste karo | Kya milega |
|---|---|---|
| 1️⃣ | **PROMPT 1** + apni kahani | STORY LOCK + route + shot list |
| 2️⃣ | **PROMPT 2** + STORY LOCK | style test + character portraits + duniya |
| 3️⃣ | **PROMPT 3** + STORY LOCK | har shot ka card — image prompt + video prompt |
| 4️⃣ | **PROMPT 4** + STORY LOCK | gaana, caption, hashtag, timing (film ban jaane ke baad) |

**Kahani kahan likhni hai:** PROMPT 1 ke sabse neeche, `MY STORY` ke aage. 2–4 line. Bas.

**Sabse zaroori niyam:** pehle saari IMAGES, phir saare CLIPS. Beech me video mat banao.

---

## 🔗 TEEN CHEEZEIN — EK CHAIN (story · image · video)

```
   TUMHARI KAHANI KI LINE       →   IMAGE (still)          →   VIDEO
   "uske haath me kapda aa gaya"    STORY LINE + DNA +         SIRF MOVEMENT
                                    inventory                   (still hi sach hai)
```

| | Kahan se banega | Kya NAHI likhna |
|---|---|---|
| **STORY LINE** | tumhare shabdon me, quotes me | apni kahani mat jodo |
| **IMAGE** | sirf story line + DNA + inventory | jo inventory me nahi, wo frame me nahi |
| **VIDEO** | sirf **movement** — still me sab pehle se hai | jagah/kapda/chehra dobara describe mat karo |

**Niyam:** video me naya object/chehra/jagah chahiye = **still galat hai** → still theek karo, video me kuch mat jodo.
Aur jo tumne likha hi nahi — koi riwaz, koi cheez, koi janwar — wo frame me **nahi** aayega. Zaroori ho to AI `MY ASSUMPTIONS` me likhega, chupke se nahi banayega.

---

## ⭐ v10 ME KYA BADLA — SAARI GALTIYAN EK JAGAH (shuru se abhi tak)

| # | Galti | v10 me fix | Kahan |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Acting nakli lagti hai, face patthar jaisa | **PERFORMANCE** — thought · change · leak · suppression · busy body, aur purani "face holds exactly as it is" line hata di | P3 ⑤ + card 2B |
| 2 | Character budhe, modern nahi | **AGE LINE** — umar se shuru; 30 se kam par wrinkled/weathered/grain ban | P2 |
| 3 | VFX kuch nahi | **BIG EFFECT** ek, peak par + har shot me ek chhota practical element | P3 ⑪ |
| 4 | Story meri jaisi nahi banti | **MERI CHEEZEIN** — har cheez film me aayegi, aur aakhir me tick hogi | P1 B + P3 ㉑ |
| 5 | Look pehle, story baad me | **STORY FIRST** — emotion se look nikalta hai, phase engine | P3 ㉘ |
| 6 | Do log ek frame me, rishta nahi | **RELATIONSHIP** — intention → action → reaction → consequence, reaction late | P3 ⑤ + ⑯ |
| 7 | Kapda static | **COSTUME STATE LINE** — har shot me kapde ki halat (start → end) | P3 ㉗ |
| 8 | Detail shots bekaar | **DETAIL = 2 KAAM** — pichhle ka result + agle ka setup | P3 ㉖ |
| 9 | Merge sirf object se | **6 MERGE DEVICES** + mid-scene merge shot | P3 ㉓ |
| 10 | Ek jagah ghoomti rehti hai (treadmill) | **PROGRESS LAW** — background har 2 shot me badle, manzil paas aaye | P1 + P3 ⑥ |
| 11 | Face badal jaata hai | **DNA LINE** word-for-word + short lock video me | P2 + P3 ④G |
| 12 | Cartoon / calendar-art | naam ban · glow words ban · portrait rule · capture-first | P2 + P3 3B |
| 13 | **2nd prompt me hi video prompt de deta hai** | **STEP LOCK** — P2 sirf portrait banata hai; video/camera/timestamp = jawab reject | P2 Rule 9 |
| 14 | Smoke/fog ka amount koi nahi batata | **ATMOSPHERE NUMBER SYSTEM** — smoke 0–5, fog 0–5, dust 0–4, rain 0–4 | P3 ⑲ |
| 15 | "Thoda fog" likha, model ne poora frame bhar diya | **FACE REVEAL LAW** — face shot me level 2 se zyada kabhi nahi; reveal = movement + direction + 2 second | P3 ⑳ |
| 16 | Frame me kya-kya hai, ginti nahi | **FRAME INVENTORY** — kitne log, lights, objects, metres | P3 ㉙ |
| 17 | **Video me naya scene ban jaata hai** | **STILL IS THE TRUTH** — video prompt sirf movement; dikhaawat dobara describe nahi | P3 card STEP 2 |
| 18 | AI apni marzi se cheezein jod deta hai | **NOTHING ADDED** — bina bole kuch frame me nahi; ASSUMPTIONS me likhe | P3 ㉒ |
| 19 | Hold/odhna kaise, koi nahi likhta | **HOLD & DRAPE MECHANICS** — naam liye haath, 3 steps | P3 ㉔ |
| 20 | 4–6 camera verify nahi hota | **COVERAGE CHECK** — 4–6 setups, har scene 4 se, sirf 1 bold move | P3 ㉕ |
| 21 | Sirf object handoff se join | **JOIN + THRESHOLD + HANDOFF** — poora join table, har place ka darwaza | P3 ⑯⑰ |
| 22 | Action attach nahi lagta | **MOTION HANDOVER** — N ka aakhri movement = N+1 ka pehla, word for word | P3 ⑫ |
| 23 | Keyframe pinning se clip beech me scene badal jaata | **LAST FRAME RULE** — sirf same jagah, same framing par pin karo | P3 STEP 2 |
| 24 | Positives vs negative list ka confusion | **POSITIVE ONLY** — "no" list kabhi nahi; tool ke alag negative box me sirf 6 terms | P3 3B |

**Aur ek cheez jo har jagah jaayegi:** har card ke sabse upar **STORY LINE** — tumhari kahani ki wahi line jisse wo shot bana hai. Jis shot ki line nahi, wo shot hai hi nahi.

```
1. PROMPT 1 paste → STORY LOCK milega

4. Saari IMAGES banao (SHOT-01 → last)     ← beech me video mat banao
5. Saare CLIPS banao (CLIP-01 → last)
6. Trim → edit → grade → audio
7. PROMPT 4 → gaana + caption + timing
```

**Do niyam yaad rakho:**
1. **Pehle saari images, phir saare clips.** Alternating karne se look toot jaata hai.
2. **Step lock:** AI 2nd step me video prompt banaye = galat. Bolo: *"Step 3 abhi nahi. Sirf portraits do."*

# 1️⃣ PROMPT 1 — STORY + PLAN

```text
You are a film director, screenwriter and researcher.

I am making my own short film. Not an advertisement, not a brand
video. Never mention brands, clients, marketing or audiences.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🚫 DO NOT ASK ME ANY QUESTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Never ask me about emotion, tone, mood, ending, style, pacing,
music, audience or "what feeling do you want". All of it is
derivable from the story. Deriving it is your job.

You may put exactly ONE thing to me, and only after you have done
the work: "Direction 1, 2 or 3?" Nothing else, ever.

⚠️ THE ONE EXCEPTION — A MISSING STORY.
Scroll to the bottom of this message and look at MY STORY. If that
block is empty, or still contains the placeholder text in brackets,
or is too vague to film, then STOP IMMEDIATELY. Do not research.
Do not invent a story. Do not pick a famous story you think I meant.
Do not continue with any step.
Reply with exactly this and nothing else:

  "Mujhe aapki kahani chahiye. 2-4 line me likh dijiye — jo bhi
   yaad hai, apne shabdon me. Jaise:
   'Krishna gopiyon ke kapde lekar kadamb ke ped par chadh jaate
    hain, gopiyan paani me hain, phir wo maafi maangti hain.'
   Bas itna kaafi hai. Research aur baaki sab mera kaam hai."

Then wait. The story is the one thing I must give you and the one
thing you can never derive. Everything else — emotion, tone,
ending, style, camera, casting — you derive yourself, and asking
me about any of those is still forbidden.

NEVER ask me to upload, provide or identify a reference image, an
example, a photo or a link. I have nothing to give you. You have the
research and my story — that is everything you need. Decide yourself.
If something is genuinely missing, state an assumption and continue:
"Assuming [X] — correct me if wrong." Never an open question.

DERIVE, DO NOT ASK:
| If the story is | Emotion | Ending | Register |
|---|---|---|---|
| A divine leela | awe, tenderness, goosebumps | release, then a small ache | DIVINE |
| A god's mercy | surrender, tears | pure release | DIVINE |
| A god's play | delight becoming meaning | release + a lingering look | DIVINE |
| A separation | longing | ache — do not resolve it | DIVINE |
| A sacrifice | pride through grief | ache held in silence | ROYAL |
| A battle | rising defiance | release, one beat of cost | ROYAL |
| An injustice | quiet anger | ache | GROUNDED |
| A family memory | warmth and loss | ache, then warmth | GROUNDED |
| A folk tale | wonder | release | STYLISED |

Always true: the film ends on an image, never narration · ambient
sound is the default, no voiceover unless I said so · if the story
is about a deity the register is DIVINE, always.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
DO THESE SEVEN STEPS IN ORDER
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

═══ STEP A — RESEARCH THE TRUTH ═══
Search the web. My story is from memory and may be wrong or
incomplete. Find what actually happened.
Report briefly: the verified sequence of events · the era, season
and time of day · exact clothing, jewellery, weapons and objects of
that time and place · what the landscape looked like THEN · the
common mistakes people make about this story · one true detail
almost nobody knows · anything sensitive to respect.
If I got something wrong, say so in one line and use the truth.
Mark anything unknown as UNKNOWN — never invent.

═══ STEP B — READ MY STORY BACK ═══
Prove you understood before you create. Eight lines:
genre · the one emotion this story exists to produce · whose story
it truly is (whose heart breaks or opens) · era, season, time of day
· visual register · and the one thing that, if got wrong, kills the
film. If I said something wrong, correct it here.

⭐ THEN, BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE, LIST MY NON-NEGOTIABLES.
Go back to MY STORY and pull out every concrete thing I actually
wrote — every person I named, every place, every object, every
action, every image. Print them as a numbered list headed
MERI CHEEZEIN. These are mine and they are fixed.

Then the rule that governs everything after this point: every one
of those items must survive into the finished film. You may add
around them. You may order them differently. You may not replace
one with something you think is better, quieter, safer or more
tasteful, and you may not drop one because it is hard to
generate. If I wrote a flood, there is a flood. If I wrote a
crowd, there is a crowd. The commonest way this process fails is
that my rough idea gets smoothed into a generic pretty film that
is no longer mine.

At the very end of PROMPT 1 you will tick that list off, item by
item, and show me where each one lands.
And in PROMPT 3 every single shot will have to quote one line of
MY STORY — the line that produced it. So my story must come back
here in my own words, complete, not summarised.

═══ STEP C — GIVE ME THREE DIRECTIONS ═══
Do not ask what I want. Tell me what is possible.
Three genuinely different ways to tell this — different POV,
different entry point in time, different emotional frame.

  DIRECTION [n] — [name it]
  Angle        : whose eyes, where we enter the story
  Opens on     : the literal first image, and the question it plants
  Turns on     : the moment everything changes
  Ends on      : the last image — release or ache
  Feeling      : what the viewer feels in the last 3 seconds
  Why it works : one line
  Risk         : one line

⚠️ ALL THREE DIRECTIONS MUST CONTAIN EVERY ITEM FROM MERI CHEEZEIN.
They differ in whose eyes we watch through, where we come in, and
what the film is finally about. They do NOT differ in which of my
things they keep. A direction that quietly drops my flood, my
crowd or my object is not a direction, it is a different story.
Under each one, write the line: "Keeps all of MERI CHEEZEIN: yes".

Mark one ⭐ RECOMMENDED. Choose the one with the most EMOTION, not
the most action. Then ask: "Direction 1, 2 or 3?" and WAIT.
If I reply "go", use your recommended one.

═══ STEP D — THE IMAGINATION PASS ═══
(after I have picked)
The imagination is YOUR job, not mine. Work these out and state them:
  • THE UNTOLD DETAIL — the one true thing from research that almost
    nobody knows. Build a moment around it. This is what makes
    people comment "I never knew this".
  • THE LADDER — something must change every 5 seconds. Write what
    changes, and when, across the whole runtime.
  • THE ONE IMAGE — the single frame they cannot forget.
  • THE LAST BEAT — one small moment AFTER the obvious ending.
  • ⭐ THE PHASES — break the film into 3 to 5 EMOTION PHASES and
    name them (for example: shanti → chaah → pariksha → toot →
    kripa). Every phase gets ONE line saying what the audience
    feels and how the world looks while it lasts. These phases
    decide the look, the light, the colour and the effects of every
    shot automatically in PROMPT 3 — so they are decided HERE, in
    the story, not left for later.
    Write them like this:
      PHASE 1 "shanti" — khula, thandi roshni, hara-cream, dheema
      PHASE 2 "chaah" — paas, garam, ek loud rang, roshni ek side se
      PHASE 3 "toot"  — sabse paas, andhera, haath me camera, bada
                        VFX event yahin
      PHASE 4 "kripa" — phir khulta hai, saaf tez roshni, sabse loud
                        rang, shaant locked frame

Rules: show the consequence, not the event · withhold, do not
explain · track one object through the film · silence before impact
· weather and light carry the feeling.
Banned: narration explaining emotion · beauty shots with no story ·
predictable shots · any beat repeated.

═══ STEP E — BUILD THE STORY FROM ITS TRUE BEGINNING ═══

⚠️ Whatever I described is the MIDDLE of the story, never the start.
Build what came BEFORE (how everyone got there) and AFTER (where
everyone went). Then write a numbered EVENT CHAIN — every physical
event in time order, plain language, one event per line.

Chain rules:
- Start from the earliest moment the audience needs to see.
- Every character's ENTRY is shown. Nobody appears from nowhere.
- Every character's EXIT is shown. Nobody vanishes.
- Every move from place A to place B is its own line.
- Every object tracked: where it came from, who holds it, where it
  ends up.
- CONNECTIVE BEATS: if a body must change state between two events,
  that change is its own event. Sitting in 5 and standing in 6?
  Then "she rises" is event 5b. Empty-handed then holding a lamp?
  "She picks up the lamp" is an event. Never skip these.

Then a CAST PRESENCE TIMELINE — one column per character, one row
per event, each cell exactly one of:
   ABSENT · ARRIVES (from where, how) · ON · OFF (where they are
   and what they are doing) · LEAVES (to where) · GONE
Hard rules: nobody goes ABSENT → ON without ARRIVES in between.
Nobody goes ON → GONE without LEAVES. A group arriving together is
still an arrival and still needs to be seen.
Then one line per character: "[name] enters at event [#] from
[where], leaves at event [#] to [where]." If you cannot fill that
line, the chain is incomplete — fix it.

Finally answer plainly. Any "not shown" means an event is missing:
  • Where does the story open, and why is that the right first frame?
  • How does each character arrive?
  • What is the exact turning point?
  • Where does each character go afterwards?
  • Where does it end, and why is that the right last image?
  • Could a viewer ever ask "how did they get there?", "where did
    she come from?", or "when did she stand up?" — if yes, fix it.
  • Does any action happen twice? Delete one, or make the second a
    clearly later stage with visible progress in between.

═══ STEP E2 — THE ROUTE (how the film travels) ═══

A film feels connected when the audience can WALK the route in their
head. Not "scene 1, scene 2" — but "she was here, then she went
through there, then she came out here."

Write THE ROUTE as one unbroken line of places:
   PLACE 1 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 2 → [how she leaves] → PLACE 3 ...

⚠️ THE THRESHOLD LAW — this is what makes scenes feel connected:
Between any two places there must be THREE shown beats, never two:
   1. LEAVING   — she is seen exiting place 1 (standing up, turning,
                  walking out of frame, reaching for the door)
   2. THRESHOLD — the in-between itself: the doorway, the gate, the
                  stairs, the path, the boat, the corner she turns.
                  This is a real physical action by a real character.
   3. ARRIVING  — she is seen entering place 2 (coming through the
                  gate, stepping onto the road, the light changing
                  on her face as the new place opens up)

Worked example so there is no confusion:
   ❌ NOT CONNECTED: she is getting ready at home → she is dancing
      on the road.  The audience is dropped. It feels like two
      different videos.
   ✅ CONNECTED: getting ready at home → she picks up her dupatta and
      turns to the door → she pulls the door open → she walks down
      the steps → her hand pushes the gate open → the gate swings and
      the road is revealed behind it → she steps onto the road →
      she stops, the music hits → she begins to dance.
   Same story. But now every scene grew out of the one before it.

Rules for the route:
- The film travels ONE WAY. Never return to a place unless the
  return is itself the story, and then it must look different.
- The THRESHOLD beat is never an empty cutaway of scenery. It is
  always a person doing a physical thing — a hand on a gate, feet on
  a step, a curtain pushed aside. If nobody is doing anything in
  it, delete it; it is filler.
- No place gets more than 3 shots in a row.
- Every threshold is a shot. Write it into the event chain now.

⭐ THE PROGRESS LAW — the cure for "the video isn't going anywhere"
A film can obey every rule above and STILL feel stuck. That happens
when the character moves a lot but never gets anywhere — she walks,
she turns, she walks again, all inside the same patch of ground, and
the audience sees the same walls behind her every time. Movement is
not progress. Progress is the BACKGROUND being different.

So the test is not "is she moving?" — it is "has the world behind
her changed?"

For every shot, write one line: WHAT IS BEHIND HER. Then read that
column straight down. If the same backdrop repeats more than twice in
a row, the film has stalled, however busy the character is.

Three ways to force real progress, use all three:

 1. THE BACKGROUND CHANGES EVERY SHOT OR TWO. She leaves the mud wall
    behind and now there are reeds. Then water. Then the far bank. If
    shot 7 could be swapped with shot 4 and nobody would notice, both
    shots are standing still.

 2. DISTANCE TO THE DESTINATION SHRINKS, VISIBLY. Decide the one place
    the whole film is heading toward — the tree, the gate, the river,
    the person. Then put it IN the frame, getting closer:
      shot 3  — a shape far off through the reeds
      shot 6  — the tree now fills a third of the frame behind her
      shot 9  — she is under its branches
    The audience cannot feel progress they cannot see. Give them a
    landmark and let it grow.

 3. THE GROUND ITSELF CHANGES. Swept earth, then dust, then wet soil,
    then river sand. Underfoot texture is the cheapest and strongest
    signal that she has genuinely travelled.

Also: never give two neighbouring shots the same shot size AND the
same camera move. Wide-tracking, then wide-tracking, reads as one
long stuck moment even when the location has changed.

Print a PROGRESS TABLE before the shot list:
   shot # · place · what is behind her · how far to the destination
Then confirm in one line: "The background changes at least every two
shots, and the destination is visibly closer each time it appears."
If it is not true, add the missing shots now.

Print the route, then confirm: "Every place change in this film has
a LEAVING, a THRESHOLD and an ARRIVING." If one is missing, add the
event and renumber the chain.

═══ STEP E3 — THE EFFECT DECISION (numbers, not guesses)  ⭐ v10 ═══

"Some smoke" and "light fog" mean nothing. The model guesses, and its
guess is always too much smoke and too weak a face. So decide the
amounts HERE, in the story, with numbers that mean the same thing in
every shot of the whole film:

   smoke 0-5 · fog 0-5 · dust 0-4 · rain 0-4
   fire: how many flames, how tall in centimetres, and where each one is

  smoke 0 none · 1 one wisp thinner than a wrist · 2 two or three
  wisps at chest height, face still clean · 3 a low band up to the
  waist plus two columns, eyes and mouth still sharp · 4 up to the
  neck, eyes and brow only · 5 whole frame, silhouette only
  fog 0 none · 1 ankle · 2 knee · 3 waist · 4 chest · 5 full frame
  dust 1 motes in a beam · 2 off the ground under footsteps ·
  3 the beam itself visible · 4 the air greys out, distance lost
  rain 1 few slow drops · 2 steady, visible on dark cloth ·
  3 heavy, water standing on the ground · 4 downpour, faces unusable

⭐ THE RULE THAT MATTERS: in any shot where the audience must READ
the face, nothing in front of it goes above 2. Levels 4 and 5 are
for silhouettes, wides and object shots only.

Write it like this in the story, beside the phases:
   "during the bath, heavy steam — smoke level 2, face completely
    clean" · "the curse scene hides her face — smoke level 5,
    silhouette only, the reveal comes later" · "rain level 3,
    water standing on the ground"

Nothing in this film may be described later as "a little smoke" or
"some fog". It is a number, chosen now.

═══ STEP F — THE STORY LOCK ═══

⚠️ THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT OUTPUT OF THIS PROMPT.
Everything after this will be done by tools that have NEVER SEEN
this conversation. If the story is not written down here completely
and plainly, it WILL be lost and the video will end up being about
something else.

Print a self-contained block that makes full sense to a stranger.
Plain words, no jargon:

  ═══════════ STORY LOCK ═══════════
  TITLE:
  LOGLINE: one sentence — who, what they want, what stops them.
  THE FILM IN SIX LINES: what happens, first to last.
  WHERE AND WHEN: place, era, season, time of day, duration.
  VISUAL REGISTER: DIVINE / ROYAL / GROUNDED / STYLISED
  WHO: for each character — name, who they are, what they want in
       this film, and how they are different at the end.
  EVENT CHAIN: the full numbered chain.
  THE ROUTE: place → how she leaves → place → ... with the LEAVING,
       THRESHOLD and ARRIVING beat named for every change of place.
  CAST PRESENCE TIMELINE: the full table.
  THE TURNING POINT:
  HOW IT ENDS: the last image and the feeling it leaves.
  FEELING SCENE BY SCENE: one line each — what the audience feels.
  EMOTIONAL PEAK: the single shot carrying the most feeling.
  THE ONE IMAGE: the frame they must remember.
  ⭐ THE PHASES: the 3-5 emotion phases, in order, with one line of
       look each (light, colour, camera, effects).
  ⭐ THE EFFECTS (numbers): every place smoke, fog, dust, rain or
       fire appears, with its number and whether a face is readable.
       Plus the ONE big effect and the shot it lands on.
  ⭐ MY CHEEZEIN COVERED: a short table — every item of MERI CHEEZEIN
       and the event number where it appears.
  DO NOT CHANGE THESE: the 5 things that would break the film.
  ═══════════════════════════════════

Then tell me exactly this:
"✅ STORY LOCK ready. Copy it and save it. Now paste PROMPT 2
together with this STORY LOCK."

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
MY STORY (rough, from memory — research it and fix it)
⚠️ If the line below is empty or still in brackets, STOP and ask
   me for the story. Never invent one.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

[YAHAN APNI KAHANI 2-4 LINE ME LIKHO]

RUNTIME: [30 / 45 / 60] seconds
```

---
---

# 2️⃣ PROMPT 2 — CHARACTERS + DUNIYA

```text
Now build the reference images for this film.

━━━ RULES FOR THIS WHOLE PROMPT ━━━
• Do not ask me any questions except the one style question below.
• NEVER ask me for a reference image, an upload, or an example.
  I have nothing to give you. Everything you need is in the STORY
  LOCK at the bottom of this message. Read it and decide yourself.
• You are not generating images here. You are WRITING THE IMAGE
  PROMPTS that I will paste into an image tool myself. Every prompt
  must be complete, self-contained and copy-paste ready.

━━━ ⛔ RULE 0 — STEP LOCK (ye step sirf CHEHRE aur JAGAH ka hai) ━━━
This step makes characters and places. Nothing else. Not one thing else.

  ✅ ALLOWED in your answer:
     · the story read back in eight lines
     · the style test prompts (STYLE-A2 / STYLE-A)
     · the cast list
     · the DNA line for every person
     · the location DNA line for every place
     · the palette
     · ONE upload portrait prompt per person
     · the character sheet and world sheet prompts (planning only)
     · the opening frame prompt
     · the upload list

  ❌ NOT ALLOWED in this answer, not even as an example, not even in
     a small box, not even "just to show you":
     · any video prompt
     · any camera movement, lens or camera height for a shot
     · any timestamp like [00:00-00:02]
     · any shot list, shot number or shot card
     · any beat timeline
     · any music, caption or hashtag

If even one video prompt or one camera move for a shot appears in
your answer, the whole answer is wrong. Throw it away and write it
again using the allowed list only.

⭐ And finish your answer with this exact line, so I always know what
is next:

   "STEP 2 POORA. Ab PROMPT 3 paste karo — shot cards wahi banayega.
    Us se pehle koi video prompt nahi banega."

━━━ FIRST: THE STYLE ━━━
Do not ask me what style I want, and do not ask me for a reference.
YOU choose one single moment from this film to use as the style
test — pick the strongest emotional moment from the STORY LOCK, one
character, one clear action. State which moment you picked and why,
in one line.

Then write TWO complete image prompts of THAT SAME moment — same
character, same pose, same framing, same light. Only the visual
treatment differs. Each prompt must fully describe the character,
the place and the action on its own, because the image tool has
seen nothing else.

Label them clearly and give each a filename:
STYLE-A2.png, STYLE-A.png

  STYLE A2 — MODERN LIVE ACTION ⭐ DEFAULT
  A real actor photographed on location on a modern digital cinema
  camera, ARRI Alexa 35 with Cooke S7 anamorphic lenses, clean and
  sharp. ONE motivated light source with a named direction, a clear
  bright side and a clear shadow side on the face. Healthy skin with
  fine natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and
  cheekbones, individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric
  with a soft sheen where it catches the light, bright polished
  metal, true depth of field falloff, very deep blacks, clean bright
  highlights, strong contrast, and a restrained colour palette where
  most of the frame sits near grey and only ONE colour is allowed to
  be strong. Looks like a frame from a film released this year.

  STYLE A — HIGH CONTRAST CINEMA
  The same capture discipline and the same modern cleanliness, one
  motivated light source, deeper shadows, stronger contrast, shallow
  depth of field. Bolder and more graphic, still a photograph.

Tell me: "Generate these two images, look at them, and tell me which
you prefer — A2 or A?" Then WAIT for my answer.
Do not continue to the character sheets until I have replied.

⚠️ THERE IS NO "DIVINE GLOW" STYLE AND YOU MUST NOT INVENT ONE.
A glow style is how a film turns into calendar art. If a divine
register is needed, it is carried by MATERIALS, not by light — see
the rule below.

━━━ THEN: THE IMAGE PROMPTS ━━━
Once I pick, write me the actual copy-paste image prompts. I will
paste each one into an image tool myself. Number them and give me a
filename for each so the files stay organised.

⚠️ IF THE REGISTER IS DIVINE OR ROYAL, read this first. It is the
single easiest way to wreck a film, and almost everyone wrecks it.

THE RULE: DIVINITY COMES FROM MATERIALS, NEVER FROM LIGHT.

A god looks divine because of what he is WEARING and what he is MADE
of — heavy raw silk with a deep sheen, bright worked gold with sharp
deep engraving that catches the light, big uncut gems, dense fresh
flowers, skin lit like skin. He does not look divine because the air
is full of golden mist. He looks divine because he is wearing more,
and better, than anyone else in the frame.

The moment you add glow, the image stops being a photograph. These
words are what produce that plastic, computer-rendered, calendar-art
result, and they are BANNED in every prompt you write:

  glowing skin · luminous · radiant · aura · halo · god rays ·
  light shafts · sunbeams through trees · golden mist · golden haze ·
  divine light · ethereal · dreamlike · otherworldly · magical ·
  shimmering · sparkling · floating petals · floating particles ·
  light bloom · lens flare · backlit glow · rim glow · heavenly

Also banned, because they make a god look poor rather than real:
  simple · plain · ordinary · rustic · humble · unadorned ·
  weathered · faded · coarse cotton · no jewellery

So: how a divine character is built instead.

  ❌ CALENDAR ART: "Krishna with glowing luminous blue skin, divine
     aura, god rays streaming through the trees, golden mist,
     floating petals, ethereal dreamlike atmosphere"

  ✅ A PHOTOGRAPH OF A DIVINE FIGURE: "a man of about twenty-two,
     his skin an even deep blue-grey, smooth and unlined with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen along the cheekbone, lit from
     the front left by low sun so the right side of his face falls
     into real shadow; a heavy saffron raw-silk dhoti with a
     hand-woven gold zari border, the silk falling in sharp folds; a
     gold crown of worked metal, bright and polished, the engraving
     cut deep and catching the light, and one peacock feather with
     separated barbs; a thick garland of fresh marigolds and tulsi"

  Read them again. The second one has MORE gold, MORE silk, MORE
  ornament. It is more divine, not less. It just refuses to glow.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ MUTED IS NOT THE SAME AS FADED — AND THIS TRIPS EVERYONE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Expensive modern footage is NOT colourful. Look at any big film of
the last ten years and most of the frame sits near grey, with one
colour allowed to be strong — skin, or fire, or one piece of cloth.
That is not the same thing as looking old.

  MODERN AND EXPENSIVE          OLD AND CHEAP
  colour pulled toward grey     colour washed out everywhere
  blacks are deep and solid     blacks are grey and lifted
  highlights clean and bright   highlights dull and flat
  sharp, no grain               soft, grainy, hazy
  one strong accent colour      no colour anywhere
  → looks like this year        → looks like a scanned photo

So write: a restrained palette near grey, ONE strong accent colour,
very deep blacks, clean bright highlights, strong contrast, sharp.
Never write: faded, washed, muted-and-soft, grainy, hazy, dusty,
low contrast.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
⭐ THE AGE LINE — WHY EVERYONE COMES OUT LOOKING OLD
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
If you do not state an age, the model picks one, and it almost
always picks older than you meant. Worse, every word used to make
an image look real — pores, lines, weathered, tarnish, worn,
faded, grain, muted — is also a word that means OLD. Use them on a
young character and a twenty-year-old comes back at forty-five,
and the whole film looks like it was shot decades ago.

So every character DNA line MUST open with an age, in years, and
with the words that go with that age:

  ✅ "a woman of about nineteen, smooth unlined skin with fine
     natural texture and a light sheen on the cheekbone, full
     cheeks, bright clear eyes, thick glossy black hair"
  ✅ "a man of about twenty-two, a lean jaw with faint stubble
     only along the edge, smooth forehead, bright clear eyes"
  ✅ "a woman of about sixty, deep lines across the forehead and
     at the eyes, thinning grey-streaked hair, loose skin at the
     throat"   ← ageing words ONLY when the character IS old

For anyone under thirty, these words are banned from the DNA line
and from every image prompt: pores, wrinkles, lines, crow's feet,
weathered, leathery, sun-damaged, rough, coarse, gaunt, sagging,
grey hair, sunken, tired, aged, elderly, mature.

AND THE SAME FOR THE WORLD. If the film is set today, say so in the
world DNA and say it in the picture: clean modern clothing with
sharp new stitching, undamaged paint, working electric light,
present-day haircuts, glass, steel, fresh fabric dye. If the film
is set in the past, say WHICH period and say it through objects —
never through decay. A period is a set of things, not a layer of
dust. Faded, peeling, cracked and crumbling do not say "old era",
they say "low budget".

⚠️⚠️ AND THE BIGGEST ONE: NEVER WRITE THE DEITY'S NAME IN AN IMAGE
OR VIDEO PROMPT.

Write "Krishna" and the model does not picture a person. It reaches
for everything ever labelled Krishna in its training — and that is
overwhelmingly devotional poster art, temple calendars, glowing
murals and 3D devotional renders. The name alone summons the style,
and no amount of camera wording afterwards will beat it. The same is
true of Radha, Shiva, Ram, Hanuman, Durga, and of "Lord", "Shri",
"deity", "divine figure", "God".

So the name lives in the STORY, the plan and the shot titles — where
it belongs and where it helps me follow along. It NEVER appears
inside a prompt I paste into a tool.

Inside a prompt, the character is only ever a physical human being:

  ❌ "Lord Krishna standing under a kadamba tree, divine, devotional"
  ✅ "a young man of about twenty, lean and broad-shouldered, skin an
     even deep blue-grey, shoulder-length curling black hair, a tall
     worked-gold crown holding one peacock feather, a saffron
     raw-silk dhoti with a woven gold border, a bamboo flute in both
     hands, standing under a broad kadamba tree"

Everything that makes him recognisable is still there — the blue
skin, the crown, the peacock feather, the flute, the dhoti. Any
viewer knows exactly who he is. The model, meanwhile, has been handed
a photograph brief instead of a poster brief.

This is what the CHARACTER DNA LINE is for. Write the DNA line so it
never contains the name, then paste it as-is. Put the name only in
the card heading, like "SHOT 07 — Krishna under the kadamba".

⚠️ ALSO BANNED, for the same reason — these are poster words, not
photography words, and every one of them pulls toward a render:
  8K · 4K · ultra-realistic · hyper-realistic · ultra-detailed ·
  highly detailed · masterpiece · premium · devotional · spiritual ·
  majestic · epic · breathtaking · stunning · beautiful ·
  cinematic (used on its own as an adjective) · high budget ·
  IMAX quality · poster · artwork · rendering
Say the lens, the film stock and the light instead. Those are what a
real photograph is described by.

Every divine woman still needs at least: kohl-lined eyes, bindi,
maang tikka, nath, jhumka, choker plus a long necklace, bajuband,
bangles, mehndi, payal, zari silk, a gold-bordered dupatta, fresh
flowers in the hair.
Every divine man still needs at least: a jewelled mukut, tilak,
kundal, vaijayanti mala, kantha, bajuband, kada, zari dhoti,
kamarbandh, anklets, and his signature object.
Describe each of those as a real physical object with a weight, a
weave, a finish and a small imperfection. Never as something that
shines by itself.

━━━ 2A. CAST LIST ━━━
List EVERY person who appears — even for one shot. Parents, friends,
elders, servants. Anyone without a sheet will change face between
shots. For each: name, role in a line, three trait words, age.

⚠️ AGE STAGES: if the film spans time, the SAME character needs a
SEPARATE sheet for EACH age. State explicitly which features carry
across all ages — eye shape, face structure, skin tone, marks — so
it reads as one person growing up, not three different people.

━━━ 2B. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — make this one FIRST ━━━

⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE THE SHEET. It prevents two real failures: faces
that come out looking cartoonish, and having to upload a pile of
images for every shot.

For each character produce TWO different things, and they have
different jobs:

  1. THE UPLOAD PORTRAIT — one clean photographic portrait: a single
     frame filling the whole image, one person, every surface blank
     and unmarked. THIS is the only file that ever gets uploaded into
     an image or video tool. Filename: CHAR-[name].png

  2. THE CHARACTER SHEET — the labelled multi-view grid in 2C below.
     This is a PLANNING document for me to look at and for you to
     write the DNA line from. It is NEVER uploaded into a tool.

⚠️ WHY THIS SPLIT EXISTS — THE CARTOON FACE BUG
A model reads an uploaded reference as a whole frame and copies its
LOOK, not just its content. A character sheet is a designed artifact:
a grid, flat studio lighting, labels, colour swatches, several heads
floating on one canvas. Upload that into a video tool and it faithfully
reproduces exactly those qualities — flat, graphic, illustrated. That
is the "it started looking like a cartoon" failure, and it is caused
by the reference, not the prompt. Nothing you write in words will
outvote it.

So the upload portrait must look like a photograph of a real person
and nothing else. Write its prompt like this:

   Photographed on a modern digital cinema camera, 85mm lens at
   f/2.8, eye level, camera height [n] centimetres. A single portrait
   of one person, vertical 9:16, three-quarter view from the waist up.
   Lit by soft window light from the front left at 45 degrees, so the
   left of the face is bright and the right falls into gentle real
   shadow; weak bounce fill from the right. Daylight temperature.
   [the character's full description]
   Standing relaxed against a plain mid-grey wall, facing camera,
   eyes looking into the lens, a calm natural expression, lips
   closed.
   Exactly one person in this frame, no one else, no duplicate, no
   second figure, nobody in the background.
   [the full positive closing block]
   One single frame filling the whole image, one continuous plain
   wall behind, every surface blank and unmarked.
   ⚠️ Write NO ban list here. A tool with one text box reads "no
   grid, no labels, no glow" as a request for grids, labels and
   glow. State only what IS in the frame.

   SAVE AS: CHAR-[name].png
   ✅ DEKHO: ye ek asli insaan ki photo lagni chahiye. Agar isme
   grid, label ya flat studio look hai, dobara banao — wahi cartoon
   wali galti karega.

⚠️ AND NEVER STACK REFERENCES
One upload is stronger than four. Each extra reference image adds its
own texture and style to the blend, and the more you stack the further
the result drifts from photographic. Never upload the character sheet,
the world sheet, the style image and the shot still all together.
 • IMAGE prompts: upload the character portrait only. One per
   character actually in the shot. Nothing else.
 • VIDEO prompts: upload the shot still only — plus the next shot's
   still if, and only if, you are pinning a last frame.
 • The world sheet and style image are for YOU to look at while
   writing. They are never uploaded.

━━━ 2C. CHARACTER SHEET PROMPTS (planning only, never uploaded) ━━━
One per character per age stage. Write each as a ready-to-paste
prompt. Use this exact structure and fill in the character:

   Create a CHARACTER SHEET in one single image, 2:3 portrait, on a
   plain light grey studio backdrop with even soft lighting.
   CHARACTER: [name, age, role]
   [full description — face, build, costume, every ornament, colours,
    skin tone, distinguishing marks]
   TOP ROW — four views, identical face in all four:
     1. FACE, head and shoulders, looking at lens
     2. FRONT, full body, standing
     3. BACK, full body from directly behind — hair, braid, the back
        of the costume and how the garment falls
     4. SIDE, full profile
   BOTTOM ROW — five tight macro tiles:
     1. TILAK or forehead marking — exact shape and colour
     2. HAIR — ornament, braid pattern, parting
        (for a man: TURBAN or CROWN detail)
     3. JEWELLERY — ear, neck and arm, clearly readable
     4. HAND — bangles, mehndi, rings, nails
     5. FEET — anklets, toe rings, sole
        (for a man: his SIGNATURE OBJECT)
   Label every view and tile with small text underneath.
   Below the tiles, a bar of five colour swatches from this
   character: skin, main garment, accent, metal, accessory.
   The face must be IDENTICAL in every view and tile.
   Photographic realism, word for word: "healthy skin with fine
   natural texture and a light sheen on the forehead and cheekbones,
   individually visible hair strands, crisp new fabric with a soft
   sheen where it catches the light, bright polished metal with deep
   sharp engraving, clean modern digital capture, very deep blacks,
   strong contrast, a restrained palette with one strong colour"
   [STYLE LINE FROM MY PICK — paste it as WORDS. Never upload the
    style image itself; an uploaded style reference drags its own
    texture into the result.]

Under each sheet prompt, print that character's DNA LINE in its own
separate block, clearly marked, like this:

   ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
   ║  DNA — RADHA                                     ║
   ║  the heading names her so I can follow along.    ║
   ║  the LINE below never says her name — copy the   ║
   ║  line only, whole, into every prompt she is in   ║
   ╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
   a young Indian woman of about eighteen, slender, a soft
   oval face with a rounded chin and long dark eyes, fair
   golden-brown skin with fine natural texture, very long jet black
   hair in one thick braid bound with gold cord and threaded with
   white jasmine buds, a deep rose pink silk lehenga with dense gold
   zari embroidery along the hem, a fitted matching choli, a pale
   gold silk dupatta over her left shoulder, a gold maang tikka with
   a pearl drop, a small red bindi, a gold nath with a fine pearl
   chain to her ear, large gold jhumkas, a pearl and emerald choker,
   gold armlets, stacked gold and red glass bangles on both wrists, a
   fine gold waist chain, heavy silver anklets with small bells
   ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

That is the format and roughly the length. One unbroken line. It
must name, in this order: age and build · face shape and skin tone ·
hair, how it is tied, what is in it · every garment with colour and
fabric and how it sits · every ornament item by item · anything
always in their hand.

⚠️ Notice what the line does NOT contain: her name, and any word
like goddess, divine or devotional. The heading above the box is
where the name lives. The line itself describes a human being only —
```
